## P0 — BROKEN NOW  (65)

### `main.py:1107` — UnboundLocalError kills startup when there is no saved bot state
`correctness` · **critical** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `_autostart_bot_count` has exactly ONE Store site, at main.py:699, and that store is nested inside `if state_mgr.has_saved_state():` (main.py:664). The Load at main.py:1107 is at main()-body indentation, outside that `if`. Python binds the name as a function-local; when the branch is not taken the name is never bound and line 1107 raises UnboundLocalError. `StateManager.has_saved_state()` (src/core/state_manager.py:164-173) returns False in three real situations: the state file does not exist (fresh install / new machine), `state["bots"]` is empty (operator deleted all bots), or the file fails to parse (`except Exception: return False`).

**Operator sees:** On a fresh install (or after deleting every bot, or with a corrupt bot_state.json) the operator sees the splash screen appear, then the app dies before `app.exec()` is ever reached. The main window never becomes interactive. Because MEM-216 hooks are installed, the traceback lands in ~/.acervator_logs/crash_*.log rather than on screen, so the visible symptom is 'Acervator flashes a logo then vanishes'. This is the Mac-Mini / new-machine install path.

**Fix:** Initialize the counter before the branch: insert `_autostart_bot_count = 0` immediately before `if state_mgr.has_saved_state():` at main.py:664.

### `src/gui/bot_visualizer.py:1609` — Simulator Swarm and Paper Swarm "+ Add" rows are inert — Run/Start only recolor a label, no engine is ever started and PnL/price never update
`dead-feature` · **high** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `_toggle_run` (and the paper-side `_toggle` at 1734) mutate a dict flag, swap two stylesheets, set a status string, and call the summary refresher. Nothing constructs a simulator, a paper exchange, or a coroutine. The row's own controls are write-only: `asset_combo`, `preset_combo` and `cap_spin` for sim, and `pair_edit`, `src_combo` for paper are stored in `bot_data` and never read again. `pnl_lbl` and `price_lbl` are created (1571, 1686, 1692) and stored, then never written anywhere in the module. `_run_all_sims`/`_stop_all_sims` (1382-1394) and `_start_all_paper`/`_stop_all_paper` (1478-1490) just fan the same no-op out via `btn.click()`. The genuinely functional path is the separate `register_sim_run`/`update_sim_run` API driven externally by the Simulator and PaperTrader tabs — these hand-added rows are a parallel, disconnected UI.

**Operator sees:** The operator clicks "+ Add Sim Bot", picks BTC/USDT, the btc_bull preset and $400 capital, hits Run — the row turns green and says RUNNING forever. No simulation executes, PnL stays "PnL: —" and price stays "—" indefinitely. The Paper Swarm tab behaves identically, and its rows are labelled LIVE while trading nothing. Both tabs advertise real behaviour in their descriptions ("Run multiple simultaneous simulators", "Run multiple live paper trading bots simultaneously... against real market data").

**Fix:** Remove the "+ Add Sim Bot" / "+ Add Paper Bot" / Run All / Stop All controls and `_create_sim_bot_row` / `_create_paper_bot_row` entirely, leaving the tabs as read-only mirrors fed by the working `register_sim_run` / `register_paper_run` API. If operator-initiated swarm launch is actually wanted, wire the buttons to the Simulator / PaperTrader controllers that already expose `set_bot_viz` (main_window.py:3816, 3821).

> **Verifier correction:** None material. One nuance: the tab-description quotes are at 1336 ('Run multiple simultaneous simulators. Each bot runs independently...') and 1432 ('Run multiple live paper trading bots simultaneously. ...'), both verbatim.

### `src/gui/bot_visualizer.py:1512` — Wire canvas overlay is shown at startup while the default List view is active, positioned over the list and intercepting mouse events
`correctness` · **high** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `_on_view_mode_changed` is the only place that hides the top-level wire canvas in list mode (`self._wire_canvas.setVisible(mode == "grid")`, line 2476), and it can only fire from `currentIndexChanged`. At construction the combo is populated with `addItem("List", ...)` first — which already sets index 0 — and `setCurrentIndex(0)` at line 1169 is therefore a no-op that emits nothing; the `connect` at 1174-1175 happens afterwards. So `_on_view_mode_changed` never runs at startup while `self._view_mode` defaults to "list" (1067) and `_view_stack.setCurrentIndex(0)` (1259) shows the list. The forced `_on_tab_changed(0)` at 1512 then calls `self._wire_canvas.show()`. `_reposition_wire_canvas` (2187-2210) sizes the canvas to `self._grid_widget.rect()` — the grid is a sibling page in the same QStackedWidget, so it carries the same geometry as the visible list page. The canvas is created at 1496, after `layout.addWidget(self._tabs)` at 1492, so it stacks above; and it is explicitly NOT click-through (`setAttribute(Qt.WA_TransparentForMouseEvents, False)`, line 1497). This is the same failure mode the v3.23.19 comment at 2188-2199 documents fixing for the header controls.

**Operator sees:** On the default Bot Swarm view (List), an invisible full-area overlay may sit on top of the bot list — clicks and scrolls on rows would be consumed by the wire canvas instead of reaching the list. Switching View to Grid and back to List would clear it, since that path does call `_on_view_mode_changed`.

**Fix:** Call `self._on_view_mode_changed()` once at the end of `__init__` (after `_wire_canvas` exists), or set `self._wire_canvas.setVisible(self._view_mode == "grid")` inside `_on_tab_changed` so tab-activation and view-mode agree. Verify on a running instance by clicking a list row at startup before touching the View combo.

> **Verifier correction:** UPGRADE, not a refutation. The claim self-rated 'suspected' with 'NOT VERIFIED: I could not run the GUI.' I ran it. Under QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen I constructed BotVisualizationTab, resized to 1400x800 and showed it: `_view_mode='list'`, `_view_stack.currentIndex()=0`, `_wire_canvas.isVisible()=True`, and — decisively — `t.childAt(t._bot_list.mapTo(t, t._bot_list.rect().center()))` returns `_WireCanvas` (accessibleName 'Wire Canvas'). So the overlay is provably the hit-test winner over the middle of the bot list on the default view. After a further resize the canvas lands at QRect(9, 58, 640, 480) — exactly the list's origin — while the list is 680x733, i.e. the overlay covers roughly the top-left 640x480 of the list. Toggling View→Grid→List clears it: after that round trip `_wire_canvas.isVisible()=False` and `childAt(list center)` returns a plain QWidget, confirming the claimed workaround. Event-consumption reasoning also holds: `_WireCanvas.mousePressEvent` (3097-3109) falls through to `super().mousePressEvent(event)`, and Qt propagates ignored mouse events UP the parent chain, never down the z-order to an occluded sibling — and there is no wheelEvent override at all, so list scrolling under the overlay is dead too.

### `src/gui/indicator_panel.py:383` — Indicator Voting Panel fabricates synthetic TA from random noise and renders it as live data with the real bot's symbol
`data-integrity` · **high** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** _auto_init_demo fires 3 s after the panel is constructed and, if self._data is empty, calls _generate_demo_ta(). _on_bot_selected (line 801) does the same on any bot selection with no data. _generate_demo_ta builds 60 candles from `rng.gauss(0, 0.015)` (lines 827-836), runs the real VotingEngine over them, and pushes the result through the normal `self.update_data({ta_tf: tf_data}, sym_text)` path at line 866 -- where sym_text is pulled from the bot selector, i.e. the operator's actual symbol. Nothing in update_data, _populate_indicator_cell, _populate_net_cell or _populate_conf_cell distinguishes demo data from real data: no badge, no colour change, no tooltip marker.

**Operator sees:** The operator opens the app, waits three seconds, and sees a fully populated 12-indicator voting matrix with net scores, confidence bars and directional arrows for e.g. BONK/USD -- generated from a seeded gaussian random walk. There is no visual cue that it is fake, and it is the exact panel the operator uses to sanity-check scrum/fold timing.

**Fix:** Either delete the demo path entirely, or gate it behind sim_mode/an explicit debug flag AND stamp it: set a bright "DEMO DATA - NOT LIVE" banner on _title/_summary_label and force _symbol_label to "SIM" whenever _generate_demo_ta produced the current _data. Clear the flag on the first real update_data call from main_window.

> **Verifier correction:** Reproduced, and it is slightly worse than claimed. Line 383 `QTimer.singleShot(3000, self._auto_init_demo)` is verbatim in __init__. _auto_init_demo (874-879) sets _selected_bot_id to 'demo' if empty and calls _generate_demo_ta whenever _data is empty; _on_bot_selected (793-802) does the same on selection. _generate_demo_ta (808-872) builds 60 candles from rng.gauss(0, 0.015)/rng.uniform (827-836), runs the real VotingEngine (838), and pushes it through the normal update_data() path at 866 with sym_text taken from the bot selector's raw (unmasked) label at 848-851. My grep for 'demo' across the file finds no badge, no tooltip, no style branch — the only demo markers are logger.info lines. THIRD path the claim missed: force_refresh() (881-892) clears self._data and calls _generate_demo_ta unconditionally, so an externally triggered refresh after bot creation also injects synthetic data. Panel is live: constructed at main_window.py:3451, real data arrives via update_data(merged, symbol) at main_window.py:4980.

### `src/gui/main_window.py:1511` — Ammo column takes max() of stale and fresh position value — inflates exposure and can invert the Scrum/Fold signal
`correctness` · **high** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The comment states the intent is 'whichever is non-zero', but max() actually selects whichever is NUMERICALLY LARGER. stats_pv comes from status['stats']['position_value'] and fresh_pv is recomputed from status['current_holdings'] x stats['current_price'] x status['quote_to_usd']. Those three inputs are written at DIFFERENT points in the bot tick: src/trading/scrumming_bot.py writes stats.current_price at lines 4574 and 4803 and _current_holdings at 4702/8655/8878/9429, but stats.position_value only at line 5655 (and 3983/3988 during bootstrap). Any tick that updates the price and then returns early before line 5655 (e.g. the ENTRY branch that `return`s at ~5645) leaves position_value stale while current_price is fresh. In a falling market the stale value is the larger one, so max() systematically discards the fresh, lower number. The bias is one-directional: it can only ever overstate exposure, never understate it.

**Operator sees:** Bot holds 100 units; last completed position_value write was $200 at $2.00/unit. Price falls to $1.50 (stats.current_price updates, position_value does not). fresh_pv = $150, stats_pv = $200, max() -> $200. With target_balance $175 the true delta is -$25 (Fold territory, red, next action BUY) but the table renders +$25 in Scrum green (next action SELL). The operator's primary decision surface shows the opposite trade direction. This is the exact column MEM-248/MEM-250 were written to make trustworthy.

**Fix:** Select by freshness, not magnitude. Prefer fresh_pv whenever holdings > 0 and cur_price > 0 (both are the most recently written fields), and fall back to stats_pv only when fresh_pv cannot be computed: `position_val = fresh_pv if fresh_pv > 0 else stats_pv`. Update the comment to match. Optionally export a `position_value_ts` in get_status_dict() so the GUI can pick the newer of the two explicitly.

> **Verifier correction:** Core defect confirmed, but the claim's chosen example branch is the weakest one available and should be replaced. Verified line 1511 is exactly `position_val = max(stats_pv, fresh_pv)` with the comment at 1509-1510 reading 'Prefer the LARGER ... whichever is non-zero is the real exposure' — the comment describes non-zero selection, the code does numeric max. Write-site counts re-grepped independently: `self.stats.position_value =` appears ONLY at scrumming_bot.py:3983, 3988, 5655 (exactly as claimed); `self.stats.current_price =` at 3980, 4574, 4803; `self._current_holdings =` at 2617, 2724, 3975, 4702, 8655, 8878, 9429 (claim listed 4702/8655/8878/9429 and missed 2617/2724/3975 — under-counted, does not weaken the finding). Units confirmed identical: scrumming_bot.py:4846-4849 `current_value = self._current_holdings * ticker.last * float(self._quote_to_usd or 1.0)` with the comment 'current_value is in USD', matching the GUI's fresh_pv formula at main_window.py:1505-1508. bot_container.py exports `"position_value": round(getattr(self.stats, "position_value", 0.0), 4)` and `"current_price": self.stats.current_price` from the same stats object, so no units mismatch — it is purely the max() selection, as claimed. CORRECTION TO THE MECHANISM: the claim cites 'the ENTRY branch that returns at ~5645'. The actual ENTRY return is at line 5652, not 5645, and it is the WORST example — that branch only fires on a zero/near-zero position, where stats_pv and fresh_pv are both ~0, so max() is harmless there. I enumerated every `return` between the price write (4803) and the position_value write (5655): 4901, 4942, 4990, 5006, 5253, 5278, 5292, 5358, 5376, 5414, 5436, 5468, 5494, 5532, 5549, 5577, 5590, 5622, 5652 — nineteen early-exit paths. The DOMINANT one is line 4901, the MEM-258 delta-zero short-circuit: `if (not self._manual_fire_pending and abs(current_value - self._target_balance) <= _dust_band_usd): ... return`. That is the bot's declared steady state ('AT TARGET (MEM-258) ... Tick exits early') and it returns on EVERY tick while parked, so stats.position_value freezes at whatever the last out-of-band tick wrote while stats.current_price keeps refreshing. Second-most-important: the fire paths at 5253-5622 return after mutating _current_holdings, so immediately post-SCRUM the stale pre-sell (larger) value wins the max().

### `src/gui/settings_dialog.py:1039` — SettingsDialog writes six settings that _load_current never reads back, so any Save silently resets them to widget defaults
`data-integrity` · **high** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** _load_current (lines 978-1020) restores only username, position_distance_pct, default_position_count, default_target_balance, accent_color, the ai_monitor block, theme, increment_style and profit_folding['active']. It never restores _visibility, _aggressive, _font_family, _font_size, _heading_size, _log_font_size, the fold/dist radio targets and counts, or the _log_24h/_log_1w/_log_1m/_log_1y and _ta_logging/_highlight_trades checkboxes -- all of which _save DOES write (lines 1039-1046, 1057-1071, 1077-1087). So the widgets always come up at their hardcoded construction defaults, and the next Save overwrites the persisted values with those defaults.

**Operator sees:** The operator's font sizes, bot visibility, aggressive-trading flag, profit-folding target selection and P/L log periodicity silently revert every time the Settings dialog is opened and saved for any unrelated reason. It reads as "my settings don't stick".

**Fix:** Extend _load_current to read every key _save writes: bot_visibility, aggressive_trading, font_family, font_size, heading_font_size, log_font_size, the profit_folding fold_target/fold_target_count/distribute_target/distribute_target_count/mode fields, and the data_logging ta_signal_logging/highlight_trade_proximity/active_periodicities fields. A round-trip pin test (set -> save -> reconstruct dialog -> assert widget state) would catch future drift.

> **Verifier correction:** Fully reproduced. _load_current (978-1020) restores only username, position_distance_pct, default_position_count, default_target_balance, accent_color, the ai_monitor block, theme, increment_style, profit_folding['active'], and the exchange list. My grep confirms _load_current is called exactly once (line 74, in __init__) and there is no other restore path. Every construction default cited is exact: _visibility addItems(["orderbook","internal"]) at 380 with no setCurrentIndex; _font_family.setCurrentText("Segoe UI") 520; _font_size.setValue(11) 526; _heading_size.setValue(14) 533; _log_font_size.setValue(10) 540; _fold_all.setChecked(True) 410; _log_24h/_log_1w checked at 575/577 while _log_1m/_log_1y are not; _ta_logging/_highlight_trades checked at 568/571. _save writes all of them back (1039-1046, 1057-1071, 1077-1087). The round-trip described is exactly what the code does. SettingsDialog is live — constructed at main_window.py:6321 and 6468.

### `src/gui/simulator_tab/fleet/sim_exchange.py:344` — Sim candle addresses are indices into the replay window, not the tablet — violating addressing.py's stated INDEX SEMANTICS contract
`correctness` · **high** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** addressing.py:37-41 defines the contract verbatim: "The index is the position within THAT TABLET'S full candle list, counted from its first candle (index 0). It is NOT a global clock tick and NOT an offset from a shared epoch." But `idx` here comes from `_cursor_index` -> `int(series.cursor)` (sim_exchange.py:329), and the series is built from the WINDOWED slice the panel loaded: fleet_replay_panel.py:715-720 calls `reg.get_candles(asset=asset, since_ms=_since_ms, ...)` where `_since_ms` is the v3.24.16 validation soft-start cap (fleet_replay_panel.py:700-706), not the tablet's first candle. So cursor 0 is the first candle of the replay window. Every stamped address is offset by the number of tablet candles preceding the soft-start, and the offset changes whenever the soft-start moves. The one validator that would catch this — `validate_trade_event`'s ADDRESS_MISMATCH branch (src/trading/sim_validation_guard.py:325-334) — has zero callers, and `verify_addressing_stable` (addressing.py:184), which addressing.py:61 says "exists to detect that", is referenced only by tests.

**Operator sees:** Every `NNNNNN_TICKER` address recorded on a sim fill points at the wrong candle. Cross-session comparison of stored addresses is meaningless, and the operator's stated goal — 'at 1234_BTC, live fired BUY; did the sim gate arm at 1234_BTC?' — silently compares different candles.

**Fix:** Either pass the tablet-absolute offset down (have the panel record `window_start_index` per symbol and add it to `series.cursor` before formatting), or resolve the address from the timestamp instead of the cursor: `address_for_ts(symbol, full_tablet_rows, sim_ts_ms)`, which addressing.py already provides and which is immune to windowing. Then wire `validate_trade_event` so ADDRESS_MISMATCH actually fires.

> **Verifier correction:** One citation is off by one: the gate_healer docstring line '``candles`` is the FULL tablet row list for the symbol.' is at gate_healer.py:190, not 189. Also, the defect is WORSE than claimed: the offset exists even when NO soft-start cap applies, because the floor is YTD_START_MS (2026-04-01) while BTC's tablet begins 2026-01-01 — roughly 25,900 candles of constant offset on the fallback path too.

### `src/core/logging_engine.py:557` — LogManager.attach_to_bus calls EventBus.unsubscribe, which does not exist; documented idempotency is false
`broken-ref` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `EventBus` (src/core/event_bus.py) exposes only subscribe / emit / get_history / clear. `subscribe()` RETURNS an unsubscribe closure (line 120); there is no `EventBus.unsubscribe(topic, callback)` method. All four `bus.unsubscribe(...)` calls at lines 557, 561, 565, 571 raise AttributeError, which the bare `except Exception: pass` swallows. Execution then falls through to lines 577-605 and subscribes all four handlers a second time. The docstring at lines 552-553 states 'Idempotent: calling twice replaces the prior subscription rather than double-writing each trade' — the opposite is true.

**Operator sees:** Today main.py:520 is the only caller so nothing doubles yet. The moment anything re-attaches (a reconnect path, a settings reload, or a test that constructs a second LogManager against the shared bus) every executed trade is appended twice to trade.log, every gate decision twice to gate.log, and every pnl.event twice to the daily cascade — silently corrupting the logs the sim parity tool treats as ground truth.

**Fix:** Store the unsubscribe closures returned by `bus.subscribe(...)` in `self._bus_unsubs` and call them in the re-attach branch, or add a real `unsubscribe(topic, callback)` method to EventBus. Either way stop swallowing AttributeError here — a missing bus API is a wiring bug, not a best-effort miss.

> **Verifier correction:** Fully correct. Quoted block is lines 555-559; cited line 557 is the first `bus.unsubscribe("trade.filled", self._on_trade_filled_bus)` — right anchor. I ran `python -c "from src.core.event_bus import EventBus; print(hasattr(EventBus,'unsubscribe'))"` -> False, members `['_matches','clear','emit','get_history','subscribe']`, and calling `b.unsubscribe(...)` on a live instance raises `AttributeError: 'EventBus' object has no attribute 'unsubscribe'`. All four call sites are at 557, 561, 565, 571 as claimed, each wrapped in `except Exception: pass`. The docstring at 552-553 reads exactly 'Idempotent: calling twice replaces the prior subscription / rather than double-writing each trade.' Execution falls through to the four `bus.subscribe` calls at 577, 587, 596-597, 603-605. I confirmed main.py:520 is the sole caller (`grep -rn attach_to_bus src/ tools/ tests/ main.py` -> logging_engine.py:528 def, :614 log string, :806 docstring reference, main.py:520).

### `src/core/rule_registry.py:58` — rule_registry persists to sadp/RULE_REGISTRY.json, a directory that does not exist — every mutating RULE command crashes
`broken-ref` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `ROOT` resolves to the repo root, and there is no `sadp/` directory there (or anywhere). `_load` tolerates the miss (line 163 `if self.path.exists()`), but `_save` at lines 181-187 is a bare `self.path.write_text(...)` with no `mkdir(parents=True)`. `Path.write_text` on a path whose parent is absent raises FileNotFoundError. Every state-changing command — lock/unlock/suspend/restore — calls `_save()`, as does `initialise_defaults()` at line 580, which is what `python src/core/rule_registry.py` runs (line 591).

**Operator sees:** version_sweep's CHECK 12 tells the operator 'sadp/RULE_REGISTRY.json not found — will be created on first RULE command → Run: python src/core/rule_registry.py' (version_sweep.py:595-597). Following that instruction raises an unhandled FileNotFoundError. The entire RULE command surface — the SADP administrative control layer — is unusable.

**Fix:** Add `self.path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` at the top of `_save`, and decide where the registry actually lives now that the `sadp/` tree is gone (the operator's SADP docs live outside this repo — see reference_sadp_paths). If the registry has moved out of the repo, point REGISTRY_PATH at the real location; if the feature is retired, delete the module and CHECK 12 rather than leaving a crash behind a printed instruction.

> **Verifier correction:** Correct in every particular. Line 58 is verbatim `REGISTRY_PATH = ROOT / "sadp" / "RULE_REGISTRY.json"`. I reproduced the crash directly: instantiating RuleRegistry() and calling `_save()` raises `FileNotFoundError [Errno 2] No such file or directory: '...\\sadp\\RULE_REGISTRY.json'`, and `REGISTRY_PATH.parent.exists()` is False. `_load` tolerates the miss at line 163 (`if self.path.exists()`); `_save` at 181-187 is a bare `self.path.write_text(...)` with no `mkdir(parents=True)` anywhere in the class. `reg._save()` inside `initialise_defaults` is at line 580 and `reg = initialise_defaults()` under `if __name__ == "__main__"` is at line 591 — both cites exact. version_sweep.py:594-597 does print 'sadp/RULE_REGISTRY.json not found — will be created on first RULE command' with suggestion 'Run: python src/core/rule_registry.py', so following the sweep's own instruction crashes.

### `src/core/version_sweep.py:732` — version_sweep R25 release gate can never pass — mandatory annotation target file was deleted
`broken-ref` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `simulator.py` no longer exists anywhere in the repo (retired per src/gui/simulator_tab/__init__.py, which calls itself 'the replacement for the retired nuclear_live.py'). `mandatory_found["simulator.py:_sim_scrumming_tick"]` therefore can never flip True, and lines 778-783 emit a Severity.HIGH finding on every run. `SweepResult.passed` (line 74) requires zero HIGH findings, so `main()` always hits `sys.exit(1)`. Compounding it, `registry_path` at line 723 resolves to `<root>/sadp/RULE_REGISTRY.json`, which does not exist, so `registry = {}` and line 765 `if rid not in registry` fires for every single `# sadp: RNN` annotation in the tree — 123 of them in src/ alone — as Severity.MEDIUM 'unknown rule'.

**Operator sees:** Running `python src/core/version_sweep.py` always prints '✗ SWEEP FAILED' and exits 1, buried under ~123 spurious MEDIUM 'annotation references unknown rule' findings. The R25 quality gate is unusable as a pass/fail signal, so it gets ignored — which is exactly how a real HIGH finding would slip through.

**Fix:** Drop the `("simulator.py", "_sim_scrumming_tick")` entry (or re-point it at the surviving gate function in src/trading/scrumming_bot.py). Separately, make the missing-registry case short-circuit: if `not registry_path.exists()`, emit one INFO and `return` instead of flagging all 123 annotations as unknown rules.

> **Verifier correction:** Substantively correct and I reproduced it empirically. Two small numeric corrections. (a) The quoted block actually begins at line 731 (`MANDATORY = [`); line 732 is `("simulator.py", "_sim_scrumming_tick"),` — the cited line is the defective entry itself, so the anchor is right. (b) The MEDIUM count is understated: the claim says '~123 spurious MEDIUM'. 123 is the number of ANNOTATION LINES in src/ (I re-counted: `grep -rno '#\s*sadp:\s*R[0-9]' --include=*.py src/ | wc -l` -> 123, whole repo 132), but a single annotation line can cite several rules and the sweep rglobs the entire repo, so the actual finding count is 215. I ran the check directly: `check_sadp_annotations` produced 217 findings = 215 MEDIUM + 1 HIGH + 1 INFO, and the single HIGH is exactly `simulator.py 0 | R38: mandatory sadp annotation missing on simulator.py:_sim_scrumming_tick.` The other four MANDATORY targets all resolve. `result.passed` came back False, and passed is `len(critical)==0 and len(high)==0` at line 74, feeding `sys.exit(0 if result.passed else 1)` at line 1148. `find . -name simulator.py` returns nothing; `sadp/` does not exist.

### `src/core/version_sweep.py:452` — version_sweep emits four permanent false DEPENDENCY findings against a requirements.txt that does not exist
`broken-ref` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** Neither requirements file exists — dependencies moved to pyproject.toml (which declares tomli_w>=1.0.0 at line 20, among others). Both `exists()` checks fail, so `reqs_text` stays empty. The loop at 466-482 then finds that none of PySide6/ccxt/reportlab/cryptography is 'in' the empty string, confirms each is imported somewhere, and files a Severity.MEDIUM finding pointing at `self.root / "requirements.txt"` — a path that isn't there. The check never reads pyproject.toml.

**Operator sees:** Every sweep run reports four MEDIUM 'X used in source but not in requirements files' findings, all false, all pointing at a nonexistent file. Combined with the ~123 false SADP-annotation MEDIUMs, the MEDIUM tier of the report is pure noise, so a genuine dependency gap would never be spotted.

**Fix:** Read `pyproject.toml` (the `[project].dependencies` list) as the dependency source, keeping requirements*.txt as an optional additional source when present. If neither source resolves, emit one INFO saying so rather than a per-package MEDIUM.

> **Verifier correction:** Correct and empirically reproduced. Lines 452-456 are verbatim; cited line 452 is the first line of the block. `test -e requirements.txt` and `test -e requirements-optional.txt` both MISSING. pyproject.toml line 20 is `    "tomli_w>=1.0.0",` — the cite is exact (dependencies block runs lines 12-22). I ran `check_requirements` in-process and got exactly 4 MEDIUM findings: PySide6, ccxt, reportlab, cryptography — all four, as claimed, all pointing at the nonexistent `requirements.txt` (the ternary at 479 falls through to `self.root / "requirements.txt"`). Worth noting for the fixer: 'reportlab' matches only because version_sweep.py's own `required_packages` dict at line 462 contains the literal string, so that one is self-triggering. The check never opens pyproject.toml — confirmed by reading the whole method.

### `src/exchange/api_load_monitor.py:90` — API-load gauge is structurally capped at 500 CPM by the 500-entry log ring — this IS the operator's "500/600 CPM saturation"
`correctness` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The monitor asks the shared APIInteractionLog for up to 10,000 entries, but `APIInteractionLog.__init__(self, max_entries: int = 500)` (src/exchange/api_logger.py:34) hard-truncates the ring to 500 entries TOTAL across all exchanges (`self._entries = self._entries[-self._max:]`, api_logger.py:93-94), and `get_api_log()` at api_logger.py:176 constructs it with the default. So `len(entries)` in `sample()` can never exceed 500, and `cpm = n * 60.0 / self._window_s` with the default 60 s window can never exceed 500.0 against a `DEFAULT_CEILING_CPM = 600.0`. `load_score` therefore saturates at 0.83 and can NEVER reach 1.0. With two connectors attached the per-exchange slice of the shared 500-entry ring is smaller still, so the reported CPM drops further below truth. The `count=10_000` argument is the author's own evidence that a much larger window was intended.

**Operator sees:** The status-bar pill rendered by main_window.py `_refresh_api_load_pill` (main_window.py:4386-4389, `f"API {worst.exchange}: {worst.calls_per_minute:.0f}/{worst.ceiling_cpm:.0f} CPM ({pct} %)"`) pins at ~500/600 CPM (83 %) and stops moving no matter how much worse the real load gets — it turns red and stays red, giving no signal about whether a fix helped. Separately `should_allow_new_phantom_set` compares an under-measured CPM against the 450 CPM safety threshold, so it will approve phantom sets that push real load well past the ceiling.

**Fix:** Size the API log to cover the monitor's window with headroom for every connected exchange — e.g. `get_api_log()` constructs `APIInteractionLog(max_entries=10_000)`, or better, have APIInteractionLog evict by age (drop entries older than ~120 s) instead of by count so the CPM math is window-bounded rather than buffer-bounded. Add a regression test asserting `sample()` reports > 600 CPM when 700 entries land inside the 60 s window.

> **Verifier correction:** All cited facts check out. api_load_monitor.py:90-91 is verbatim `all_entries = self._api_log().get_for_exchange(` / `exchange, count=10_000)`. api_logger.py:34 is `def __init__(self, max_entries: int = 500):`; the truncation is at 93-94 (`self._entries = self._entries[-self._max:]`); the ONLY construction in the repo is api_logger.py:176 `_global_log = APIInteractionLog()` with no argument (I grepped `APIInteractionLog(` across the whole tree — other hits are only in _archive/ and .session26_backups/). Arithmetic reproduced: n<=500, window 60.0 => cpm = n*60/60 <= 500.0 against DEFAULT_CEILING_CPM=600.0 (line 34), so load_score (line 115) is bounded at 0.833 and can never reach 1.0. Two corrections to the framing: (a) the pill text quoted for main_window.py:4386-4389 is a paraphrase — the actual code is a 4-line f-string split across lines 4386-4389 reading `f"API {worst.exchange}: "` / `f"{worst.calls_per_minute:.0f}/"` / `f"{worst.ceiling_cpm:.0f} CPM ({pct} %)"`, same meaning, not one literal; (b) the causal attribution to the operator's observed '500/600' is inference, not proof — data_pool.py:157 does contain that phrase verbatim but describes a v3.23.74 fix, it does not state the ring is the cause. Additional evidence the scanner missed that STRENGTHENS the under-measurement: get_orderbook (856), get_open_orders (1094), get_order (1087), cancel_order (1080), get_my_trades (1107) and get_all_tickers (838) never call _log.record at all, so the monitor already misses those call classes entirely. Only get_ticker (828), get_ohlcv (878), get_balances (907), place_order (1005, 1069) record.

### `src/exchange/ccxt_connector.py:681` — Trade-historian threads race on `_scan_symbols`: N bots spawn N threads that swap the shared set, permanently dropping symbols
`threading` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `add_scan_symbol` (ccxt_connector.py:645-666) adds to `self._scan_symbols` and, when already connected, spawns a daemon thread running `refresh_history(symbol=...)`. `refresh_history` then does a non-atomic read-swap-restore of the SHARED `self._scan_symbols` attribute with no lock. `BotContainer.set_connector` calls `connector.add_scan_symbol(bot.config.symbol)` inside a `for bot in self._bots.values()` loop (src/trading/bot_container.py:1755-1757), so with N bots you get N concurrent threads all executing that swap. Interleaving with two bots A then B: thread-A captures orig={A,B} and sets the set to {A}; thread-B then captures orig={A} (the truncated value) and sets {B}; thread-B restores {A}; thread-A restores {A,B} — or the reverse order, in which case B is permanently gone from the registry. Worse, `_scan_trade_history` reads `list(self._scan_symbols)` at ccxt_connector.py:604 after another thread has already replaced it, so a thread can scan a symbol that is not its own and its own symbol is never scanned at all.

**Operator sees:** With more than one bot running, the History tab shows trade history for a random subset of symbols (often just one) and stays that way. Because `_scan_symbols` itself gets clobbered, the loss is persistent — the manual Refresh button (bot_container.py:2146 -> `refresh_history()` with no symbol) and the next reconnect scan both iterate the truncated set and keep missing the same symbols. This is a recurrence of the exact MEM-248 symptom the `add_scan_symbol` docstring at ccxt_connector.py:635-643 claims to have fixed.

**Fix:** Stop mutating shared state to pass an argument. Give `_scan_trade_history` an explicit `symbols: list[str] | None = None` parameter and have `refresh_history(symbol)` call `self._scan_trade_history(symbols=[symbol])` without touching `self._scan_symbols`. Guard `_scan_symbols` add/discard/read with a `threading.Lock` and always read through a snapshot copy. Optionally serialize late-registration scans onto one worker thread instead of one thread per bot.

> **Verifier correction:** LINE NUMBER OFF BY ONE: the cited line is 681, but the quoted block starts at line 680 (`if symbol:`). Lines 680-684 are the swap; 681 is `orig = self._scan_symbols.copy()`. The quote itself is verbatim. Everything else reproduced: `self._scan_symbols: set[str] = set()` at line 254 (plain set, no lock anywhere — I grepped every `_scan_symbols` occurrence in the tree: 254, 604, 645, 647, 669, 681, 682, 684, plus comments); thread spawn at 651-659 (`threading.Thread(target=self.refresh_history, kwargs={"symbol": symbol}, daemon=True, ...)`); the racing reader at 604 (`symbols = list(self._scan_symbols) if self._scan_symbols else []`). Call sites confirmed exactly as claimed: bot_container.py:1757 inside `for bot in self._bots.values():` (loop starts 1756, immediately after `self._connector = connector` at 1754), bot_container.py:2083 in add_bot, main_window.py:5827 pre-sync_connect (sync_connect is at 5837, so _ccxt_sync is None there and no thread spawns — the scanner got this right). set_connector is invoked at main_window.py:5973, i.e. AFTER sync_connect, so _ccxt_sync IS set and the loop does spawn. I additionally traced a lost-update the scanner did not spell out and which is worse than the one it describes: add_scan_symbol's idempotency guard at 645 (`if symbol in self._scan_symbols: return`) reads the same unsynchronised attribute, so while thread-A holds the set at {A}, the main loop's next `add_scan_symbol(B)` adds B to {A}, and thread-A's restore at 684 writes back its pre-swap `orig` snapshot — permanently dropping B.

### `src/gui/bot_visualizer.py:3045` — `logger` is undefined in bot_visualizer.py — the exception handler in update_bots() raises NameError instead of degrading
`broken-ref` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** bot_visualizer.py never imports `logging` and never assigns a module-level `logger`. Line 3045 is the ONLY occurrence of the name `logger` in the entire 3,257-line module. The handler is meant to be "best-effort" — swallow a list-mirror failure and continue — but instead it raises `NameError: name 'logger' is not defined`, which escapes `update_bots()` entirely.

**Operator sees:** Whenever `_refresh_bot_list_rows()` raises (e.g. a bot status carries a non-numeric `ytd_folded_usd`/`ytd_scrummed_usd` so `float()` throws at bot_visualizer.py:2517-2522, or BotListView/LaneWireCanvas raises), the whole Bot Swarm refresh aborts. The locust grid + list view freeze on stale data and every subsequent panel update in that refresh tick (exchange selector rebuild, visible-bot filter, quick-routing scope, wire-canvas raise at lines 3048-3082) is skipped. The operator sees a Bot Swarm tab that silently stops updating with no log line explaining why.

**Fix:** Add `import logging` and `logger = logging.getLogger("acervator.gui")` at module top (matching native_chart.py:19-24 and bot_wizard.py:13-16, which both do this correctly).

> **Verifier correction:** The defect is real but the claimed EFFECT overstates it. Claim says the operator gets 'a Bot Swarm tab that silently stops updating with no log line explaining why.' False — the sole caller wraps the call: src/gui/main_window.py:4825-4829 `try: self._bot_viz.update_bots(all_statuses) / except Exception as e: logger.error("DASHBOARD: Bot Viz CRASHED: %s", e); import traceback; traceback.print_exc()`. So the NameError IS logged at ERROR level with a full traceback. Everything else holds: the refresh tick aborts, and lines 3048-3082 (grid re-layout, wire-canvas raise, exchange-selector rebuild, visible-bot filter, quick-routing scope) are skipped.

### `src/gui/history_tab.py:699` — History tab trade grader inverts past and future - page rows are newest-first, not oldest-first
`correctness` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** page_rows comes from self._filtered[start:end], which preserves the order of self._all_trades. src/gui/history_helpers.py:295 sorts that list `all_trades.sort(key=lambda r: r.get("timestamp", 0), reverse=True)` and its docstring says "Returns newest-first list". So a LOWER index means a LATER trade. _grade_row treats j < row_i as "prior" (feeding statistics.median into PriceContext.ref_price_at_decision, line 718-720) and j > row_i as "future" (feeding PriceContext.future_prices). Both are backwards: the reference price is the median of trades that happened AFTER the graded trade, and the MFE/MAE "future prices" are trades that happened BEFORE it. src/trading/trade_grader.py:71 documents future_prices as "next N candles after the trade".

**Operator sees:** Every letter in the History tab's Grade column is computed with time running backwards. Good entries get D/F, bad entries get A/B, and the tooltip confidently explains the wrong verdict ("traded near local extreme with the trend").

**Fix:** Reverse the comparison: for a newest-first page, j > row_i are the prior (older) trades and j < row_i are the future (newer) ones. Swap the two append targets, and take same_sym_prior_prices[:5] (nearest-in-time older) rather than [-5:]. Add a pin test asserting that a synthetic buy-the-dip sequence grades A.

> **Verifier correction:** Reproduced end to end. history_helpers.py:295 is `all_trades.sort(key=lambda r: r.get("timestamp", 0), reverse=True)` and its docstring line 252 says 'Returns newest-first list of normalized trade dicts.' history_tab.py:361-392 assigns that result straight to self._all_trades; _apply_filters (491-505) appends in iteration order without re-sorting; _render_page slices `self._filtered[start:end]` at line 534 and passes it to _grade_row at 580. Lines 699-702 are verbatim, so j < row_i (a lower index = a LATER trade) is fed to same_sym_prior_prices -> statistics.median -> PriceContext.ref_price_at_decision (706-720), and j > row_i (EARLIER trades) is fed to future_prices. trade_grader.py:71 does document future_prices as 'Future prices = next N candles after the trade.' The time axis is inverted.

### `src/gui/simulator_tab/fleet/fleet_replay_controller.py:56` — Anchored screening runs are indistinguishable from authoritative parity runs in the persisted run record
`data-integrity` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `anchored` is written in three places (fleet_replay_panel.py:871, 913, 918) and read nowhere in src/, tools/ or main.py. `candles_skipped` is incremented at fleet_replay_controller.py:451 and read only by tests. Neither appears in `_refresh_progress`'s status line (panel lines 1271-1276) nor in the `finish_run(summary={...})` payload written to ~/.acervator_logs/sim/ (controller lines 558-575). The controller's own comment at lines 436-448 says a skipped candle 'cannot produce a sim trade, so sim-only divergence ... becomes undetectable' and 'Hence: screening pass, never the authoritative parity run' — yet nothing records which kind of run was persisted. Worse, `candles_played` at line 458 counts skipped candles, so the summary's `trades_per_1k_candles` and `candles_per_s` are computed over a denominator inflated by candles that were never evaluated.

**Operator sees:** The default mode is anchored (checkbox unchecked, panel line 245), so most persisted runs are screening passes. Reviewing ~/.acervator_logs/sim/runs/*/meta.json later, the operator cannot tell a 28%-evaluated screening run from a full parity run, and the throughput/trade-density numbers used to diagnose the 29x GUI-vs-headless gap (v3.24.19, controller lines 543-549) are diluted by skipped candles.

**Fix:** Add `anchored`, `candles_skipped` and `candles_evaluated` (= candles_played - candles_skipped) to the finish_run summary at controller lines 558-575, and surface the anchored flag in `_refresh_progress`'s status line. Compute `trades_per_1k_candles` over evaluated candles, not played candles.

> **Verifier correction:** None.

### `src/gui/simulator_tab/fleet/fleet_replay_controller.py:659` — Anchor indices are computed on a uniform 5-minute grid but consumed as master-clock union indices
`correctness` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `build_anchor_indices` maps a trade timestamp to an index by assuming a gapless uniform grid from `base_ts_ms` at `step_ms` (300,000 ms). The consumer compares that set against `candle_i` in `_run()` (line 449-450), and `candle_i` counts MasterClock ticks — indices into the sorted UNION of the series' actual timestamps (master_clock.py:62-76). The two coincide only if the union contains a candle at every 5-minute slot from base_ts. Additionally the panel sizes the anchor space with `_n = ... or max(len(r) for r in candles.values())` (fleet_replay_panel.py:883-884), i.e. the LONGEST single series, while the controller actually iterates `total_clock_ticks()` = the union size (controller line 292); whenever two series carry different timestamp sets the union is strictly larger, so anchors with `idx >= n_candles` are silently dropped at line 662.

**Operator sees:** In the DEFAULT (anchored) mode, the read head can evaluate candles that are not the ones carrying historical trades, and trades near the end of the window can lose their anchor entirely. The result is a validation run that reports parity numbers derived from the wrong candles, with nothing on screen indicating the misalignment.

**Fix:** Build anchors from the same index space the controller iterates: after `_build_sim`, resolve each trade timestamp against `exchange.master_clock.timestamps` with a bisect, rather than dividing by a nominal step. At minimum, replace `_n` at panel line 883-884 with `exchange.total_clock_ticks()`.

> **Verifier correction:** The claim understates its own second half. At fleet_replay_panel.py:883-884, `_n = self._controller.progress.total_candles or max(len(r) for r in candles.values())` — the first operand is ALWAYS 0 at that point, because the controller is constructed at panel:831 (ReplayProgress() with total_candles=0) and `controller.start()`, which sets total_candles from the union, is not scheduled until panel:944-945, well after the anchor build. So _n is unconditionally the longest single series, never the union size. The claim marked this 'suspected'; the index-space mismatch is provable from code and I am upgrading it to confirmed. The unverifiable part remains the same: I did not open the operator's Stone Tablet files, so I cannot quantify how many holes exist relative to a strict 5-minute grid.

### `src/gui/simulator_tab/fleet/fleet_replay_panel.py:723` — Per-symbol tablet 'shortfall' is measured against the FULL YTD span while candles are fetched from the soft-start cap — every symbol reports a fake coverage gap
`correctness` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `_expected_per_sym` is computed at line 673-674 from the FULL YTD span: `_ytd_days = (now_ms - YTD_START_MS) / 86_400_000` then `_expected_per_sym = int(_ytd_days * 288)`. It is never recomputed. But v3.24.16 then narrows the fetch window at lines 700-706 (`_since_ms = YTD_START_MS`, overwritten by `_since_ms = _win.replay_start_ms` when the soft-start cap exists), and line 715-720 fetches `reg.get_candles(asset=..., since_ms=_since_ms, until_ms=now_ms, ...)`. So `len(rows)` covers [soft_start, now] while `_expected_per_sym` covers [YTD_START, now]. The difference is reported as a tablet defect.

**Operator sees:** With the soft-start cap active (e.g. gate coverage starting mid-June against an April 1 YTD start), the Activity Log's per-symbol table reports every tablet at ~40% coverage with a ~20,000-candle shortfall, and line 770-773 then instructs the operator to re-run `stone_tablets.fetcher build-ytd` to fix tablets that are already complete.

**Fix:** Recompute the expected count from the window actually fetched: move the `_ytd_days` / `_expected_per_sym` calculation to after `_since_ms` is finalised (line 706) and base it on `(now_ms - _since_ms)`. Also drop the hardcoded literal '(122 days × 288 5m candles)' at line 743, which contradicts the computed `_ytd_days` printed in the same string.

> **Verifier correction:** One clause of the claimed EFFECT is wrong: lines 770-773 ('Fix: run ... build-ytd') are inside `if skipped_no_tablet:` (line 765), i.e. they fire only for symbols with NO tablet at all — they are NOT triggered by shortfall. So the operator is not directly instructed to rebuild complete tablets; the harm is confined to the misleading coverage percentage and shortfall column at lines 741-760. Severity downgraded high -> medium accordingly (operator-facing misinformation, no trading effect).

### `src/gui/simulator_tab/nuclear_controller.py:298` — Nuclear scout is built WITHOUT sim_mode=True — writes sim reservations into the operator's live capital registry
`data-integrity` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The Fleet path constructs its bots as `ScrummingBot(bot_config, exchange, enable_phantoms=False, sim_mode=True)` (fleet_replay_controller.py:150-152). Nuclear's scout omits sim_mode entirely, so `self._sim_mode` is False. `ScrummingBot._ensure_capital_reservation` (scrumming_bot.py:956) is gated ONLY by `if getattr(self, "_sim_mode", False): return` at scrumming_bot.py:987; its own v3.24.14 comment says the registry 'PERSISTS to ~/.acervator/reservation_state.json' and that the prior leak produced '16,558 bot_ids in that file against 35 real ones — 16,523 orphans, 6.5 MB of accumulated sim residue'. Setting `scout._bus = self._sim_bus` at nuclear_controller.py:299 closes the event-bus route but NOT the on-disk reservation route, which the same comment calls out as 'worse: that one was in-memory per-process, this one survives on disk and feeds live allocation decisions.'

**Operator sees:** Every Nuclear Mode scout run pollutes ~/.acervator/reservation_state.json with orphan sim bot_ids and emits 'over-commit ... > total holdings 0' warnings, and those reservations feed real capital-allocation decisions on the live side. Currently latent because Nuclear Mode has no tapes (see the dead-cache findings), but pending task #3 'Re-point Nuclear Mode tapes to Stone Tablets' arms it the moment tapes exist.

**Fix:** Add `sim_mode=True` to the ScrummingBot construction at nuclear_controller.py:298 and drop the now-redundant manual `scout._bus = self._sim_bus` at line 299 (sim_mode already swaps in a private EventBus at scrumming_bot.py:307-313).

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded high -> medium. The claim's own hedge is correct and load-bearing: Nuclear Mode currently has no tapes (sadp/ does not exist), and nuclear_mode_panel.py:258 disables the Start button when list_tapes() is empty, so _construct_scout is unreachable today. This is a latent defect armed by pending task #3, not an active leak.

### `src/trading/scrumming_bot.py:7309` — Smart Ceiling FOLD gate compares a quote-denominated position against a USD ceiling
`correctness` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** Missing the `* self._quote_to_usd` factor. Two lines later this value is compared against `_mem253_smart_ceiling_usd = _anchor * _smart_mult_253` (line 7320-7322), where `_anchor` is `_anchor_target_balance` — operator-stated USD. The identical computation is done correctly at three other sites: the `ceiling_ratio` property (line 3145-3146), the fold-taper log (line 7838-7841 `_ceiling_pos_usd = self._current_holdings * ticker.last * float(self._quote_to_usd or 1.0)`), and `_pre_buy_allowed` (line 4092). This one site is the outlier.

**Operator sees:** On any crypto-quoted pair, _quote_to_usd is >> 1 (e.g. ~100000 for a /BTC pair), so the position is understated by that factor and `_mem253_at_smart_ceiling` is effectively always False. The Smart Ceiling maturity stop on the FOLD side never engages, so the bot keeps compounding past the operator's configured position_ceiling_multiple with no 'FOLD HOLD (Smart Ceiling)' log ever emitted. The value is also written into gate.log's fold_fixture (mem253_current_pos), so post-hoc forensics on those bots read a wrong position number.

**Fix:** `_mem253_current_pos = float(self._current_holdings) * float(ticker.last) * float(self._quote_to_usd or 1.0)` — matching line 7839. Better: replace all four sites with the existing `self.position_value_usd` property (line 3014) so there is exactly one implementation.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded high -> medium: the failure requires BOTH a crypto-quoted pair (_quote_to_usd != 1) AND config.position_ceiling_enabled=True. On USD-quoted pairs the line is arithmetically identical to the three correct sites.

### `src/trading/scrumming_bot.py:6705` — All 27 tick() early returns skip the gate-fixture write, so cartridge/wire-stack/manual/entry fires log null fixtures
`data-integrity` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `_last_gate_state` is written at only two places: the SCRUM block (6694-6750) and the FOLD block (7414-7455). tick() has 27 return statements before line 6705 (4553, 4646, 4668, 4694, 4768, 4901, 4942, 4990, 5006, 5253, 5278, 5292, 5358, 5376, 5414, 5436, 5468, 5494, 5532, 5549, 5577, 5590, 5622, 5652, 5705, 5767, 5779). Four of those returns are TAKEN IMMEDIATELY AFTER A TRADE FIRES and after `_emit_gate_decision_at_fire` has already read the dict: WIRE_STACK (5006), CARTRIDGE (5278), AUTO_DETONATION (5292), ENTRY (5652), plus operator Manual Fire (4942). Because `__init__` seeds the dict with only five keys (332-336) and no fixture keys, `self._last_gate_state.get('scrum_fixture')` (4383) returns None until some tick reaches 6705.

**Operator sees:** gate.log rows for CARTRIDGE_SCRUM / CARTRIDGE_FOLD / WIRE_STACK_* / ENTRY / AUTO_DETONATION / MANUAL_* carry `scrum_fixture=null`, `fold_fixture=null` and `scrum_blockers=['pre-tick']` — the operator cannot reconstruct why any of those trades fired. Worse for at-target bots: the MEM-258 dust-band return at 4901 means an at-target bot NEVER refreshes `_last_gate_state`, so the dashboard fire button (bot_container.py:1337 -> main_window.py:1117) shows 'pre-tick' blockers permanently, and any Manual Fire from that state emits a gate row that is pure __init__ default. This is the same null-fixture symptom v3.24.18 tried to fix at 6751-6765 — but that fix only covers exception-during-write, not never-reached-write.

**Fix:** Hoist a minimal fixture write to a point every path passes — e.g. immediately after `current_value` is computed (line 4849) seed `_last_gate_state['scrum_fixture'] = {'ticker_last': float(ticker.last), 'delta': float(current_value - self._target_balance), 'stage': 'pre-gate'}` and let the full write at 6705 overwrite it. At minimum, add a `'stage'` key so consumers can distinguish 'gates never evaluated this tick' from 'gates evaluated and passed'. Also correct the `_emit_gate_decision_at_fire` docstring (4358-4365) to list CARTRIDGE/WIRE_STACK/ENTRY as out-of-band, not in-tick-refreshed.

> **Verifier correction:** Two supporting assertions are overstated. (1) The docstring is NOT false for the cartridge/detonation paths: 4361-4365 already says 'for out-of-tick sites (manual_fire_tranche, self_destruct, _execute_manual_rebalance, _execute_detonation) the state reflects the most-recent autonomous tick's evaluation — informational ... not authoritative'. CARTRIDGE and WIRE_STACK both fire via _execute_manual_rebalance (5267 / 4998) and AUTO_DETONATION via _execute_detonation, so all three are already disclaimed. ENTRY (5652) is the one genuinely undisclaimed fire path. (2) 'at-target bot NEVER refreshes' is only true for a bot that has been inside the dust band continuously since restart; any bot that has taken one out-of-band tick reaches 6694-6705 and the state goes stale rather than staying literal ['pre-tick']. Severity downgraded high -> medium: this is forensic/diagnostic data quality with no trading-behavior impact.

### `src/trading/scrumming_bot.py:9811` — Stack Mode success returns the failure sentinel — caller logs SCRUM ABORTED and re-opens a stack every tick
`correctness` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The method's own docstring five lines above says the opposite: 'Returns a sentinel non-None to signal "handled" without emitting a fill price the caller can rely on' (line 9795-9796). It returns None. Every caller treats None as failure — the SCRUM path at 6973 (`if sell_fill is None or sell_fill <= 0`) emits 'SCRUM ABORTED: sell failed', skips tranche creation and leaves `_main_lots` untouched. Because no units were actually sold, `delta` is unchanged on the next action tick, the SCRUM gate arms again, `_execute_sell` is called again, and `_open_stack_from_scrum` appends another N tranches to `self._stack_tranches` — unbounded, with no dedup or 'stack already open' check anywhere in the file.

**Operator sees:** Currently masked by the AttributeError at line 9563 (which fires first). The moment that is fixed, enabling Stack Mode produces a 'SCRUM ABORTED: sell failed' line on every action tick while `_stack_tranches` grows by stack_tranche_count_target entries per tick — and in Visible mode each of those entries places a real LIMIT SELL on the exchange (line 9608), so the operator's order book fills with duplicate resting sells.

**Fix:** Introduce an explicit sentinel the callers understand — e.g. return a dedicated `STACK_OPENED` object, or (simplest) have `_execute_sell` return `float(price)` and set a `self._stack_open_for_scrum = True` flag that the SCRUM caller checks before emitting SCRUM ABORTED. Independently, add an 'is a stack already pending?' guard at the top of `_open_stack_from_scrum` that returns 0 when `any(t.get('status')=='pending' for t in self._stack_tranches)`.

> **Verifier correction:** The two-line quote begins at 9810 (`if _n > 0:`); 9811 is the `return None`. Severity downgraded high -> medium because the code is currently UNREACHABLE: _open_stack_from_scrum raises AttributeError at 9563 on every invocation (finding #2), so `_n` is never bound and the `if _n > 0` branch never evaluates. This is a correct latent finding that becomes live the moment #2 is fixed — it must be fixed in the same ship as #2, not after.

### `src/trading/scrumming_bot.py:7964` — Fold and initial-entry lot bookkeeping omits the quote-to-USD divisor that _execute_buy applies
`data-integrity` · **medium** · verdict PARTIAL · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `_execute_buy` sizes the actual order as `amount = cost / price` and then, at lines 10570-10571, `if _qrate_buy > 0 and abs(_qrate_buy - 1.0) > 1e-9: amount = amount / _qrate_buy` — and it is `amount` that is added to `self._current_holdings` (line 10656). The callers that append the matching `_main_lots` entries never apply that divisor: FOLD at 7964 (`buy_asset = buy_cost / buy_fill`, appended at 7985-7988 as `buy_asset * _share`) and INITIAL ENTRY at 5624 (`bought_units = buy_cost / entry_fill`, appended at 5625-5628). So on crypto-quoted pairs `_main_lots` accumulates `_quote_to_usd` times more units than the exchange actually bought.

**Operator sees:** On a crypto-quoted pair the MEM-069/MEM-171 invariant breaks by a factor of _quote_to_usd after the first FOLD or INITIAL ENTRY. That matters beyond diagnostics because the init handshake rebuilds holdings from lots — line 4702-4704 `self._current_holdings = sum(float(lot.get('units',0) or 0) for lot in self._main_lots)` — so after a restart the bot believes it holds ~100,000x its real position, and every downstream USD comparison (Target Delta, Smart Ceiling, cartridge threshold) is computed off that number. `_main_lots` is persisted (export_scrumming_state line 3288), so the corruption survives on disk.

**Fix:** Have `_execute_buy` return both the fill price and the base-unit amount it actually placed (or expose `self._last_buy_amount`), and have the callers append THAT value rather than re-deriving `cost/fill`. If the return signature must stay, apply the same divisor at both caller sites: `_q = float(self._quote_to_usd or 1.0); buy_asset = (buy_cost / buy_fill) / (_q if _q > 0 else 1.0)`. Add an assertion on `_main_lots_invariant_ok()` in the fold/entry tests with _quote_to_usd != 1.0.

> **Verifier correction:** The diagnosis is INVERTED at the primary anchor (7964). buy_cost on the FOLD path is in QUOTE currency, not USD: 7851 `_fusd = sum(t["usd"] for t in _eligible)`, 7856 `buy_cost = _fusd * _taper`, and the tranche 'usd' field is derived at 7043 from scrum_usd, which line 7188-7190 explicitly documents as 'scrum_usd is in QUOTE currency (the proceeds of the sell)' and multiplies by _quote_to_usd to reach true USD. The fold path re-confirms this at 7874 (`_fold_notional_usd = buy_cost * _qrate_fc`), 8209 and 8219 (`total_folded_usd += float(buy_cost) * float(self._quote_to_usd or 1.0)`). Therefore `buy_asset = buy_cost / buy_fill` at 7964 is QUOTE / (QUOTE per BASE) = BASE and is CORRECT. The defective line on the fold path is inside _execute_buy: 10570-10571 applies `amount = amount / _qrate_buy` to a cost that is already QUOTE, so the exchange under-buys by _quote_to_usd and _current_holdings is under-credited. The INITIAL ENTRY half of the claim IS correct: 5593 `buy_cost = self._target_balance` is genuinely USD, so _execute_buy's divisor is right there and 5624 `bought_units = buy_cost / entry_fill` genuinely overstates units by _quote_to_usd. Net: the invariant break the claim predicts is real at both sites and both are crypto-quoted-only, but the fix at 7964 must NOT be 'add a /_qrate' — that would double the error. The real root cause is that _execute_buy's `cost` parameter has no documented unit and receives USD from one caller and QUOTE from another.

### `src/trading/stack_math.py:138` — Stack Mode emits tranches below the exchange minimum order size whenever the 0.1% merge rule fires
`correctness` · **medium** · verdict PARTIAL · shared-with-live

**Broken:** _apply_min_order_size runs AFTER _apply_merge_rule (stack_math.py:212-215). Merging collapses tranches, so len(tranches) < n_target, but the surviving unmerged tranches still carry the ORIGINAL per-slice size scrum_size/n_target. The guard compares max_viable_n against the post-merge COUNT, not against the actual per-tranche SIZE, so it early-returns 'already fine' while individual tranches sit below min_order_size. The module's own FALSIFICATION clause (line 32) says the module is wrong if "the returned tranche list ever contains a tranche with size < min_order_size".

**Operator sees:** In Visible Stack Mode each undersized tranche is placed via guarded_place_order and rejected by the pre-flight at bot_container.py:1005 with 'PRE-FLIGHT REJECTED: SELL amount ... below min_amount'. The operator sees 'STACK TRANCHE n PLACEMENT FAILED ... Tranche cancelled' (scrumming_bot.py:9632) and silently loses that portion of the scrum size from the stack. In Invisible mode the tranche sits pending and fails the same way at fire time.

**Fix:** In _apply_min_order_size, compute the viable count from the actual per-tranche sizes rather than the count: replace the `max_viable_n >= len(tranches)` early return with a check that every tranche's size >= min_order_size, and redistribute (reduce N, even-split total_size) whenever any tranche falls short. Add a regression case with a merge-collapsing split_distance_pct so the merge/min-size interaction is pinned.

> **Verifier correction:** The stack_math defect is REAL and I reproduced it byte-for-byte: split_scrum_into_tranches(100.0, 10.0, 4, 0.06, 'quadratic', 1.0, min_order_size=3.0) returns Tranche(0, 101.0606, 5.0), Tranche(1, 101.1818, 2.5), Tranche(2, 101.3636, 2.5) — two tranches at 2.5 against a 3.0 floor, violating the module's own FALSIFICATION clause (which is at line 31, not 32). Line 138 `if max_viable_n >= len(tranches): return tranches` is exactly as quoted; the quote block spans 132-139. Call site confirmed at scrumming_bot.py:9566 with min_order from 9563.

BUT THE CLAIMED LIVE EFFECT IS REFUTED, and for a reason the scanner missed entirely. Line 9563 reads `min_order = float(getattr(self.exchange_interface, "min_order_size", 0.0) or 0.0)`. **ScrummingBot has no `exchange_interface` attribute.** I verified: `grep -rn exchange_interface --include=*.py src tools main.py` returns exactly ONE hit — line 9563 itself. Nothing assigns it. I loaded the class: MRO is ['ScrummingBot','BotContainer','object'], hasattr(ScrummingBot,'exchange_interface') is False, and there is no __getattr__. The `getattr()` guards the *inner* attribute name, not `self.exchange_interface`, so this line raises AttributeError. It sits at 9563, BEFORE the `try:` at 9565, and the caller at scrumming_bot.py:9801 has no try/except around it. So enabling Stack Mode raises AttributeError before split_scrum_into_tranches is ever reached — min_order can never be non-zero in production, and _apply_min_order_size early-returns at line 130 (`if min_order_size <= 0`). The operator would never see 'STACK TRANCHE n PLACEMENT FAILED'; they would see an unhandled AttributeError.

Corroborating evidence: tests/test_stack_mode_execution.py:157 and tests/test_stack_mode_visible.py:235 both do `self.exchange_interface = _StubExchangeInterface()` on a stub bot — the tests invent an attribute production never sets, which is why the 875-test suite is green over a broken production path.

### `src/trading/stone_tablets/fetcher.py:489` — build_universe merges USD and USDC markets into a single tablet — two different order books blended into one price series
`data-integrity` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `discover_all_exchange_markets` returns `(base, quote)` pairs and the default `quote_filter` is `("USD", "USDC")` (fetcher.py:463), so for any base listed against both quotes the loop runs twice with different `quote_currency` — but both runs call `fill_asset(base, ...)` with the SAME base. The registry's tablet key carries no quote: `key = (asset_u, exchange_id, timeframe, year)` (registry.py:351), and neither does the filename: `f"{asset.upper()}_{timeframe}_{int(year)}_{exchange_id}.json"` (storage.py:163). `ingest_candles` dedupes on timestamp only — `existing_ts = {int(r[0]) for r in tab.candles}` then `new_rows = [r for r in year_rows if int(r[0]) not in existing_ts]` (registry.py:358-360). Because `sorted()` puts ("X","USD") before ("X","USDC"), USD wins every contested timestamp and USDC silently backfills only the slots where the USD book had no trades. The resulting tablet is a blend of two markets with a single `source` string that records neither quote.

**Operator sees:** Backtests and parity runs on a dual-quoted asset replay a price series stitched from two different books. For thin alts where USD and USDC diverge, the sim would fire scrums/folds on price moves that never happened in either real market, and parity would be unmeasurable for exactly the reason the parity harness was built to eliminate.

**Fix:** Add `quote` to the tablet key and filename (`{ASSET}_{QUOTE}_{tf}_{year}_{exchange}.json`) plus a `quote` field on Tablet/TabletEntry, with a manifest migration; or, as a one-line stopgap, change the `quote_filter` default at fetcher.py:463 to `("USD",)` and make `build_universe` skip a base it has already filled under a different quote.

> **Verifier correction:** Add the reachability caveat the claim omits: build_universe has zero callers and its CLI subcommand hits the blanket refusal at fetcher.py:671-676, so this path cannot execute today. The claim's own admission that contamination of the CURRENT 406 tablets is unverified stands — I could not verify it either.

### `src/trading/stone_tablets/registry.py:192` — _persist_manifest silently deletes MANIFEST rows for any tablet whose file failed to load, orphaning it permanently
`data-integrity` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The manifest is rebuilt from scratch out of `self._tablets` only. But `_load_from_manifest` drops any entry whose file is unreadable: registry.py:174-179 `if tab is None: logger.warning(... "file is missing or unreadable — skipping") ; continue`. So a tablet that is transiently locked (Windows AV scan, OneDrive sync — the archive lives under OneDrive on this machine), truncated, or has a JSON parse error is not in `self._tablets`. The very next `ingest_candles` that appends anything calls `_persist_manifest()` (registry.py:372-373) and writes a MANIFEST that no longer contains that row. Because the registry only ever discovers tablets THROUGH the MANIFEST (`_load_from_manifest` is the sole enumerator — there is no directory glob anywhere in the module), the file becomes permanently invisible even after it becomes readable again.

**Operator sees:** One transient read failure at boot permanently drops an asset from the 406-tablet archive. The operator sees the asset silently vanish from `coverage_summary()`, the Simulator skips it as 'no tablet', and the JSON file sits on disk unreferenced. There is no error dialog — only a `logger.warning` at boot that scrolls past.

**Fix:** Make `_persist_manifest` merge rather than replace: read the existing MANIFEST first, and carry forward any row whose file still exists on disk but is not in `self._tablets`. Alternatively track skipped entries in `_load_from_manifest` (e.g. `self._unloadable: list[TabletEntry]`) and re-emit them verbatim. Also promote the skip to an operator-visible notice, not a bare logger.warning.

> **Verifier correction:** The claimed effect overstates the trigger. `_persist_manifest` fires from exactly ONE place — ingest_candles at registry.py:373 — and I grepped ingest_candles across src/, tools/, main.py: its only non-test callers are fetcher.py:267 (inside the dead GapFiller) and tools/migrate_stone_tablets.py:152. Nothing in the running application ever ingests, so 'the very next ingest_candles' cannot happen from normal app use today; it requires running the migration tool.

### `src/trading/stone_tablets/registry.py:241` — missing_ranges cannot converge — legitimately-absent candles (late listings, no-trade slots) are re-requested forever
`correctness` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The function assumes every 5-minute slot in [since_ms, until_ms] must contain a candle. It never consults `listed_at_ms` / `AvailabilityInfo` (which the same class exposes at registry.py:405) and keeps no record of slots the exchange has already been asked for and confirmed empty. Two classes of slot are permanently unfillable: (a) slots before an asset's Coinbase listing, and (b) 5m windows with no trades, which Coinbase simply does not emit. `GapFiller.fill_asset` (fetcher.py:233-278) walks whatever `missing_ranges` returns, appends 0 candles, and the identical gap is returned on the next run.

**Operator sees:** Once the fetch path is repaired (finding #1), a universe build will spend hours per run re-requesting windows that can never return data — CAP alone is ~71 chunks x 1.3s of pure waste every run — and the coverage report will show those assets as permanently gapped no matter how many times the operator runs the fill.

**Fix:** Clamp the walk to `max(since_ms, availability.listed_at_ms)` using the AvailabilityInfo the registry already computes, and persist a per-tablet `confirmed_empty_slots` (or a `filled_through_ms` watermark) so a slot the exchange has already answered 'no data' for is not re-requested. Emit the listing notice (`AvailabilityInfo.listing_notice`) instead of a gap for the pre-listing span.

> **Verifier correction:** Cited line 241 is the `def missing_ranges(` line; the quoted body is at registry.py:255-266. The 'hours per run of waste' effect is contingent on repairing findings 1 and 3 first — missing_ranges' only caller is GapFiller (fetcher.py:233), which is dead, so nothing is being wasted today.

### `tools/build_release_zip.py:321` — Every release zip is misnamed 'session27' — the session lookup reads a deleted file and silently falls back
`broken-ref` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `sadp/EPISODIC_MEMORY.json` does not exist — the entire `sadp/` tree is gone. `p.read_text()` raises FileNotFoundError, which is an OSError and is caught by the `except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError)` on line 327, returning the hardcoded literal `27`. There is no log line, no warning, no stderr note — the failure is completely invisible. The returned value flows straight into the zip filename at line 383: `base = f"acervator_session{session}_CLOSE_hop5_v{version_us}"`. The same file has two more dead sadp references: `_RAINTSIMBAT_REPORTS_REL = ("sadp", "RAIntSimBat", "reports")` on line 223 (a classification rule that can never match), and the ADDITIONAL-bucket routing built on it.

**Operator sees:** Every zip the operator builds is named `acervator_session27_CLOSE_hop5_v3_24_19.zip` regardless of the real session. Successive releases collide on filename, and the archive's session provenance — the thing the naming convention exists to record — is permanently wrong with no error to notice.

**Fix:** Point `read_session_number()` at a source that still exists (parse the session number from the repo directory name, or from the MEM/Chronicle location under docs/), and on fallback print a loud warning to stderr instead of silently returning a hardcoded integer. Also delete the dead `_RAINTSIMBAT_REPORTS_REL` rule at line 223 and the unused empty `ADDITIONAL_REGEXES` tuple at line 150.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded high -> medium: blast radius is limited to release-packaging filenames; nothing in the running application or the trading path depends on it.

### `tools/harness/gui_archetype.py:215` — GUI001 accessibility rule is a tautology — fires HIGH on every Qt class including decorative ones
`correctness` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `_INTERACTIVE_QT_WIDGETS` is a strict subset of `_QT_WIDGET_BASES`, and this line is only reached after `_is_qt_widget_class(node)` (line 182) already proved at least one base is in `_QT_WIDGET_BASES`. Therefore `is_interactive_widget` is unconditionally True and the `b in _INTERACTIVE_QT_WIDGETS` half is dead. The consequence is that GUI001 — severity 'high', which is what `ArchetypeReport.passed` keys off (line 76) — fires on purely decorative widgets (QLabel/QFrame subclasses) that have no interactive surface to make accessible.

**Operator sees:** Any Acervator display widget (status badge, chart frame, header label) that subclasses a Qt base without calling setToolTip/setAccessibleName gets a HIGH finding, which drives `passed` to False and fails the GUI archetype self-check. The operator either chases false positives or learns to ignore GUI001 — destroying the rule's value for the real interactive widgets it was written for.

**Fix:** Compute the flag from the interactive set alone: `is_interactive_widget = any(b in _INTERACTIVE_QT_WIDGETS for b in base_list)`, OR additionally check whether the class body instantiates any interactive widget (`self._interactive_instances` is already collected on line 178 and is non-empty exactly in that case).

### `tools/harness/gui_archetype.py:332` — GUI004 parent-argument heuristic treats a button's label text as a parent
`correctness` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `bool(node.args)` is True for ANY positional argument, but the single most common Qt idiom passes text, not a parent: `QPushButton("Click me")`, `QLabel("Total")`, `QCheckBox("Enable")`. Qt's own signature is `QPushButton(text, parent=None)` — the parent is the SECOND positional. So every text-labelled interactive widget is scored as having a parent and GUI004 never fires on it, which is precisely the orphaned-widget-lifetime case the rule exists to catch.

**Operator sees:** GUI004 is effectively inert across Acervator's GUI tree, since nearly every button, label and checkbox is constructed with its display text. The operator gets a rule that reports clean while genuinely parentless widgets ship.

**Fix:** Only count a positional as a parent when it is plausibly one — e.g. require `len(node.args) >= 2`, or accept a positional only when it is `self`/an attribute rather than an `ast.Constant` string. Simplest correct form: `has_parent = any(kw.arg == "parent" for kw in node.keywords) or any(not (isinstance(a, ast.Constant) and isinstance(a.value, str)) for a in node.args)`.

### `tools/harness/rules/hallucination.py:165` — Hallucination detector cannot see the dead sadp imports that exist in its own tools/ tree
`correctness` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** Two independent gaps make the module blind to the single most obviously hallucinated reference in the repo. (1) H003 only resolves imports whose dotted name starts with `src.`/`tests.`/`tools.` — `sadp._tools.orphan_widget_scan` matches none, so the unresolvable import is never checked. (2) H002's `_RETIRED_NAMES` on line 125 lists `"sadp/"` and `"sadp\\"` WITH trailing path separators, so it matches the filesystem form but not the dotted-import form `sadp._tools`. The module docstring lines 17-23 explicitly names `sadp/` as a retired subsystem the rule exists to flag.

**Operator sees:** The archetype harness reviews tools/orphan_widget_scan.py and tools/build_agents_md.py and reports them clean, while both crash on import. The operator's own hallucination-detection instrument certifies two entirely dead files as sound.

**Fix:** Add `"sadp."` to `_LOCAL_IMPORT_PREFIXES` on line 165 (and any other retired top-level package), and add the bare dotted forms `"sadp."`, `"deprecated_sadp."`, `"RAIntSimBat."` to `_RETIRED_NAMES` on line 124 so H002 catches import-form references too.

### `tools/harness/rules/scaffolding.py:409` — S003 uninitialized-attribute rule silently skips every async method
`correctness` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `ast.AsyncFunctionDef` is NOT a subclass of `ast.FunctionDef`, so this filter drops every `async def` method before the attribute-read scan runs. The same module handles both correctly everywhere else — `_collect_init_attrs` checks `(ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)` on lines 337 and 348-349, and `_find_wallclock_completion_functions` checks both on line 124. Nothing in the S003 specification (module docstring lines 32-37) or the FALSIFICATION list (lines 46-56) declares an async exclusion, so the rule silently under-reports rather than declaring a known gap.

**Operator sees:** The v3.23.87 defect class this rule was written to catch (a rename purge missing one callsite, AttributeError swallowed by a Qt slot) goes undetected in async code. Acervator's hottest paths — bot tick loops, exchange coroutines, chart fetches — are async, so the rule is blind exactly where the operator needs it.

**Fix:** Change line 409 to `if not isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):` and line 411's `__init__` skip stays as-is. Matches how the rest of the module already walks functions.

### `tools/orphan_widget_scan.py:10` — tools/orphan_widget_scan.py is a shim importing sadp._tools.orphan_widget_scan — the sadp/ tree does not exist, so the tool cannot run
`broken-ref` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The shim inserts the repo root on sys.path and imports `sadp._tools.orphan_widget_scan`. There is no `sadp/` directory at the repo root. The module raises `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'sadp'` at import time — before `main()` is ever reached. This is the same dead-SADP-tree class as the confirmed NuclearCandleSource `_default_cache_dir()` defect.

**Operator sees:** The repo's own orphan-widget detector — the tool most directly aimed at the scaffolding defects the operator keeps getting burned by — is unrunnable. Anyone invoking it gets a bare ModuleNotFoundError rather than a scan result, and may reasonably conclude the widget audit "passed".

**Fix:** Either restore the canonical implementation into this repo (e.g. `tools/_impl/orphan_widget_scan.py`) and have the shim import that, or delete tools/orphan_widget_scan.py so it stops advertising a capability the tree cannot deliver. Note: this file is outside the gui-widgets partition — flagged here only because the partition brief named it as a corroboration tool.

> **Verifier correction:** None.

### `src/competition/bot_identity.py:266` — BotIdentity fallback signature verification rubber-stamps any 32-byte signature as valid
`data-integrity` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** When the cryptography package is unavailable, _CRYPTO_OK is False (line 41-42) and BotIdentity.verify_trade routes to this method at line 230. It performs no cryptography at all — it returns True for any 32-byte blob, ignoring pubkey_bytes and msg entirely.
