That return value is the R28 integrity gate for the append-only trade log: MerkleTradeLog.append raises ValueError 'Invalid signature on trade seq=...' only 'if not skip_sig_verify and not BotIdentity.verify_trade(record)' (merkle_log.py:143-145). So in fallback mode a forged TradeRecord carrying any 64-hex-char signature is admitted into the Merkle log and becomes part of the published commitment. The comment says 'accept as valid in test mode', but there is no test-mode flag anywhere — this is the production path whenever the import fails, and the downgrade is completely silent: no warning is logged at import time (lines 33-42), and no field in submission_summary() or the on-chain payload records that the log was built without real signatures. The module header's own claim at line 19 is 'R28: All cryptographic failures raise explicitly — no silent fallbacks.'

**Operator sees:** If the cryptography wheel ever fails to load — a broken venv, a stripped frozen build, an unsupported platform in the Mac-Mini install manifest — the PoA layer degrades from Ed25519 authorship proof to no verification whatsoever, with zero operator-visible signal. Every downstream artifact (merkle_root, submission_summary, the on-chain ResultSubmitted event) continues to assert cryptographic authorship it no longer has.

**Fix:** Make the degradation loud and narrow. At minimum, log a WARNING in the ImportError handler at line 41 naming the consequence, and add a 'crypto_backend' field ('ed25519' or 'hmac-fallback') to MerkleTradeLog.submission_summary() so a consumer can refuse an unsigned log. Better: make _fallback_verify actually verify by recomputing HMAC(secret, msg) — which requires the verifier to hold the secret, so it is only sound for self-verification — and have the static verify_trade raise RuntimeError('signature verification unavailable: cryptography not installed') rather than silently returning True for third-party records. Best: honour the header's own R28 claim and refuse to construct a BotIdentity at all when _CRYPTO_OK is False, since cryptography is already a hard dependency in pyproject.toml.

> **Verifier correction:** Minor cite drift: _fallback_verify's def is at line 263 (262 is the @staticmethod decorator, which is the quote's first line); verify_trade's def is at 215 (214 is its decorator); _CRYPTO_OK is set at lines 40/42, not 41-42. Severity downgraded medium -> low: doubly latent — cryptography 50.0.0 is installed and is a hard pyproject requirement, AND the whole competition subsystem has no reachable GUI surface.

### `src/competition/competition_engine.py:110` — CompetitionEngine writes adjudication results into the process CWD, not ~/.acervator/
`data-integrity` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The default is a bare relative path, so _save_result (line 370) resolves it against whatever directory the process happens to be running in and does 'self._results_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)' followed by 'path.write_text(...)' at lines 375-377. For a GUI app launched from the repo root or an install directory, that means a 'competition_results/' tree materialises inside the source/install tree rather than under ~/.acervator/. Crucially, run_demo_competition takes care to redirect the other two persistence sinks to a tempdir — BotIdentity paths via os.path.join(tmpdir, f"bot{i}.json") at local_testnet.py:583, and the TokenLedger via os.path.join(tmpdir, "ledger.json") at line 589 — but it constructs 'CompetitionEngine(season=season, symbol=symbol, ledger=ledger)' at line 590 WITHOUT passing results_dir, so the one sink it forgets is the one that escapes the tempdir. The same relative-default pattern is repeated at TokenLedger.LEDGER_FILE = "acrv_ledger.json" (token_ledger.py:70), BotIdentity.KEY_FILE = "bot_identity.json" (bot_identity.py:116) and RatingRegistry.REGISTRY_FILE = "elo_registry.json" (challenge_protocol.py:94) — any direct construction of those classes writes to CWD too. The rest of the platform gets this right: shared_testnet.py:54 uses 'DEFAULT_PERSIST_PATH = Path.home() / ".acervator" / "testnet_chain.json"'.

**Operator sees:** The first time a PoA competition is adjudicated after the testnet UI is un-shelved, a competition_results/ directory with one JSON per competition appears inside the operator's install or source tree — outside the ~/.acervator/ runtime root where everything else lives, invisible to backup and reset flows, and untracked by SharedTestnetBridge.reset() (which only unlinks its own _persist_path at shared_testnet.py:203-204).

**Fix:** Default to the runtime root like the rest of the platform: 'self._results_dir = Path(results_dir) if results_dir else (Path.home() / ".acervator" / "competition_results")'. Do the same for TokenLedger.LEDGER_FILE, BotIdentity.KEY_FILE and RatingRegistry.REGISTRY_FILE, or make all four require an explicit path with no default. Separately, pass results_dir=tmpdir at local_testnet.py:590 so run_demo_competition is fully hermetic.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded medium -> low: no reachable code path calls adjudicate() (both competition GUI tabs are None), so no artifact can be produced today. One additional instance the claim missed: src/gui/competition_tab.py:200 has the same relative default, 'data_dir: str = "competition_data"', and mkdirs it in __init__ at line 204 — same class of bug, same currently-unreachable status.

### `src/competition/local_testnet.py:486` — merkle_root.lstrip("0x") silently corrupts any root beginning with a zero before it is committed on-chain
`data-integrity` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** str.lstrip(chars) removes every leading character that is a member of the SET {'0','x'} — it is not a prefix strip. The roots handed to this method come from MerkleTradeLog.root (src/competition/merkle_log.py:157), which returns a bare 64-char SHA-256 hex digest with no '0x' prefix, so the call can only do harm. Any digest starting with '0' loses that nibble; ljust then pads a '0' back onto the RIGHT end, shifting the entire value one nibble left. Probability a SHA-256 hex digest starts with at least one '0' is 1/16 = 6.25%; roots starting with '00' shift two nibbles, and so on. The corrupted bytes are then written into LocalRegistry._subs[comp_id][wallet]['merkle_root'] (line 363) and emitted in the ResultSubmitted event (line 377) as the competition's cryptographic commitment. Nothing compares it back against the off-chain root, so the mismatch is silent.

**Operator sees:** Roughly one competition in sixteen records an on-chain commitment that does not match the trade log it is supposed to commit to. The entire point of the PoA layer — 'The root hash is a compact commitment to all trades' — silently fails, and it fails in exactly the cases nobody tests. Latent today: the only caller of run_demo_competition is TestnetTab (src/gui/testnet_tab.py:405, :473), which main_window.py:3810 sets to None per MEM-178, so the shelved UI is the only trigger. It fires the moment PoA is un-shelved.

**Fix:** Replace with a real prefix strip and a length assertion: 'h = merkle_root[2:] if merkle_root.startswith("0x") else merkle_root' then 'if len(h) != 64: raise ValueError(f"merkle root must be 64 hex chars, got {len(h)}")' then 'root = bytes.fromhex(h)'. Drop the ljust padding entirely — silently padding a short root to 64 chars is the same class of bug as the lstrip.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded high -> low. The claim itself correctly flags this as latent, and I confirmed the latency is total: BOTH GUI entry points to the competition subsystem are hard-nulled — main_window.py:3804 'self._competition_tab = None' and :3810 'self._testnet_tab = None'. SharedTestnetBridge.request_competition (shared_testnet.py:192) is the only enqueue path and its sole callers are inside testnet_tab.py. Nothing reachable can trigger this today.

### `src/competition/token_ledger.py:205` — TokenLedger.load() appends without clearing — a second load doubles every ACRV balance and the next award() persists the doubled ledger
`data-integrity` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** load() appends into self._events unconditionally. It never clears the list first, and unlike award() (which checks 'if event_id in self._seen: return None' at line 96) it never consults the idempotency set it is populating. Calling load() twice on the same instance — or calling it on an instance that has already recorded awards in memory — duplicates every AwardRecord. Because all balances are derived by replaying the list (balance() sums at line 144, total_minted() at line 155, season_minted() at line 161, leaderboard() at line 167, supply_summary() at line 183), every figure doubles. The corruption then becomes permanent: the next successful award() calls self.save() at line 135, which writes 'events': [e.to_dict() for e in self._events] (line 198) — the duplicated list — back over the file. The module docstring at lines 5-6 states 'Awards are idempotent: awarding the same competition result twice produces exactly one entry (R29)'; load() is the hole in that guarantee. MerkleTradeLog.load (src/competition/merkle_log.py:239-243) has the identical unguarded-append shape, where a double load changes the Merkle root itself.

**Operator sees:** The hard-capped 10,000,000 ACRV supply and every bot's balance can be silently inflated by a reload, and the inflated figure is written back to disk on the next award — turning a transient read bug into permanent ledger corruption. The supply-cap guard at line 100 then starts rejecting legitimate awards with 'Supply cap 10,000,000 ACRV would be exceeded'.

**Fix:** Make both loaders idempotent. In TokenLedger.load, clear first ('self._events.clear(); self._seen.clear()') before the loop, or skip records already seen ('if record.event_id in self._seen: continue'). Apply the same to MerkleTradeLog.load (clear _records and _leaves before replaying). Add a regression test that calls load() twice and asserts total_minted() and root are unchanged.

> **Verifier correction:** The anchor 205 is the 'for rd in ...' line; the quoted block begins at 204 ('data = json.loads(...)'). Severity downgraded medium -> low: the only in-tree consumer that calls TokenLedger.load() is CompetitionTab (src/gui/competition_tab.py:206-207), and it calls load() exactly once per construction — and CompetitionTab is set to None at main_window.py:3804, so it is never constructed.

### `src/core/notifications.py:330` — SMS alert channel is dead (wrong arg order + config never reaches engine) yet reports itself as delivered
`correctness` · **low** · verdict PARTIAL · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `SMSEngine.send` is `send(self, message: str, event_type: str = "info")`. The call passes the phone number as `message` and the alert text as `event_type`. Inside `send`, `type_checks.get(event_type, False)` (sms_engine.py:85) looks the alert text up in a dict keyed by 'buy_fill'/'error'/etc., misses, and returns False. It never gets that far anyway: `SMSEngine.update_config` has zero callers, so the singleton keeps `SMSConfig(enabled=False)` and `send` bails at line 73-74. Worse, `send` returns False rather than raising, so back in `NotificationManager.send` (line 234) `channels_sent.append(channel.name)` still runs — the persisted Notification record claims SMS was sent.

**Operator sees:** DRAWDOWN_CRITICAL is the only rule routed to SMS (notifications.py:113). The operator configures a phone number in the Alerts tab, a critical drawdown fires, the notification history shows the alert with channel 'SMS' recorded — and no text message is ever sent. The one channel that reaches the operator when they are away from the machine silently does nothing.

**Fix:** In `_send_sms`, push the phone/provider config into the engine and call with the right signature: build an `SMSConfig` from `self._sms_config`, `engine.update_config(cfg)`, then `ok = engine.send(f"{title}: {message}", event_type="error")`; raise (or return False and have `send` skip the append) when `ok` is False so `channels_sent` stops lying. Also give `NotificationManager.configure_sms` a path that actually reaches `SMSEngine`.

> **Verifier correction:** The code facts are exactly right; the reachability story is wrong. Reproduced: `SMSEngine.send` is `def send(self, message: str, event_type: str = 'info') -> bool` at sms_engine.py:72, so notifications.py:330 `engine.send(phone, f'{title}: {message}')` does pass the phone as `message` and the alert text as `event_type`. `type_checks.get(event_type, False)` is at sms_engine.py:85 exactly. `update_config` (sms_engine.py:69) has zero callers — the only `update_config` call in the tree is src/gui/settings_dialog.py:723, and I read 705-730: it constructs `SoundConfig` and calls `SoundEngine.update_config`. DRAWDOWN_CRITICAL routes to SMS at notifications.py:113 verbatim, and channels_sent.append is at line 234 with no re-raise. WHAT THE SCANNER MISSED: `_send_sms` never reaches line 330 at all. Line 324-326 is `phone = self._sms_config.get('phone')` / `if not phone: return`, and `_sms_config` is only populated by `configure_sms` (line 187), whose sole caller is src/gui/alerts_tab.py:206 inside `AlertsTab._save_config`. `AlertsTab` is never instantiated: src/gui/main_window.py:3842-3844 reads `# --- Alerts & Notifications — REMOVED per P1.7 / MEM-178 ---` / `# Underlying NotificationManager kept intact.` / `self._alerts_tab = None`, and src/gui/stock_main_window.py:381 does the same. So the operator cannot configure a phone number, `_sms_config` stays `{}`, and the arg-order bug is unreachable dead code. The 'notification record falsely claims SMS was sent' half is still technically true (the early `return` raises nothing, so channels_sent gets 'SMS'), but the history is also unviewable since the tab that rendered it was removed.

### `src/core/version_sweep.py:955` — version_sweep recreates the deprecated sadp/RAIntSimBat/ tree in the repo root on every run
`data-integrity` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `sadp/RAIntSimBat/` is the deprecated tree — the same one the Nuclear Mode empty state was called out for referencing. `mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` does not just tolerate the missing path, it manufactures it. And `main()` calls `save_json_report` unconditionally (line 1138), so every sweep run resurrects `sadp/RAIntSimBat/reports/` in a repo where `sadp/` was deleted. Related scaffolding: the `--json` CLI flag is still declared at lines 1128-1130 but `args.json` is never read — the v3.19.12 comment at 1136-1137 acknowledges the write is now unconditional but the flag was left behind.

**Operator sees:** Running the release gate silently re-creates a directory tree the project deliberately deleted, so the next person to look at the repo finds `sadp/RAIntSimBat/` present again and has to work out whether it is live. `--json` in `--help` implies the report is opt-in when it is always written.

**Fix:** Point reports_dir at a live location — `ROOT / "docs" / "audits"` or `~/.acervator_logs/_meta/` via src/core/log_paths.get_meta_dir() — and either honour `args.json` or drop the flag from the parser.

> **Verifier correction:** Correct as stated. Lines 955-956 are verbatim `reports_dir = ROOT / "sadp" / "RAIntSimBat" / "reports"` / `reports_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`. `ls -d sadp` -> No such file or directory, so `parents=True` manufactures the deprecated tree rather than tolerating its absence. main() calls it unconditionally at line 1138 with the v3.19.12 comment at 1136-1137 exactly as quoted. The `--json` flag is declared at 1128-1130 with help text 'Save JSON report to sadp/RAIntSimBat/reports/', and I confirmed `args.json` appears nowhere else: `grep -n "args\.json|args\.report|args\.fix"` returns 1132 (args.fix), 1136-1137 (the comment mentioning args.json), 1141 (args.report) — no read of args.json.

### `src/exchange/ccxt_connector.py:193` — `_with_retry` treats ccxt.BadRequest as retryable because "request" is matched as a substring
`correctness` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The keyword `"request"` was added to catch `ccxt.RequestTimeout`, but it is matched as a substring against the lowercased class name, so it also matches `ccxt.BadRequest` -> `"badrequest"`. `BadRequest` is a permanent 4xx (malformed params, unsupported argument, bad granularity) — retrying it cannot succeed. Every decorated read method (`get_ticker` line 812, `get_orderbook` 856, `get_ohlcv` 868, `get_balances` 889, `get_balance` 917, `cancel_order` 1080, `get_order` 1087, `get_open_orders` 1094, `get_my_trades` 1107) therefore burns 3 API calls and sleeps 1 s + 2 s before surfacing an error that was permanent on the first attempt. The `"timeout"` keyword already covers `RequestTimeout`, so `"request"` is redundant for its intended purpose.

**Operator sees:** A misconfigured request — e.g. asking Coinbase for a 4h candle granularity it does not serve — takes 3+ seconds and 3 wasted API calls to report instead of failing immediately, and inflates the CPM the operator is trying to keep under 600. During a period of many such errors the amplification is 3x on every read.

**Fix:** Drop `"request"` from the tuple (`"timeout"` already matches `RequestTimeout`), or better, replace the ad-hoc tuple with `from .circuit_breaker import _is_transient_error` so the retry classifier and the breaker classifier cannot drift apart again.

> **Verifier correction:** Lines 193-196 verbatim as quoted, cited line 193 exact; the test is `kw in exc_name.lower()` with no word boundary (line 194). I went further than the scanner and executed against the installed ccxt: `ccxt.BadRequest` exists and `any(kw in 'badrequest' for kw in ('ratelimit','timeout','network','request'))` evaluates True. Also verified the neighbours: BadSymbol, InvalidOrder, ArgumentsRequired, NotSupported, OperationRejected all evaluate False, so BadRequest is the only false-positive from the 'request' keyword (BadSymbol subclasses BadRequest in ccxt but its own class name does not match, so it is correctly not retried). RequestTimeout is already covered by the 'timeout' keyword, confirming 'request' is redundant for its stated purpose. Decorated set confirmed at 812, 856, 868, 889, 917, 1080, 1087, 1094, 1107. Drift against circuit_breaker.py's exact-pattern classifier (TRANSIENT_ERROR_NAME_PATTERNS, lines 62-74) confirmed — it lists 'Timeout' and 'NetworkError' and no bare 'request'.

### `src/exchange/ccxt_connector.py:46` — MEM-220 module docstring points at tests/test_mem220_ccxt_serialization.py, which was archived out of the live suite
`broken-ref` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The referenced path does not exist. The file was moved to `_archive/tests_pre_2026_07_25/` in the 2026-07-25 test-suite prune, so the pin that was supposed to protect the single-worker-executor invariant is no longer collected by pytest. The `_call_sync` choke point (lines 489-578) — the fix for the Windows urllib3/OpenSSL access violation described at lines 32-46 — is now unpinned in the 875-test live suite, which is exactly the condition under which a future refactor silently reintroduces the crash.

**Operator sees:** A developer follows the pointer to understand or re-verify the serialization contract and finds nothing; the invariant that prevents a native access violation on Windows has no regression guard, so a change to `_call_sync` or `_sync_executor` ships green.

**Fix:** Restore `_archive/tests_pre_2026_07_25/test_mem220_ccxt_serialization.py` into `tests/`, confirm it passes, and delete the orphaned `tests/__pycache__/test_mem220_ccxt_serialization.*.pyc`. If the test is genuinely obsolete, replace the comment at line 46 with the real reason the pin was dropped rather than leaving a dangling path.

> **Verifier correction:** Line 46 is verbatim `# See also: tests/test_mem220_ccxt_serialization.py`. My own `find . -name "test_mem220*"` returns exactly three paths: ./.session26_backups/tests_td021/test_mem220_ccxt_serialization.py, ./_archive/tests_pre_2026_07_25/test_mem220_ccxt_serialization.py, and ./tests/__pycache__/test_mem220_ccxt_serialization.cpython-314-pytest-9.0.3.pyc. `ls tests/test_mem220*` -> No such file. The referenced path does not resolve. I also independently verified the 'now unpinned' half, which the scanner asserted without proof: `grep -rln "_call_sync|_sync_executor|MEM-220|MEM_220" tests/` matches ONLY .pyc files in tests/__pycache__ — no live .py test in the 56-file suite exercises _call_sync (defined at line 489) or _sync_executor (263, 273, 533-534, 781-783). So the single-worker-executor invariant genuinely has no regression guard. One terminology nit: line 46 is a module-level COMMENT block, not a docstring (the file's docstring is elsewhere); the finding's title says 'module docstring'.

### `src/exchange/crypto_assets.py:310` — Coingecko logo URLs for 30 bulk-registered assets all point at image directory `1` (Bitcoin's) — guaranteed 404, and the fallback URL is unreachable by construction
`correctness` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** CoinGecko's asset-image path is `/coins/images/<numeric-image-id>/small/<file>.png`, where the numeric id is per-coin — the individually registered coins get this right (BTC uses `/images/1/`, ETH `/images/279/` at line 82, XRP `/images/44/` at line 138, DOT `/images/12171/` at line 211). The bulk loop at lines 245-314 hardcodes `/images/1/` — Bitcoin's directory — for all 30 assets (MATIC, UNI, LTC, ATOM, XLM, ALGO, FIL, NEAR, APT, ARB, OP, SUI, INJ, AAVE, MKR, CRV, RUNE, FTM, SAND, MANA, GRT, RENDER, TIA, SEI, PEPE, WIF, SHIB, BONK, FLOKI, JUP), with a filename that does not exist in that directory. Every such URL 404s. The `logo_fallback_url` set on the very next line can never be tried, because `AssetManager.get_logo_url` (line 379-384) returns `asset.logo_url or asset.logo_fallback_url` — `logo_url` is always a non-empty string, so the `or` short-circuits and the fallback is dead code for these assets. `download_logo` (line 354-377) then swallows the failure at `logger.debug("Logo download failed for %s: %s", ...)` (line 376) and returns None.

**Operator sees:** Coin icons for BONK, SHIB, PEPE, MATIC, ATOM and 25 other assets never resolve — the wizard silently falls back to the generated coloured-letter circle (bot_wizard.py:52-60) forever, and nothing above DEBUG log level ever says why. The operator's live BONK bot is in the affected set.

**Fix:** Drop the bogus primary and let the working fallback win: in the bulk loop set `logo_url=""` and keep only `logo_fallback_url`, OR change `get_logo_url` (line 379-384) to probe the primary and fall through to `logo_fallback_url` on failure rather than relying on truthiness. Raise the failure log at crypto_assets.py:376 from DEBUG to INFO so the next silent breakage is visible.

> **Verifier correction:** Lines 310-311 verbatim as quoted. The bulk loop runs 245-314 over exactly the 30 symbols listed. Per-coin numeric ids confirmed distinct at 62 (/images/1/ bitcoin), 82 (/279/ ethereum), 102 (/825/), 120 (/4128/), 138 (/44/), 156 (/975/). I DID what the scanner explicitly did not: I probed the CDN. `curl -o /dev/null -w %{http_code}` gives https://assets.coingecko.com/coins/images/1/small/bonk.png -> 403 and https://assets.coingecko.com/coins/images/1/small/bitcoin.png -> 200. So the bulk URLs are empirically unreachable (403 rather than 404 — S3 no-such-key without list permission — the functional result is identical: urlopen raises HTTPError). Fallback-unreachability confirmed: get_logo_url at 379-384 returns `asset.logo_url or asset.logo_fallback_url`, and logo_url is always a non-empty f-string for these 30, so logo_fallback_url is dead for them. Silent swallow confirmed at line 376 `logger.debug("Logo download failed for %s: %s", symbol, exc)`. Consumption path confirmed: bot_wizard.py:33-64 `_get_coin_icon` -> get_logo_path -> download_logo -> coloured-letter-circle fallback at 51-64. I also checked resources/logos/ — it is EMPTY, so there is no pre-cached override that would mask the bug.

### `src/exchange/idempotency.py:102` — Idempotency coid cannot distinguish a retry from a second legitimate identical trade within the 5-minute TTL
`correctness` · **low** · verdict PARTIAL · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The fingerprint (lines 70-84) is `bot_id|symbol|side|amount:.10f|price:.8f|purpose|session_nonce` with no time or sequence component, and the cache TTL is 300 s (line 90). Two genuinely distinct trades that happen to share all six fields inside that window collide and get the SAME `coid`, which the connector forwards to Coinbase as `client_order_id` (ccxt_connector.py:1026-1028). The exchange then refuses the second order as a duplicate — the very rejection the design relies on (module docstring line 19-21: "Coinbase: 409 Duplicate"). The comment at line 111 asserts "this IS a retry" but the code has no evidence for that: it cannot tell a retry from a repeat. Compounding it, `BotContainer.guarded_place_order` calls `_idem.mark_fulfilled(_intent)` only on success (bot_container.py:1083) and the except branch deliberately does NOT invalidate (bot_container.py:1085-1092, "err on the side of keeping cached"), so once a collision rejection happens the coid stays cached and EVERY subsequent identical intent is rejected for the remainder of the TTL. `IdempotencyLayer.invalidate` (line 135) and `is_known` (line 143) have zero callers, so nothing can clear it.

**Operator sees:** A bot that scrums the same tranche size at the same price twice within five minutes would have the second trade rejected by Coinbase as a duplicate, and would then keep failing on that intent for the rest of the TTL. The operator sees a bot that stops firing with a duplicate-order error and no obvious cause, since nothing in the log explains that the block came from Acervator's own coid cache.

**Fix:** Add a monotonically increasing per-intent sequence number (or a coarse time bucket, e.g. `int(time.time() // 5)`) to the fingerprint so a genuine repeat gets a fresh coid while a true retry inside the same tick reuses it — the retry path should pass an explicit `attempt` marker rather than relying on field equality. Separately, wire `invalidate()` into the exception branch of `guarded_place_order` for non-409 rejections, which is what idempotency.py's own docstring (lines 29-30) says the design requires.

> **Verifier correction:** LINE NUMBER WRONG: the cited line is 102, which is `def derive_coid(self, intent: TradeIntent) -> str:`. The quoted 3-line block (`cached = self._cache.get(fp)` / `if cached is not None:` / `# Reuse the same coid — this IS a retry of the same intent`) is at lines 108-110, six lines below the cite. The quote itself is verbatim. Code facts all reproduce: fingerprint at 70-84 has no time or sequence component (raw = bot_id|symbol|side|amount:.10f|price:.8f|purpose|session_nonce at 78-81); TTL default 300.0 at line 90; coid forwarded as Coinbase client_order_id at ccxt_connector.py:1026-1028. bot_container.py:1076/1083 confirmed (derive_coid then mark_fulfilled ONLY on success), and the except branch at 1085-1091 is a bare `raise` with a comment explaining the deliberate non-invalidation. `invalidate` (135) and `is_known` (143) have zero callers — my grep of the whole src/tools/tests/main.py tree returns only derive_coid and mark_fulfilled as live calls. WHY PARTIAL, NOT CONFIRMED: the 'collision' is the module's stated design, not an oversight. TradeIntent's own docstring at lines 60-62 says 'identical intents collide deliberately so a retry cannot create a second order.' Calling that a correctness bug mischaracterises it. There is zero runtime evidence the collision has ever fired — the scanner admits this, and I could not obtain any either (no instrumentation, no log evidence, and the operator's runtime tree is read-only). The DEFENSIBLE sub-finding, which I confirm outright, is narrower: the module docstring at lines 29-30 promises 'On confirmed failure (4xx other than 409), marks for deletion so a future legitimate retry generates a new coid' — that behaviour is NOT implemented anywhere, and the `invalidate()` method written to provide it is dead. That is a real stale-doc + incomplete-implementation defect.

### `src/gui/bot_visualizer.py:2556` — Quick Routing Matrix does a whole-file read-modify-write on the operator's live bot_state.json, using the same temp path as StateManager and skipping its backup
`data-integrity` · **low** · verdict PARTIAL · shared-with-live

**Broken:** Three compounding problems. (1) Shared temp path: `StateManager.save_state` uses `tmp_path = self._path.with_suffix(".tmp")` where `self._path = self._dir / "bot_state.json"` (state_manager.py:59, 103) — i.e. the identical `~/.acervator/bot_state.tmp`. Two unsynchronised writers renaming the same temp file can promote a half-written or wrong-author document to bot_state.json. (2) No backup: StateManager copies the live file to `bot_state.backup.json` before every replace (state_manager.py:108-112); this GUI path does not, so the corruption-recovery copy at state_manager.py:134-136 is bypassed. (3) Whole-document clobber: `_apply_routes_to_state` calls `load_state()`, mutates one key, and writes the ENTIRE dict back — any `save_all_state()` that lands in between is reverted wholesale. Separately, the key it writes (`scrumming_state.smart_wire_routes`) is not part of the persistence schema at all: `ScrummingBot.export_scrumming_state()` never emits it, so the next `save_all_state()` erases it regardless.

**Operator sees:** Clicking Connect / Disconnect / Disconnect All in the Bot Swarm Quick Routing panel rewrites the operator's entire live bot_state.json from a stale in-memory snapshot. If BotManager persisted between the read and the write, that save is lost — fold tranches, main lots, circuit-breaker trip state, target-balance anchors all revert to whatever was on disk when the panel loaded. With the shared temp path there is also a window in which bot_state.json can end up truncated, and unlike every other write to that file no backup copy is taken first.

**Fix:** Stop writing bot_state.json from the GUI. The Connect/Disconnect handlers already emit `wire.created` / `wire.removed`, which `BotContainer._on_wire_created_mgr` (bot_container.py:1465-1495) consumes and `SmartWireManager.export_wires()` persists under the schema's own `smart_wires` key — that is the working path. Delete `_save_bot_state_dict`, `_apply_routes_to_state` and `_clear_all_routes_in_state`, and have `_hydrate_smart_wire_routes_from_disk` read `state["smart_wires"]` instead of the orphan `scrumming_state.smart_wire_routes`. If a GUI-owned write must stay, route it through `BotContainer` so it shares StateManager's temp path and backup.

> **Verifier correction:** Three of the four asserted harms do not survive verification. (a) NO CORRUPTION RACE: both writers run on the Qt GUI thread — the only save_all_state timer is `save_timer.start(60000)` on a QTimer in main.py:973-979, and the other call sites are main.py:1134 (shutdown) and main_window.py:5525 (a button slot). `_apply_routes_to_state` does load→mutate→write synchronously without yielding, so the two writers cannot interleave and `Path.replace` (os.replace) is atomic on Windows for same-volume renames. (b) NOT A STALE SNAPSHOT: `_load_bot_state_dict` (2542-2547) calls `StateManager().load_state()` — a FRESH read off disk immediately before the write — so no BotManager save is 'reverted wholesale'. (c) ROUTES ARE NOT LOST: `_on_connect_clicked` also emits `wire.created` on the bus (bot_visualizer.py:981-986), and BotManager subscribes at bot_container.py:1465 → `_on_wire_created_mgr` (1474-1485) → `self._smart_wire_mgr.register_wire(src, tgt, pct)`, which IS persisted through the schema's top-level `smart_wires` key (state_manager.py:92). Real residual defect, and it is a scaffolding/dead-write one, not data-integrity: the `scrumming_state.smart_wire_routes` key is not in the persistence schema (grep for it across src/ hits ONLY bot_visualizer.py; `grep -c smart_wire_routes src/trading/scrumming_bot.py` → 0), so the next 60-second autosave erases it — and `_hydrate_smart_wire_routes_from_disk` (2636) therefore replays nothing. Secondary real issue: `bots.setdefault(bid, {})` at 2578 can inject a phantom bot entry with only a scrumming_state stub if the bot isn't yet on disk. The 'no backup' point is technically true but immaterial given atomic replace.

### `src/gui/main_window.py:5618` — AI Monitor status lines pass a CSS colour string where StatusLog expects a severity level, silently losing the colour
`correctness` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** StatusLog.log(message, level) forwards `level` to _render_safe, which resolves it via `colors = {"info": "#00ffcc", "success": "#00ff88", "warning": "#ffaa00", "error": "#ff3366"}` and `color = colors.get(level, "#e0e0f0")` (lines 391-395). '#666' is not a key, so it falls through to the default. The same mistake appears at line 5643 where _on_ai_feedback passes `"#00ddff" if authenticated else "#ffaa00"` as the level. The author clearly intended a colour; the API takes a severity name.

**Operator sees:** AI Monitor lines — including the authenticated/unauthenticated feedback banner, which is exactly the line the operator would want colour-coded — render in the neutral default #e0e0f0 instead of cyan or amber, so an UNAUTH warning is visually indistinguishable from routine chatter.

**Fix:** Pass severity names: `"info"` at line 5618, and `"info" if authenticated else "warning"` at line 5643. If a bespoke colour really is wanted, add a hex passthrough in _render_safe: `color = colors.get(level, level if str(level).startswith("#") else "#e0e0f0")`.

> **Verifier correction:** Line 5618 is verbatim as quoted: `self._status_log.log("AI Monitor: disabled (configure in Settings → AI Monitor)", "#666")`. The API contract is confirmed: `def log(self, message: str, level: str = "info") -> None:` at line 256, which calls `self._render(ts, message, level)` at 268; `_render` (def at 305) delegates to `self._render_safe(ts, message, level)` — so the claim's 'forwards level to _render_safe' is accurate, via one hop it did not mention. _render_safe (def at 336) resolves it at 391-395: `colors = {"info": "#00ffcc", "success": "#00ff88", "warning": "#ffaa00", "error": "#ff3366",}` then `color = colors.get(level, "#e0e0f0")` — a plain dict lookup with no hex passthrough, exactly as claimed. Second call site confirmed at 5641-5643: `self._status_log.log(f"[AI MONITOR {tag}] {feedback[:200]}", "#00ddff" if authenticated else "#ffaa00")`. Worth noting the trap the claim did not call out: the else-arm passes '#ffaa00', which is the VALUE of the 'warning' key — so it looks correct at a glance but is still a miss, since dict.get is keyed on 'warning', not on the hex.

### `src/gui/shared_testnet.py:29` — shared_testnet's stated threading invariant is false - the worker mutates LocalTestnet off the GUI thread while TestnetTab reads it unlocked
`threading` · **low** · verdict PARTIAL

**Broken:** The module docstring (lines 12-16 and 29-32) says mutations land on the Qt main thread and that direct reads are "safe because all writes are marshaled through main thread". The code does the opposite: _CompetitionWorker is a QThread and its run() calls self._testnet.run_demo_competition(...) on the worker thread (lines 95-105) -- its own class docstring at line 80 even admits "The mutation itself happens here". The writer holds _mutation_lock, but the readers do not: TestnetTab's 3-second QTimer calls _refresh_blocks, which iterates the live dict at testnet_tab.py:502 `for tx in list(reversed(list(self._testnet.chain._txs.values())))[:15]:`, and _refresh_holders iterates `self._testnet.acrv._balances` (line 527-529), both without acquiring the bridge's lock. _save_now/_serialize_state (lines 247-296) likewise reads chain._txs/_blocks/_events on the GUI thread without the lock.

**Operator sees:** Running a competition (or the Stress Test x10, which queues 10 workers) can make the Block Explorer / Transaction Log / Token Holders tables blank out or stall, with at most three "TestnetTab._refresh_blocks: RuntimeError" lines on stderr and nothing thereafter. Intermittent and unreproducible on demand -- the classic symptom this docstring was written to prevent.

**Fix:** Pick one: (a) make the reads honest by exposing bridge.snapshot() that copies chain/_acrv state under _mutation_lock and have TestnetTab read only that, or (b) move the mutation onto the GUI thread as the docstring claims and drop the QThread. Either way, correct lines 12-16 and 29-32 so the documented invariant matches the code.

> **Verifier correction:** Line is off by one: the quoted 'THREADING INVARIANT (critical)' block starts at line 28, not 29 (line 29 is the box-drawing rule). Text otherwise verbatim, as are the WRITE/READ PATH claims at 10-20. The code contradiction is real: _CompetitionWorker is a QThread (line 78) whose run() calls self._testnet.run_demo_competition under self._lock at 95-105, with its own docstring at 80-81 admitting 'The mutation itself happens here'; _drain_queue calls worker.start() at 227; _serialize_state (270-296) reads chain._blocks/_txs/_events and acrv._balances on the GUI thread with no lock; testnet_tab.py:502 is verbatim `for tx in list(reversed(list(self._testnet.chain._txs.values())))[:15]:` and :527 reads self._testnet.acrv._balances, both under the 3 s QTimer (126-130) with the 3-report error cap at 155-179. BUT the race is unreachable in the shipping app: the ONLY caller of bridge.request_competition is testnet_tab.py (386-387, 457-466), and main_window.py:3810 sets `self._testnet_tab = None` ('--- Tab 8: Local Testnet — REMOVED per P1.7 / MEM-178 ---'). Nuclear/simulator code does not call request_competition (my grep for `_testnet_bridge|CompetitionRequest` outside shared_testnet.py hits only main_window:3348 and testnet_tab). The bridge is installed at main_window.py:3339-3340, but no worker is ever spawned and no concurrent reader exists.

### `src/stocks/market_hours.py:203` — MarketHours computes Eastern Time one hour wrong, year-round, on this operator's machine
`correctness` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** Two independent errors that do not cancel. (1) time.daylight is a STATIC flag meaning 'this timezone has DST rules at all', not 'DST is active right now' — the correct runtime test is time.localtime().tm_isdst. On any DST-observing machine the branch always selects altzone, so in winter the computed local_offset is one hour east of reality. (2) et_offset is hard-coded to -5 (EST) even though the adjacent comment acknowledges ET is -4 during EDT, so in summer the target offset is one hour west of reality. _to_eastern (line 197) then applies the bad delta to every session decision. Worked example on this machine (measured: time.timezone=28800, time.altzone=25200, time.daylight=1, tm_isdst=1, true UTC offset -7): local_offset = -25200/3600 = -7.0, et_offset = -5, so _tz_offset_hours = -2 and _to_eastern adds 2 hours. Local 06:30 PDT is 09:30 EDT — the opening bell — but _to_eastern yields 08:30, which get_session (line 87) classifies as PRE_MARKET, so is_market_open() returns False. The error persists in winter: with tm_isdst=0 the code still uses altzone (-7) while the true local offset is -8, so the delta is again +2 against a true +3, still one hour short.

**Operator sees:** Every consumer of MarketHours is shifted one hour late: is_market_open() reports the market closed for the first hour of the session (9:30-10:30 ET) and reports it open for an hour after the close (16:00-17:00 ET). get_status_string() — rendered by src/gui/stock_main_window.py:452 — would tell the operator 'Pre-Market (4:00-9:30 ET)' while the bell has already rung. This is the one stocks module with a live consumer, so it is the first thing that would misfire the moment finding #1 is reversed.

**Fix:** Replace the whole offset dance with a real timezone. Python 3.9+ ships zoneinfo: 'from zoneinfo import ZoneInfo; ET = ZoneInfo("America/New_York")' and make _to_eastern do 'return dt.astimezone(ET) if dt.tzinfo else dt.astimezone().astimezone(ET)'. That handles both EST/EDT and the DST transition dates correctly and deletes _detect_et_offset entirely. If a stdlib-free version is required, at minimum change time.daylight to time.localtime().tm_isdst AND derive et_offset from the same tm_isdst rather than hard-coding -5.

> **Verifier correction:** get_status_string() is called at stock_main_window.py:450, not :452 (452 is the MarketSession import line); _to_eastern's def is at 195, line 197 is its return statement. Severity downgraded high -> low: MarketHours' only consumer is StockMainWindow, which is never constructed (finding #1), so this never executes today.

### `src/trading/capital_arbiter_bridge.py:156` — capital_arbiter_bridge passes an absolute USD amount where the arbiter expects a [0,1] pool fraction, and has zero callers
`correctness` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** select_top_n_within_pool documents pool_capacity as 'total share of pool available (0.0 to 1.0)' (opportunity_arbiter.py:275) and subtracts each candidate's pool_share_needed from it — and OpportunityCandidate.pool_share_needed is explicitly 'fraction of shared pool this trade would consume. e.g. $20 trade in a $200 pool = 0.1' (opportunity_arbiter.py:91-92). The bridge's default path passes free_usd, an absolute dollar figure, as that fraction. With a $200 free pool and candidates at pool_share_needed=0.1, remaining starts at 200 and every candidate fits — the capacity cap is effectively removed (2,000 candidates would be accepted). The comment above the assignment admits the units are being mixed, and the 30-line stream-of-consciousness block at lines 124-148 shows the author never resolved it.

**Operator sees:** Currently none — nothing calls rank_for_pool. The moment it is wired (the docstring at line 22 says the operator opts in by calling it), the pool-capacity cap silently does nothing and the arbiter returns every candidate above the signal threshold as fundable.

**Fix:** Make the unit explicit in the signature: require the caller to pass total pool USD, compute pool_capacity = free_usd / total_usd, and validate it is in [0,1]; or change the arbiter to work in absolute USD and rename pool_share_needed to pool_share_usd. Delete the abandoned reasoning block at lines 124-148 either way — it documents a decision that was never made.

> **Verifier correction:** Verified. capital_arbiter_bridge.py:149-150 is the arbiter_kwargs.pop, :151 the None test, :152-155 the comment, :156 `pool_capacity_fraction = free_usd` — the cited line, exact. free_usd does come from registry.get_free at :108-112. Arbiter contract confirmed independently: opportunity_arbiter.py:275-277 documents pool_capacity as 'total share of pool available (0.0 to 1.0)'; :286 `remaining = pool_capacity`; :290 `if s.candidate.pool_share_needed > remaining: continue`; :293 `remaining -= s.candidate.pool_share_needed`; and OpportunityCandidate.pool_share_needed at :90-92 is documented 'fraction of shared pool this trade would consume. e.g. $20 trade in a $200 pool = 0.1'. Units are genuinely mixed. The unresolved stream-of-consciousness block is exactly at :124-148 as claimed. Zero production callers re-confirmed: `rank_for_pool` appears only at capital_arbiter_bridge.py:22 (prose), :50 (def), :104 (its own error string); every other hit is under _archive/. Minor: the claim's quote elides 'This is a defensible / default when the caller hasn't pre-normalized.' from the middle of the comment — an elision, not a paraphrase; the code lines are exact.

### `src/trading/capital_registry.py:471` — CapitalRegistry drift metric recomputes free capital, so any under-reserved wallet fires a false capital.drift_alert
`correctness` · **low** · verdict PARTIAL · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The generator inside drift_usd is character-for-character the same sum already assigned to reserved_usd at lines 465-467. So drift_usd == free_usd identically, and drift_pct = 100 * |free_usd| / wallet_usd is just 'percent of the wallet not reserved'. Drift is supposed to measure registry-vs-exchange disagreement; instead it measures under-reservation, which is the normal healthy state.

**Operator sees:** The operator gets a 'capital drift' warning and a capital.drift_alert notification every reconcile cycle for any pool where bots reserve less than 95% of the wallet — i.e. essentially always. The alert is pure noise, which trains the operator to ignore the one signal that would matter if real drift ever occurred.

**Fix:** Define drift against something the registry does not already know. The meaningful quantity is exchange-reported balance vs the registry's own last-known wallet snapshot, or reserved_usd vs the sum of what bots believe they hold. If no such second source exists yet, remove drift_usd/drift_pct from the returned dict and delete the threshold branch in bot_container.reconcile_capital_registry rather than shipping an alert keyed on free capital.

> **Verifier correction:** The MATH BUG IS REAL and line-exact. capital_registry.py:465-467 assigns reserved_usd from `sum(r.reserved_usd for r in self._reservations.values() if r.exchange_id == exchange_id and r.base_currency == base)`; :469 is `free_usd = wallet_usd - reserved_usd`; :471-473 is `drift_usd = wallet_usd - sum(<character-identical generator>)`. So drift_usd == free_usd identically and drift_pct is just percent-unreserved. Cited line 471 is exactly the drift_usd line. Consumer verified at bot_container.py:1844-1855 (drift_pct read, `if abs(drift_pct) > drift_threshold_pct:` → emit capital.drift_alert + logger.warning), default threshold 5.0 at bot_container.py:1865.

THE CLAIMED EFFECT IS REFUTED. There is no reconcile cycle. I grepped the entire repo (minus build/dist): `reconcile_capital_registry` appears only at its def (bot_container.py:1795), inside its own log string (:1860), and one internal call from `reconcile_all_capital` (:1882). `reconcile_all_capital` appears only at its own def (:1864). Zero external callers — no timer, no background task, no GUI button. The only other hits are in _archive/ (a pre-2026-07-25 test) and _archive docs. Its own docstring at :1870-1872 says 'The 5-min cadence callers (Phase D background task / GUI manual-refresh) invoke this' — those callers were never built. So the operator gets NO drift alerts at all, ever, not 'an alert every cycle'.

### `src/trading/extractor_bot.py:1165` — manual_fire_position mutates the shared per-bot config across an await on the same event loop the tick runs on — a concurrent auto-exit can be silently forced to 100%
`threading` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live  `[UNVERIFIED PROOF]`

**Broken:** `self.config` is one object shared by every position this bot holds, and `extractor_exit_pct` is read inside `_execute_bullish_exit` (line 1210) and `_exit_is_profitable_in_base` (line 910) for whichever position is being processed. The mutation window spans `await self._execute_bullish_exit(...)`, which awaits `guarded_place_order` -> a network round-trip. The GUI dispatches this coroutine with `run_coroutine_threadsafe(coro, loop)` onto `BotManager._async_loop` — the same loop that runs `bot.tick()` — so the bot's own tick task is free to resume at any await point inside that window and process a *different* open position.

**Operator sees:** An operator with two or more artillery rounds in flight who clicks Manual Fire on one pair can have a second, unrelated position closed at 100% instead of the configured `extractor_exit_pct` (e.g. 60%), destroying the intended 'rider' tail. Because the activity log for the second close reports it as a normal EXTRACTOR EXIT, the operator has no signal that the exit percentage was overridden. A shutdown during the window (main.py:975/1134 call save_all_state -> asdict(self.config)) would persist extractor_exit_pct=100.0 to bot_state.json permanently.

**Fix:** Do not mutate shared config. Give `_execute_bullish_exit` an explicit `exit_pct: Optional[float] = None` parameter that defaults to `self.config.extractor_exit_pct`, and have `manual_fire_position` pass `exit_pct=100.0`. That removes the mutation window entirely and also removes the need for the try/except/finally restore dance at lines 1163-1180.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded medium -> low, and the taxonomy key is wrong. This is NOT 'threading' — it is single-threaded coroutine re-entrancy on one asyncio loop; there is no cross-thread data race and no lock would be the idiomatic fix (a per-call exit_pct parameter would be). More importantly, BotConfig.extractor_exit_pct DEFAULTS TO 100.0 (bot_container.py:407, mirrored as the default at main_window.py:6757, bot_container.py:2601, and bot_live_settings.py:2977), so in the shipped default configuration the override is a no-op and the window is harmless. The bug only bites an operator who has explicitly dialled exit_pct below 100 AND holds >=2 open positions AND has an auto-exit land inside the order round-trip. The persistence sub-claim (shutdown mid-window writing 100.0 to bot_state.json) I did NOT verify — I did not open main.py:975/1134 or trace save_all_state.

### `src/trading/poa_tournament.py:646` — PoA tournament replay determinism is broken by hash() on strings — replay_hash differs between processes
`correctness` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** Python randomises str hashing per interpreter process (PYTHONHASHSEED is random by default), so hash(p.participant_id) yields a different value on every run. Every participant's PnL walk is seeded from it, so the standings differ run-to-run for identical inputs. _compute_outcome then folds `"standings": standings` into hash_material (line 766) and sha256s it into replay_hash — the value the module docstring sells as the audit anchor ('Events are SEEDED PER TOURNAMENT ... reproducible across replays. R57 EPM discipline applied to tournament play: same seed -> same event sequence -> same outcome') and the comment at line 754 restates ('any two replays with the same seed + config produce the same hash'). _tick_npc at line 672 has the identical defect.

**Operator sees:** Two runs of the same tournament config produce different winners and different replay_hash values, so the persisted logs/tournaments/<id>.json audit trail cannot be used to reproduce or dispute an outcome — the one property the R58/R57 framing exists to provide.

**Fix:** Replace hash(p.participant_id) with a stable digest, e.g. int.from_bytes(hashlib.sha256(p.participant_id.encode()).digest()[:4], 'big') — the same construction _hash_seed already uses at line 823. Apply to both _tick_participant and _tick_npc. Add a test that runs the same build_duel twice in subprocesses with different PYTHONHASHSEED values and asserts equal replay_hash.

> **Verifier correction:** Line-exact and verbatim: :646-648 `rng = random.Random((seed * 1_000_003 + hash(p.participant_id) + tick) & 0xFFFFFFFF)`; the same defect at :672-673 in _tick_npc. I closed the gap the scanner left open — the claim admitted it never executed the cross-process test, so I ran it: three separate interpreters seeding `random.Random((42*1000003 + hash('participant-A') + 7) & 0xFFFFFFFF).gauss(0,1)` produced 0.11410723936841734, 0.15878704327260262, -0.031489255185959714. Non-reproducible across processes, measured, not inferred. The consequence chain also verified: p.stats.pnl accumulates from that rng at :653, standings are built from p.stats.pnl at :749-751, standings go into hash_material at :766, and replay_hash is sha256 of that at :768-770. Determinism claims verified at :29-31 ('same seed → same event sequence → same outcome') and :753-756 ('any two replays with the same seed + config produce the same hash').

### `src/trading/scrumming_bot.py:6798` — GateContext.delta_pct is fed a raw USD amount instead of a percentage
`correctness` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `GateContext.delta_pct` is documented in gate_chain.py:78 as `delta_pct: float  # |delta| / target_balance x 100`. Both construction sites pass `abs(float(delta))` — the raw USD magnitude — while the correctly-computed local variable `delta_pct = abs(delta) / (self._target_balance + 1e-9) * 100` already exists at line 5661 and is used everywhere else in the tick (5754, 6174, 6296, 6377). Both the SCRUM context (6798) and the FOLD context (7578) have the same defect.

**Operator sees:** Latent today because no gate consumes the field, so nothing misbehaves right now. It is a live trap: the next gate added to gate_chain.py that reads `ctx.delta_pct` against `ctx.scrumming_interval_pct` (both are in the context, and comparing them is the obvious use) will compare dollars against percent — on a $200-target bot a $6 delta would read as '6 >= 3%' and pass a gate it should fail, or on a $10,000-target bot a $6 delta would read as 6 and also pass. The GateChain framework's whole value proposition is that the context is a faithful snapshot; this field is not.

**Fix:** Pass the already-computed local: `delta_pct=float(delta_pct)` at both 6798 and 7578. If the intent was to expose the USD magnitude too, add a separate `delta_abs_usd` field to GateContext rather than overloading a field whose documented contract is a percentage.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded medium -> low. The claim concedes it is latent and my grep confirms it: nothing reads ctx.delta_pct today, so there is no current misbehavior. It is a correct latent-trap finding, not a present defect.

### `src/trading/triangular_swarm.py:325` — TriadSpawner.rank_triads raises KeyError 'arm_scores' whenever a triad's shortest series is under 20 points
`correctness` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** score_triad's insufficient-data early return (line 203) is `return {"score": 0.0, "loop_deviation": 0.0, "exploitable": False}` — it omits the "arm_scores" key that the happy path returns at line 227. rank_triads dereferences result["arm_scores"] unconditionally, so any triad whose shortest of the three series has fewer than 20 points crashes the whole ranking pass. The `if not pa or not pb or not pc: continue` guard at line 316 only rejects empty lists, not short ones. The synthetic-cross fallback above it is also inverted: line 310 tests `if len(pa) < 20 or len(pb) < 20` — i.e. it synthesises the cross series only when the two ARMS are too short, never when the cross pair pc is the one missing data, which is the case the comment on line 311 describes.

**Operator sees:** spawn_optimal_triads (line 340) and therefore run_surrounding_swarm's periodic rebalance (line 655) abort with an unhandled KeyError as soon as one cross pair has a short history — which is the normal case for a newly listed quote currency. Currently latent only because triangular_swarm.py has zero importers.

**Fix:** Give score_triad's early return the same key set as the success path (`"arm_scores": {"a": 0.0, "b": 0.0, "c": 0.0}`), or have rank_triads use result.get("arm_scores", {}). Separately fix the inverted fallback at line 310 to trigger on `len(pc) < 20` (the cross pair) rather than on the arm lengths. Note this module has no importers in src/, tools/, main.py or tests/ — consider deleting all 1,090 lines instead.

> **Verifier correction:** Reproduced independently in a fresh interpreter. score_triad([1.0]*25, [1.0]*25, [1.0]*10) returns {'score': 0.0, 'loop_deviation': 0.0, 'exploitable': False} — no 'arm_scores' key. TriadSpawner(base='BTC', exchange='COINBASE').rank_triads({'BTC/USD': 60 pts, 'BTC/USDC': 60 pts, 'USD/USDC': 10 pts}) raises KeyError: 'arm_scores'. Lines verified: :202-203 the n<20 early return, :225-231 the happy-path dict with 'arm_scores' at :227, :305-317 the loop with the `if len(pa) < 20 or len(pb) < 20` synthetic-cross test at :310 and the emptiness-only guard at :316, :319 `result = score_triad(pa, pb, pc)` and :325 `"arm_scores": result["arm_scores"],` — the cited line, exact. The inverted synthetic-cross logic is real: the guard keys on the two arms' length while the comment at :311 describes the cross pair being the one without data.

### `src/trading/volume_guard.py:228` — MEM-259 VolumeGuard disable is enforced at only one call site — execute() still gates on config.enabled
`correctness` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The MEM-259 force-disable is implemented as a property override (lines 183-190) that always returns False, with the setter at 192-196 writing to self.config.enabled where it 'does not affect the disabled property'. But execute() reads self.config.enabled, which main.py:639 leaves at its dataclass default of True. The disable therefore only holds because BotContainer.guarded_place_order happens to test the property (`if self._volume_guard and self._volume_guard.enabled:` at bot_container.py:1027). Any direct call to guard.execute(...) runs the full profile/chunk/iceberg path the directive shut off. get_status() at line 674 has the same split — it reports `"enabled": self.config.enabled` (True) for a guard that is force-disabled.

**Operator sees:** The operator's 'Disable the fucking thing' directive is one bypass away from being undone: a new call site that reaches guard.execute() directly re-enables iceberg chunking with no code change and no warning. Any diagnostic that reads get_status() is told the guard is enabled while it is not.

**Fix:** Make the disable structural rather than call-site-dependent: set `enabled: bool = False` on VolumeGuardConfig and have execute() short-circuit on the `enabled` property (not config.enabled), and have get_status() report the property. Delete get_volume_guard / get_status / get_market_profile / get_execution_history if the guard stays off — they have no callers.

> **Verifier correction:** Every element re-verified line-exact. volume_guard.py:182-190 is the @property `enabled` returning False with the MEM-259 comment; :192-196 is the setter writing `self.config.enabled = val` with the 'does not affect the disabled property' note; :228 is `if not self.config.enabled:` under the `# Pass-through if disabled` comment at :227 — quote verbatim. :62 `enabled: bool = True` is the dataclass default. :674 `"enabled": self.config.enabled,` in get_status. main.py:638-640 confirmed: `volume_guard = VolumeGuard(config=VolumeGuardConfig())` then `bot_manager.set_volume_guard(volume_guard)`. bot_container.py:1027 `if self._volume_guard and self._volume_guard.enabled:` is the sole property consumer (the only other _volume_guard sites are 871, 1030 inside that branch, 1425, 1571-1575, 2018-2019 — all plumbing). get_volume_guard at :737 has zero callers; VolumeGuard.get_status/get_market_profile/get_execution_history have zero external callers (all `.get_status()` hits in src belong to bots or RiskManager).

### `tools/harness/claim_ledger.py:80` — Claim ledger silently drops malformed lines, then permanently deletes them on the next write
`data-integrity` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED  `[UNVERIFIED PROOF]`

**Broken:** Two compounding problems. (1) A line that fails to parse is skipped with no counter, no warning, no stderr note — so a claim in `open` state simply vanishes from `list_open()`, and `check_no_open()` prints `[OK] no open claims` while an unresolved claim sits in the file. (2) `_write_all` (lines 86-90) opens the ledger with mode "w" and rewrites it from the in-memory list — so the very next `log`/`verify`/`refute` command permanently erases every line `_load_all` could not parse. Separately, `Claim(**d)` on line 82 is unguarded: a line with an extra key raises TypeError, which `check_release_readiness._run_claim_ledger_check` does not catch (it only catches ImportError at line 123), so the whole release gate dies with a raw traceback.

**Operator sees:** The release gate's step 3 — the mechanism that structurally enforces 'show don't tell' — reports clean while an open, unverified claim exists. The evidence trail is then destroyed on the next ledger write, so the loss is unrecoverable and leaves no trace of having happened.

**Fix:** Count and report unparseable lines: accumulate them and have `_load_all` raise or print `[WARN] N unparseable ledger line(s)`; preserve them verbatim in `_write_all` (write a `_raw` passthrough list) instead of dropping them. Wrap `Claim(**d)` in a try/except TypeError, and broaden the `except ImportError` in check_release_readiness.py:123 to `except Exception` so a corrupt ledger fails the gate cleanly rather than crashing it.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded medium -> low on one decisive ground the finding never checked: docs/audits/CLAIMS.jsonl DOES NOT EXIST (`ls docs/audits/CLAIMS.jsonl` -> No such file or directory). _load_all returns [] at line 71-72 before any parsing happens, list_open() returns [], and step 3 of the release gate currently passes vacuously for an entirely different reason than the one claimed. Every scenario in the finding — the dropped open claim, the destructive rewrite, the unguarded Claim(**d) TypeError — requires a ledger file that has never been created. The code defects are real; the described harm is presently unreachable.
