## P1 — SHARED WITH LIVE (gated cascade)  (7)

### `src/exchange/ccxt_connector.py:876` — CcxtConnector.get_ohlcv passes `limit` into ccxt's `since` slot — Coinbase is asked for candles from 1970 and returns nothing
`correctness` · **high** · verdict PARTIAL · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `_call_sync(self, fn, *args, **kwargs)` (ccxt_connector.py:489) forwards positionally, so this becomes `ccxt_exchange.fetch_ohlcv(symbol, timeframe, limit)`. ccxt's signature is `fetch_ohlcv(self, symbol, timeframe='1m', since=None, limit=None, params={})` — the third positional is `since`, not `limit`. So `limit=100` lands in `since` as 100 MILLISECONDS since epoch, and ccxt's own `limit` defaults to None. Inside ccxt.coinbase.fetch_ohlcv that produces `request['start'] = str(int(100/1000)) = '0'` and, with no `until`, `request['end'] = '0' + 300*granularity`. Coinbase is therefore asked for the 300 candles beginning 1970-01-01T00:00:00Z.

**Operator sees:** Every OHLCV fetch against Coinbase returns an empty list. The API log line at ccxt_connector.py:882 writes "No data" for every FETCH_OHLCV entry. The 7-indicator TA engine gets zero candles for BB / Vortex / MACD / StochRSI / Ichimoku / Volume / Slingshot voting on the live path.

**Fix:** Change ccxt_connector.py:876 to pass limit as a keyword so it cannot slide into the `since` slot: `data = await self._call_sync(self._ex.fetch_ohlcv, symbol, timeframe, None, limit)` or better `self._call_sync(functools.partial(self._ex.fetch_ohlcv, symbol, timeframe, since=None, limit=limit))`. Add a regression test asserting the ccxt stub receives `since is None` and `limit == 100`.

> **Verifier correction:** The MECHANISM is confirmed but the CLAIMED EFFECT IS FALSE. The call does NOT return an empty list and the TA engine does NOT get zero candles. I ran the real call against Coinbase: `ccxt.coinbase().fetch_ohlcv('BTC/USD','5m',100)` returns 300 candles, not 0. Coinbase IGNORES the epoch-0 start/end window and returns its most recent 350 candles; ccxt then sorts ascending and filter_by_since_limit(limit=300) keeps the FIRST 300 — silently discarding the 50 NEWEST bars. The real defect is a stale read head, not a dead one.

### `src/trading/extractor_bot.py:1377` — Inverted Extractor sizes its artillery SELL by dividing base-denominated ammo by the pair price — order is 1/price times too large
`correctness` · **high** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The `amount` argument of an order on symbol BASE/QUOTE is denominated in the pair's BASE asset. For a NORMAL Extractor the pair is ALT/base (`_pair_filter_matches` keeps `m_quote == base_currency`), so `artillery_base` is in QUOTE units and dividing by price correctly converts to ALT units. For an INVERTED Extractor `_pair_filter_matches` keeps `m_base == base_currency` (extractor_bot.py:737-739), so the pair is BASE/QUOTE where BASE *is* the ammo asset, and `artillery_base` (from `_usd_to_base`, which divides USD by `_chunk_to_base_rate` = USD-per-base-unit) is ALREADY in the pair's base units. Dividing it by `alt_price_in_base` again produces base_units^2/quote — a number inflated by 1/price. The identical bug is at line 1016 in `_maybe_fire_correction` (`alt_units_to_buy = add_amount_base / alt_price_in_base`, side = `_entry_order_side()` = SELL for inverted). Worse, the internal ledger then records `actual_base_spent = filled_units * fill_price` (line 1405), which multiplies the error back out — so the bot's `_chunk_free_base` shows a correct-looking small debit while the wallet actually lost 1/price times that quantity.

**Operator sees:** An operator who creates an Inverted Extractor on a standing LINK/SOL position sees the bot dump orders massively larger than the configured artillery size — for low-priced quote pairs the very first artillery round attempts to sell the operator's entire standing position (or the whole wallet balance of that asset). The bot's own Pool/Chunk readouts continue to show a small, correct-looking debit, so the discrepancy is invisible in the GUI until the operator checks the exchange.

**Fix:** In `_fire_artillery` and `_maybe_fire_correction`, branch the amount computation on `self._is_inverted`: for inverted, the order amount IS the base-denominated ammo (`amount = artillery_base` / `add_amount_base`) because the ammo asset is the pair's base side; keep the divide-by-price form only for the normal direction. Correspondingly, `actual_base_spent` for inverted is `filled_units` (base units sold), not `filled_units * fill_price`. Add a regression test that asserts the `amount` kwarg passed to `guarded_place_order` for an inverted bot equals `artillery_base` within tolerance.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded critical -> high. The dimensional analysis holds exactly as claimed, but the most common real-world outcome is an exchange-side rejection (insufficient balance), not a silent oversell: with the claimed 5 LINK standing / 0.25 LINK intended / 1250 LINK requested example the venue simply refuses. Actual overselling requires the operator's standing position to be large relative to artillery_size_usd (e.g. 5000 LINK standing, 1250 LINK request executes). Also note the second site is line 1016, which I confirmed verbatim ('alt_units_to_buy = add_amount_base / alt_price_in_base', side=_entry_order_side()).

### `src/trading/scrumming_bot.py:5445` — tick() calls a method that does not exist — initial entry raises AttributeError
`broken-ref` · **high** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `_sum_sibling_base_currency_claims` is not defined on ScrummingBot nor on its base class BotContainer. The real helper is `BotManager.sum_sibling_base_currency_claims` (bot_container.py:2218) — a different class that ScrummingBot does NOT inherit from (ScrummingBot(BotContainer); BotContainer spans bot_container.py:836-1411, BotManager starts at 1412). The wrapper method that used to bridge them was deleted; only an archived test (_archive/tests_pre_2026_07_25/test_scrumming_base_currency_claim_isolation.py) still references it. The call is NOT inside a try/except, so the AttributeError propagates out of `_execute`... out of tick() entirely.

**Operator sees:** Any Scrumming bot whose position value is below 1% of target (fresh bot, or one emptied by SELF-DESTRUCT keep_running=True / manual liquidation) throws AttributeError on every action tick once Max Cartridge cannot preempt it. Reachable whenever `max_cartridge_size_pct = 0` (an explicitly documented and GUI-settable disable — see the comment at line 5023 'Set max_cartridge_size_pct = 0 to disable', and the spin box at bot_live_settings.py:2539), or when the cartridge is hysteresis-blocked at line 5189 (that branch deliberately does NOT return — comment at 5210 'Do NOT return'). The bot never buys in; the operator sees repeated tick errors and a bot that will not enter a position.

**Fix:** Re-add the wrapper on ScrummingBot, delegating to the manager back-reference already stored in `self._bot_manager` (set by set_bot_manager, scrumming_bot.py:1075), returning 0.0 when `_bot_manager` is None: `def _sum_sibling_base_currency_claims(self) -> float: mgr = getattr(self,'_bot_manager',None); if mgr is None or not hasattr(mgr,'sum_sibling_base_currency_claims'): return 0.0; try: return float(mgr.sum_sibling_base_currency_claims(self.bot_id, self.config.exchange_id, self.config.base_currency) or 0.0) except Exception: return 0.0`. Add a unit test that constructs a bot with no manager and drives the initial-entry branch to completion.

> **Verifier correction:** Reachability is narrower than stated. With the default max_cartridge_size_pct=10.0, a fresh bot (holdings 0, |delta| = full target >> 10% threshold) fires the CARTRIDGE branch and returns at 5278 BEFORE ever reaching 5445, so the default-config fresh-bot case is preempted. The claim's own caveat (cartridge disabled, or hysteresis-blocked at 5189 which deliberately falls through) is the real trigger set. Also: the AttributeError does not escape the process — bot_container.py:1162-1200 _run_with_guard catches it, increments stats.consecutive_errors, and cooldowns after MAX_CONSECUTIVE_ERRORS. Symptom is a bot stuck in an error/cooldown loop, not a crash.

### `src/trading/scrumming_bot.py:9563` — Stack Mode is fully dead — _open_stack_from_scrum reads a nonexistent attribute
`dead-feature` · **high** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `self.exchange_interface` does not exist anywhere in the codebase — BotContainer stores the connector as `self.exchange` (bot_container.py:862). `getattr(self.exchange_interface, ...)` evaluates the attribute access FIRST, so the getattr default never helps: it raises AttributeError. The line sits ABOVE the try block at 9565, so nothing catches it inside the method, and neither `_execute_sell` (9801) nor the SCRUM call site (6964) wraps the call — it unwinds all the way out of tick(). Secondarily, even if the attribute were `self.exchange`, ExchangeInterface has no `min_order_size` member (the real field is `AssetInfo.min_amount`, exchange/base.py:140) so the stack would always be built with min_order_size=0.

**Operator sees:** Stack Mode is presented as a working feature — a checkbox in the New Bot wizard (bot_wizard.py:623, persisted at :1492), a checkbox in Live Settings (bot_live_settings.py:2037), a whole 'Stack Tranches' tab (bot_live_settings.py:1447), and a config field (bot_container.py:249). Turning it on makes the very first autonomous SCRUM raise AttributeError out of tick(); the bot logs a tick error and never sells. No stack tranche is ever created on any code path.

**Fix:** Replace with the real limits source already used elsewhere in this file: `_min_amt, _min_cost, _ = await self._get_market_limits(self.config.symbol)` (BotContainer._get_market_limits, bot_container.py:899 — already awaited at scrumming_bot.py:6911 and 7876) and pass `_min_amt` as min_order_size. Then fix the companion bug in finding 'Stack Mode success returns the failure sentinel'. Add a test that enables stack_mode and drives one SCRUM end-to-end.

> **Verifier correction:** The claim's grep scope omitted tests/. Two test files DO define `exchange_interface` — tests/test_stack_mode_execution.py:157 and tests/test_stack_mode_visible.py:235 — but both set it on a local `_StubBot` whose own docstring says 'Minimum surface _open_stack_from_scrum needs. NOT a ScrummingBot', then invoke the unbound method. That is why 875 green tests never caught this: the suite fabricates the attribute the production class lacks. It strengthens rather than weakens the finding, but the claimed proof ('exactly one hit in the whole tree') is inaccurate as written. Severity downgraded from critical: stack_mode defaults False (bot_container.py:249), so only an operator who opts in is affected.

### `src/trading/scrumming_bot.py:6878` — Autonomous SCRUM sizes the sell in USD/quote-price, omitting the quote-to-USD divisor
`correctness` · **high** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `delta` is USD: it comes from `current_value - self._target_balance` where `current_value = self._current_holdings * ticker.last * float(self._quote_to_usd or 1.0)` (line 4847-4849). `ticker.last` is in QUOTE units. To convert a USD delta into BASE units the divisor must be `ticker.last * _quote_to_usd`, not `ticker.last`. Every other sizing site in this file does it correctly: `_execute_manual_rebalance` line 8809-8810 (`_denom_sc = price * _qrate; sell_amount = delta_usd / _denom_sc`), `_execute_detonation` line 9373 (`excess_usd / (price * _qrate)`), and `_execute_buy` lines 10570-10571 (`amount = amount / _qrate_buy`). Only the autonomous SCRUM path was never converted — the v3.15.55 quote-to-USD sweep missed it.

**Operator sees:** Concrete case — SOL/BTC bot, _quote_to_usd = 100000 (BTC/USD), holdings 2 SOL, ticker.last 0.0015 BTC/SOL, target $250. current_value = $300, delta = $50. Correct sell = 50/(0.0015*100000) = 0.333 SOL. Code computes 50/0.0015 = 33,333 SOL — 100,000x oversized. The CRR gate in _execute_sell (line 9905, `amount > _crr_effective`) refuses it, so every autonomous SCRUM on a crypto-quoted pair logs 'SELL REFUSED (capital reservation)' and never fires. If the CRR pre-check itself raises (swallowed at 9926) the order reaches the exchange and is rejected, and on the success path `self._current_holdings -= amount` (line 10050) has no floor and would drive holdings deeply negative. Crypto-quoted pairs (BTC/ETH, SOL/BTC) are the exact case the operator's 2026-04-25 directive built _quote_to_usd for.

**Fix:** `_denom = float(ticker.last) * float(self._quote_to_usd or 1.0); scrum_asset = (abs(delta) / _denom) if _denom > 0 else 0.0` — mirroring line 8809-8810 exactly. Add a regression test with _quote_to_usd != 1.0 asserting the computed sell amount re-zeros the USD delta.

> **Verifier correction:** No correction to the defect. Scope note: manifests only when _quote_to_usd != 1.0, i.e. crypto-quoted pairs (BTC/ETH, SOL/BTC). On USD-quoted pairs (_quote_to_usd = 1.0) the line is correct and this is inert.

### `src/trading/stone_tablets/fetcher.py:130` — CoinbaseAdapter.fetch_chunk passes a `since=` kwarg that no Acervator connector accepts — every Stone Tablet fetch raises TypeError
`broken-ref` · **high** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The comment above it (lines 127-129) claims "ccxt get_ohlcv accepts (symbol, timeframe, since, limit)". Acervator's connector does NOT. `ExchangeInterface.get_ohlcv` is declared `(self, symbol: str, timeframe: str = "1h", limit: int = 100)` at src/exchange/base.py:192-194, and the only concrete live implementation `CcxtConnector.get_ohlcv` has the identical signature at src/exchange/ccxt_connector.py:869-871. Passing `since=` therefore raises `TypeError: get_ohlcv() got an unexpected keyword argument 'since'` on the FIRST call, before any network I/O. The `except Exception` at line 153 swallows it, sleeps retry_base_s * 2**attempt (2+4+8+16 = 30s), and after 4 attempts returns FetchAttempt(candles=[], error=...). Secondary defect in the same expression: `since_ms / 1000.0` converts ms to float SECONDS, so even after adding the parameter the value would be wrong by 1000x (ccxt's `since` is milliseconds).

**Operator sees:** The Stone Tablets archive can never be extended or refreshed from inside the application. Every gap-fill attempt burns 30 seconds of exponential-backoff sleep per chunk and appends 0 candles. On the operator's disk the 406 tablets all end 2026-08-01 while today is 2026-08-04; `main.py:492` already reports all 406 assets as stale at boot, and there is no working path to un-stale them.

**Fix:** Add a `since_ms: Optional[int] = None` parameter to `ExchangeInterface.get_ohlcv` (src/exchange/base.py:192) and `CcxtConnector.get_ohlcv` (src/exchange/ccxt_connector.py:869), forwarding it to ccxt as INTEGER MILLISECONDS. Then change fetcher.py:130-133 to `since_ms=int(since_ms)` (drop the /1000.0). Add a test that calls `CoinbaseAdapter.fetch_chunk` against a stub whose signature matches `ExchangeInterface.get_ohlcv` exactly, so signature drift fails the suite instead of hiding behind `_FakeAdapter`.

> **Verifier correction:** Class name in the claim is wrong: the concrete connector is `CCXTConnector` (ccxt_connector.py:213), not `CcxtConnector`. Everything else holds.

### `src/trading/ta_engine.py:289` — ADX is computed on an accumulation (sum) scale ~14x the textbook value, so the indicator's own 20/35/50 thresholds never fire and its confidence is permanently pinned at 1.0
`correctness` · **high** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `_wilder_smooth` (line 231-240) seeds with `first = sum(values[:period])` and recurses `result[-1] - result[-1]/period + v` — the SUM form. That is correct for DM+/DM-/TR (the scale cancels in the DI+ = 100*sDM/sTR ratio) but wrong for the DX series: textbook ADX is the AVERAGE of DX, i.e. `(prev*(p-1) + cur)/p`. The result is `adx ~= period * true_ADX` (~14x with the default period=14). The four regime flags and the confidence formula on lines 294-320 were written against the textbook 0-100 scale and were never recalibrated. `ranging` and `developing` are therefore unreachable in practice, `parabolic` is always True, and `confidence` saturates to 1.0 whenever DI+ != DI-, making ADX the loudest permanently-maxed voter in VotingEngine (weight 1.0 x confidence 1.0 on every tick).

**Operator sees:** The dashboard ADX cell can never display the 'Rng NN' ranging state (the accumulation-ideal regime the operator most wants to see), and the ADX voter contributes a maximum-strength directional vote on essentially every tick, skewing every VotingEngine consensus and every multi-timeframe aggregate. The indicator_panel tooltip (src/gui/indicator_panel.py:528-541) tells the operator the cell shows a value "0-100" and that "ADX <20: ranging" — the actual displayed number is in the 62-681 range.

**Fix:** Either (a) add a dedicated averaging smoother for the DX series (`adx = wilder_avg(dx_series, period)`) so ADX returns to the 0-100 scale — then per gate_chain.py:530-533 the `ADXTrendSuppressionGate` default MUST be reset from 500.0 to 30 and the indicator_panel tooltip is already correct; or (b) if the scale is intentionally kept, divide by `self.period` before the threshold comparisons in `compute()` and fix the indicator_panel tooltip. Option (a) is preferable — it is the only version that makes the tooltip, the docstring at lines 209-217, and the gate agree.

> **Verifier correction:** None material. Minor: the claim's specific numbers (adx=253.66) are from the scanner's own synthetic series and I could not reproduce those exact values; I reproduced the same phenomenon on an independent series. Also worth adding to the finding: the indicator_panel tooltip is stale in a SECOND way the claim did not mention — src/gui/indicator_panel.py:539-541 tells the operator 'the separate ADXTrendSuppressionGate uses ADX<30 -> blocks scrum', but gate_chain's actual default threshold is 500.0 (gate_chain.py:505).


---

## P2 — DEAD WEIGHT  (59)

### `src/core/version_sweep.py:419` — Two version_sweep checks silently no-op because their target files were deleted, and print a pass tick
`dead-feature` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** Neither path exists. `check_r6_two_paths` (CHECK 7) and `check_snapshot_consistency` (CHECK 11, same guard at line 518 on `src/gui/simulator.py`) both return before doing any work. The `run()` loop at lines 697-712 counts findings before/after and prints '✓' when the delta is zero, so a check that did literally nothing is indistinguishable from a check that passed. CHECK 11's own docstring (lines 513-515) says it exists to catch a bug class that is 'invisible at runtime and undetectable by syntax checking' — that safety net is gone.

**Operator sees:** The sweep console prints 'Checking: R6 two-path (R6)... ✓' and 'Checking: Snapshot key sync... ✓' on every run. The operator reads that as 'the R6 two-path invariant and the ta[] snapshot-key contract were verified'. Neither was examined at all.

**Fix:** Re-point both checks at the live equivalents (src/gui/simulator_tab/ and src/trading/scrumming_bot.py's ta snapshot builder), or delete them. If a check cannot resolve its inputs it must emit a Severity.MEDIUM 'check could not run — target missing' finding rather than returning silently, so 'skipped' never renders as '✓'.

> **Verifier correction:** Correct as stated. The quoted block spans lines 416-420; the cited line 419 is the `if not sim_path.exists() or not bat_path.exists():` guard, which is the right anchor. CHECK 11's guard is at line 517-519 (`sim_path = ...` at 517, `if not sim_path.exists(): return` at 518) — the claim's line 518 is exact. I verified both targets are gone (`test -e src/gui/simulator.py` -> MISSING, `test -e sadp/RAIntSimBat/RAIntSimBat.py` -> MISSING) and then RAN both checks in-process: `check_r6_two_paths` produced 0 findings and `check_snapshot_consistency` produced 0 findings. The run() loop at 697-712 is verbatim as described (`before = len(self.result.findings)` / `fn()` / `if new_count == 0: print("✓")`), so both print a pass tick having examined nothing. CHECK 11's docstring at 508-515 does say the bug class is 'invisible at runtime and undetectable by syntax checking'.

### `src/exchange/circuit_breaker.py:313` — `call_with_breaker` has zero callers — every exchange READ path is unprotected while `_with_retry` retries 3x into an outage
`dead-feature` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** This is the module's only generic integration surface, and nothing in the live tree calls it. The breaker is wired at exactly ONE site — the hand-rolled inline block in `CCXTConnector.place_order` (ccxt_connector.py:976-983 and 1063-1066). Every other exchange call — `get_ticker`, `get_ohlcv`, `get_balances`, `get_balance`, `get_orderbook`, `get_open_orders`, `get_order`, `cancel_order`, `get_my_trades`, `get_all_tickers` — has no breaker. Those are precisely the paths that storm: they are decorated with `@_with_retry()` (ccxt_connector.py:181-207), which retries 3 times with 1 s + 2 s exponential backoff on every timeout/network/rate-limit error. During an exchange outage each read multiplies 3x with zero breaker to short-circuit it. The module docstring at circuit_breaker.py:32-37 states the whole purpose is "prevents retry storms from depleting the operator's API rate-limit quota during exchange outages" and cites "8 bots running 1Hz tick rate = 240 wasted calls per outage, plus IP-ban risk" — none of that protection exists on the read paths.

**Operator sees:** During a Coinbase outage or rate-limit event, ticker/OHLCV/balance polling from every bot keeps hammering the API at 3x amplification with no fail-fast, burning the operator's quota and risking a venue-side IP ban. The operator sees the app appear to hang on refreshes rather than getting the fast "circuit breaker for coinbase:BONK/USD is OPEN" message that the order path produces.

**Fix:** Either route the read methods through `call_with_breaker(f"{self._exchange_id}:{symbol}", self._call_sync, fn, ...)`, or delete `call_with_breaker` and factor `place_order`'s inline block into a small `_with_breaker` decorator applied to the read methods too. Order the decorators so the breaker check happens OUTSIDE `_with_retry`, otherwise the retry loop still fires 3 times before the breaker sees anything.

> **Verifier correction:** Zero-caller claim independently reproduced. `call_with_breaker` at circuit_breaker.py:313 is verbatim as quoted. I grepped the entire repo (all extensions, not just .py): the only hits are the definition and _archive/tests_pre_2026_07_25/test_circuit_breaker.py (lines 13, 148, 163, 171, 176, 184, 190). `ls tests/` shows 56 .py files, none named test_circuit_breaker.py. src/exchange/__init__.py is 1 line, so there is no re-export making it a package-level public API. The only live breaker integration is the hand-rolled block at ccxt_connector.py:976-983 (get_breaker_registry / is_call_allowed / record_short_circuit / CircuitBreakerOpenError) plus record_failure at 1063 and record_success at 1066, all inside place_order. I read ccxt_connector.py:808-1152 and confirmed no other method touches a breaker. The @_with_retry() decoration of the read methods is confirmed at 812, 856, 868, 889, 917, 1080, 1087, 1094, 1107 (place_order at 959 is correctly NOT decorated). One framing correction: this is a dead HELPER, not a 'wired into the GUI but non-functional' feature — nothing in the GUI ever promised the operator read-path breaker protection, so the operator sees no broken surface. The 3x-amplification consequence is a design gap the scanner inferred, not an observed failure.

### `src/gui/bot_wizard.py:1834` — Bot Wizard's ProfitFoldingPage is unreachable and its get_config() is never consumed — profit_folding_active is never emitted at bot creation
`dead-feature` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `ModeSelectionPage.is_grid()` is hard-coded `return False  # Grid Bot deprecated` (bot_wizard.py:295). PAGE_FOLDING is therefore the target of exactly one unreachable branch. Worse, `BotCreationWizard.get_bot_config()` (lines 1842-1868) never calls `self._folding_page.get_config()` at all — so even if the page were reachable, its six output keys (`profit_folding_active`, `fold_mode`, `fold_target`, `fold_target_count`, `distribute_target`, `distribute_target_count`) would be discarded. Meanwhile `TradingParamsPage.get_config()`'s own docstring at line 1446-1448 asserts the opposite: "profit_folding_active is set by the dedicated ProfitFoldingPage (page 4), not this page."

**Operator sees:** Every bot created through the wizard silently gets `profit_folding_active=True` (the schema default), with no operator control anywhere in the creation flow — the compounding drain that grows the effective target balance is switched ON by default and unmentioned. The operator only discovers the setting later in Bot Details, whose own comment (bot_live_settings.py:2336-2338) tells them it "matches the dedicated Profit Folding page at bot creation" — a page they were never shown.

**Fix:** Pick one: (a) delete `ProfitFoldingPage`, the `PAGE_FOLDING` constant, lines 1772/1778/1833-1834/1836, and correct the docstring at 1446-1448; or (b) if operator-set profit folding at creation is wanted, add a `profit_folding_active` checkbox to `TradingParamsPage` § 2 (mirroring bot_live_settings.py:2329-2342) and emit it from `TradingParamsPage.get_config()`.

> **Verifier correction:** Effect is softer than claimed. profit_folding_active=True is the schema default (bot_container.py:98) and IS operator-controllable post-creation in Bot Details (bot_live_settings.py:2330-2342). The wizard also self-documents the dead grid branch at bot_wizard.py:1850-1852 ('grid branch removed ... is_grid() has returned False permanently since then — the elif branch was provably dead code'), so this is a known-and-partially-annotated leftover, not an undiscovered trap. TradingParamsPage.get_config()'s docstring at 1446-1448 is genuinely stale.

### `src/gui/main_window.py:665` — CapitalRegistryPanel is never instantiated — MEM-419 Phase D reservation table is inert, and its docstring asserts a QTimer that does not exist
`dead-feature` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The class defines a 9-column operator-facing table (Bot ID / Exchange / Base / Reserved USD / Reserved Base / Mode / Last Rate / Initial USD / Profit Delta) and an update_from_registry() feeder, but nothing in the live tree ever constructs it, adds it to a layout/tab, or calls its methods. The docstring's claim that 'The MainWindow wires a QTimer at 300000ms intervals' is false — there is no 300000ms timer anywhere in src/. The data source it needs is live and available (BotManager.capital_registry property exists at src/trading/bot_container.py:1791), so this is purely missing GUI wiring, not a missing backend.

**Operator sees:** The operator has no way to see per-bot capital reservations. There is no Capital Registry tab, panel, or menu entry anywhere in the GUI, so the Profit Delta / initial-vs-grown reservation view promised by MEM-419 Phase D simply does not exist. Anyone reading the docstring will hunt for a 5-minute refresh timer that was never written.

**Fix:** Either (a) wire it: instantiate in _setup_ui, add it as a tab or a section of the Trading tab, and add `QTimer(self)` at 300000ms calling `panel.update_from_registry(self._bot_manager.capital_registry)`; or (b) delete the class and its docstring. Do not leave the docstring asserting wiring that does not exist.

> **Verifier correction:** Line 665 is exactly `class CapitalRegistryPanel(QTableWidget):`. The quoted docstring fragment is verbatim at lines 679-683. Re-ran the greps myself: `grep -rn "CapitalRegistryPanel" --include=*.py .` returns main_window.py:665 plus only _archive/tests_pre_2026_07_25/test_capital_reconciliation_v3_20_73.py and _archive/docs_general/tools/build_manual_v4_part3.py — zero live constructions. `grep -rn "300000|300_000" src/ tools/ main.py` returns main_window.py:680 (the docstring itself), fleet_replay_controller.py:626 `step_ms: int = 300_000`, fleet_replay_panel.py:855, and gate_coverage.py:414 — all unrelated candle-step constants, no QTimer. `update_from_registry` (defined at line 706) has no live caller. Backend confirmed present: bot_container.py:1790-1793 defines the `capital_registry` property with the docstring 'Expose the broker for tests + Phase D's GUI registry table.' One structural detail the claim did not mention but which I checked because it would have invalidated the archived test: the class is indented 4 spaces because the entire module body sits under `if _HAS_QT:` at line 120 — it is still a module-level name, so `from src.gui.main_window import CapitalRegistryPanel` resolves. The finding stands.

### `src/gui/native_chart.py:1875` — Asset-chart timeframe dropdown is a hardcoded list that offers 4h/1w (unsupported on Coinbase) and omits 30m/2h/6h (supported) — bypasses available_timeframes()
`duplication` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** src/exchange/timeframes.py was built in v3.15.61 for exactly this, under an explicit operator directive quoted in its own header: "TF choices for given exchanges should change based on availability. For example, 4h should be disabled for Coinbase." Coinbase's allow-list there is `{"1m","5m","15m","30m","1h","2h","6h","1d"}`. Three other TF selectors call `available_timeframes()` correctly (bot_live_settings.py:2169, bot_wizard.py:1389, bot_wizard.py:1689). ChartPanel does not — it hardcodes a seventh, drifted list, and `ChartPanel(symbol)` is constructed with no exchange context at all (main_window.py:1013, 1238), so it has nothing to filter against. The intersection error runs both ways: 4h and 1w are offered but unavailable, while 30m, 2h and 6h are available but unreachable.

**Operator sees:** On the operator's Coinbase bots, selecting 4h or 1w on an Asset Chart requests a granularity the exchange does not serve; the chart either falls through to a secondary data source with different candles or lands on the "FAILED: ..." status text, with no explanation that the timeframe is unsupported. Meanwhile 30m, 2h and 6h — granularities Coinbase does serve, and that the bot's own TA Timeframe selector offers — cannot be charted at all.

**Fix:** Give ChartPanel an `exchange_id` (both construction sites in main_window.py already know it) and populate `_tf_combo` from `available_timeframes(exchange_id)` instead of the class constant, matching `TradingParamsPage.set_exchange_id` (bot_wizard.py:1381-1405). Delete both hardcoded `TIMEFRAMES` lists.

> **Verifier correction:** Effect is milder than stated. ChartDataProvider.fetch (chart_data.py:130-179) tries the exchange first and then FALLS BACK to CoinGecko (line 158) before returning 'FAILED: ...' (179), so a Coinbase 4h selection degrades to a different data source rather than hard-failing. The unreachable-30m/2h/6h half is exact and unmitigated.

`scaffolding` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

Reopening the dialog shows the defaults again. SMS notifications will never work no matter what is entered here.

My own greps: `_ta_weight_sliders` -> only settings_dialog.py:444 and :458; `_sms_*` -> only the 18 construction sites at 750-844 in this file, never read; `_sound_buy` -> only 597-600; `_sound_volume` -> only 655-667 (signal wiring). `_phantoms_enabled`/`_phantom_tf_checks`/`_lock_candles` hits outside this file are all in bot_live_settings.py (3087-3341) and bot_wizard.py (1666-1678), which are per-bot widgets with their own identically-named attributes reading from the bot object — not consumers of the dialog's copies. _save's complete write set is the 13-key `pairs` dict plus profit_folding, data_logging and ai_monitor. ### `src/gui/simulator_tab/fleet/fleet_replay_controller.py:190` — FleetReplayController's YTD window bounds are stored and never read — the comment claims the run loop applies them
`scaffolding` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** Both constructor parameters (lines 173-174) are stored and then never referenced again anywhere in the tree. The docstring above them (lines 182-189) states 'When set, the sim plays only candles whose ts falls within [window_since_ms, window_until_ms]' and quotes the operator directive of 2026-08-02 as satisfied. _build_sim's comment at lines 300-307 doubles down: 'The YTD window is applied at master-clock iteration time (see the run loop): the cursor advances only across [window_since_ms, window_until_ms] via CandleSeries.step_to_ts.' The run loop (_run, lines 410-531) contains no window check at all — it steps the master clock from index 0 to exhaustion.

**Operator sees:** The windowing feature the operator asked for ('the Simulator only plays the sections of the Stone Tablets that align with the user's YTD data') does not exist, but the code reads as though it shipped — so the next person debugging a window mismatch trusts a guarantee that was never implemented. Window narrowing is in fact done elsewhere (panel-side `_since_ms` at fleet_replay_panel.py:700-720), which makes the false claim harder to catch.

**Fix:** Either implement the bounds in _run() (skip master timestamps outside the range) or delete both parameters, both attributes and the two comment blocks at lines 182-189 and 300-307 that assert they work.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded high -> medium. Zero runtime effect, and the operator's directive is in fact substantively satisfied by the panel-side narrowing (_since_ms at fleet_replay_panel.py:700-720), so nothing is functionally missing — what is wrong is two comments asserting a mechanism that does not exist plus two dead public constructor params.

### `src/gui/simulator_tab/populate_nuclear_cache.py:60` — Nuclear Mode's empty-state offers a second remedy — populate_nuclear_cache — that is itself dead
`dead-feature` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The Nuclear panel's empty-state text (nuclear_mode_panel.py:260-271) gives the operator two ways out: run an RAIntSimBat battery, or 'Run the focused one-shot helper: python -m src.gui.simulator_tab.populate_nuclear_cache'. The known dead-cache finding covers the first. The second is equally dead: `populate()` calls `_load_raintsimbat()` on its first line (line 81), which resolves to `<repo_root>/sadp/RAIntSimBat/RAIntSimBat.py`. There is no `sadp/` directory at the repo root at all, so the helper raises RuntimeError before fetching anything. The module docstring at lines 21-26 still asserts 'RAIntSimBat populates the cache as a side effect of running its batteries.'

**Operator sees:** The operator follows the panel's own instruction, runs the module, and gets `RuntimeError: populate_nuclear_cache: cannot find RAIntSimBat at ...\sadp\RAIntSimBat\RAIntSimBat.py`. Both documented escape hatches from Nuclear Mode's empty state are non-functional, so Nuclear Mode cannot be un-stuck by any route the GUI describes.

**Fix:** Delete populate_nuclear_cache.py and rewrite the nuclear_mode_panel.py:260-271 empty-state message to point at the Stone Tablets fetcher (`python -m src.trading.stone_tablets.fetcher build-ytd`) — which is the same re-pointing already queued as pending task #3.

> **Verifier correction:** None. Severity kept at high-adjacent but set to medium: no data or trading harm, but unlike claims 1 and 4 this one IS reachable today — the empty-state label is on screen right now and running the documented command crashes immediately.

### `src/stocks/alpaca_connector.py:48` — The entire src/stocks package is unreachable — no GUI entry point constructs any of it
`dead-feature` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** This is the ONLY concrete BrokerBase implementation in the tree, and it is never instantiated by any live code. The whole package hangs off a GUI door that was nailed shut: src/gui/launcher.py:98 defines LauncherWindow with a 'Stock Trading' ModeCard whose click emits stocks_selected (launcher.py:168), and src/gui/stock_main_window.py:110 defines StockMainWindow — but NEITHER CLASS IS EVER INSTANTIATED. main.py:627 comments '--- Direct launch to Crypto MainWindow (no launcher) ---' and main.py:652 '--- Create main window directly (no launcher) ---'. Nothing anywhere connects the stocks_selected signal to a receiver. Downstream of that: TradingViewBridge is never constructed (stock_main_window.py:122 takes it as an injected ctor param, self._tv_bridge = tv_bridge, and the only constructor of StockMainWindow is nobody); StockBot, StockBotConfig and StockBotManager have zero users outside stock_bot.py; stock_assets.py's STOCK_UNIVERSE / STOCK_MAP / SECTORS / search_stocks / get_stocks_by_sector / get_stocks_by_tier have zero users despite the module docstring saying 'for the bot wizard'. Only market_hours.MarketHours has a consumer, and that consumer (stock_main_window.py:130) is itself unreachable. The single test touching the package (tests/test_acervator_integration.py:51) asserts only that 'src.stocks' imports cleanly — no functional coverage at all.

**Operator sees:** ANSWER TO THE ASSIGNED QUESTION: no, stock trading is not reachable. 1,772 lines across 7 modules — Alpaca broker integration, a TradingView webhook server, two bot engines, a stock universe, market-hours tracking — ship in every release, are imported by the test suite, appear in the manual, and cannot be reached by any sequence of clicks in the running application. If the operator ever re-wires the launcher expecting this to work, findings #2, #3, #4, #6, #7 and #15 below mean it still would not trade.

**Fix:** Decide the package's status explicitly and record it the way the PoA tabs were recorded (src/gui/main_window.py:3801 '--- Tab 7: Proof of Accumulation — REMOVED per P1.7 / MEM-178 ---'). Either (a) move src/stocks/ and src/gui/{launcher,stock_main_window}.py to _archive/ and drop 'src.stocks' from the test_subpackage_imports parametrize list, or (b) if it is meant to be revived, add a module-level banner to src/stocks/__init__.py (currently a 0-byte file) stating UNREACHABLE + the list of defects that must be fixed first. Do not leave it in the ambiguous middle.

> **Verifier correction:** Line count is wrong: src/stocks is 1,872 lines across 7 modules (276+178+207+405+127+309+370), not 1,772. Severity downgraded from high to medium — nothing malfunctions, this is shipped dead weight plus a misleading manual, not an active defect.

### `src/trading/analytics_engine.py:88` — AnalyticsEngine trade store is never fed — Analytics tab renders win rate, Sharpe, profit factor and per-bot performance from an always-empty list
`scaffolding` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** Neither record_trade nor record_trade_from_dict is called anywhere in the product. self._trades therefore stays empty for the life of the process. get_portfolio_summary() takes its `if not self._trades` early-return branch and returns all-zeros; get_bot_performance() returns [] because by_bot is empty; get_timeframe_comparison() returns {}. Only snapshot_equity() is actually driven (main_window.py:5028/5031), so the equity curve populates while every trade-derived metric is inert.

**Operator sees:** The Analytics tab permanently shows 0 trades, 0% win rate, 0.00 Sharpe, 0.00 profit factor, an empty per-bot performance table and an empty timeframe-comparison table, no matter how much the fleet trades. This is the 'renders dashes forever' pattern the operator has been burned by.

**Fix:** Emit into AnalyticsEngine from the fill path. The cleanest hook is BotContainer/ScrummingBot's existing trade-notification emit: on a completed scrum->fold round trip build a TradeRecord and call shared_analytics.record_trade_from_dict(...). main.py:649 already constructs `shared_analytics`; pass it to BotManager the same way set_volume_guard/set_state_manager do. If nobody wants the tab, delete AnalyticsEngine and analytics_tab together rather than shipping a permanently-zero panel.

> **Verifier correction:** Re-ran the grep myself. `grep -rn record_trade src tools main.py` returns: competition_engine.py:158 (CompetitionEngine.record_trade, different class), local_testnet.py:622/626 (calls that CompetitionEngine), version_sweep.py:837 (a regex string literal, not a call), fleet_replay_controller.py:381 (SimRunLog.record_trade, sim-only), sim_run_log.py:182 (its def), and analytics_engine.py:88/98/120 — where :120 is the internal `self.record_trade(trade)` inside record_trade_from_dict. Zero external calls to either AnalyticsEngine method. Confirmed `def record_trade(self, trade: TradeRecord):` is at line 88 verbatim with the sadp comment placed above the docstring as quoted. Consumers verified at analytics_tab.py:239/258/282. Engine instances: main_window.py:3152 (`self._analytics = AnalyticsEngine()`) and main.py:649 (`shared_analytics`, which I grepped — it has zero further references, so that one is also dead). snapshot_equity is indeed the only driven method (main_window.py:5028/5031).

### `src/trading/bot_container.py:1724` — AI Live Monitor is fed a hard-zero portfolio: check_live_monitor reads get_status() keys that no bot class emits
`scaffolding` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** Neither `BotContainer.get_status()` (bot_container.py:1242-1343) nor the only override, `ExtractorBot.get_status()` (extractor_bot.py:1573-1594), ever puts a `portfolio_value` or `passive_value` key in the returned dict. Both `.get()` calls therefore always return the default 0, so `total_port` and `total_passive` are permanently 0.0 and the `advantage` the monitor computes (`adv = portfolio - passive`, live_monitor.py:215) is permanently $0.00.

**Operator sees:** Every AI Monitor check bills an LLM call whose prompt reads "Portfolio: $0.00 | Passive: $0.00 | Adv: $+0.00" (live_monitor.py:218). The resulting advice is emitted on the `ai.feedback` bus event and shown to the operator as a real performance assessment, but the model never saw any portfolio data — it can only comment on trade counts. Any operator acting on that feedback is acting on analysis of a zeroed account.

**Fix:** Add `portfolio_value` and `passive_value` to `BotContainer.get_status()` (portfolio = `stats.position_value + stats.cash_balance_usd`, or reuse the aggregate the SpendableWidget already consumes via `get_aggregate_stats()['total_account_value_usd']`), or change `check_live_monitor` to call `self.get_aggregate_stats()` instead of summing non-existent per-bot keys. Add a test asserting the two keys exist in the status dict.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded high -> medium. The defect is exactly as described, but the AI Monitor is an opt-in feature gated on an api_key + enabled flag (check_live_monitor returns None at line 1714 when unconfigured), and the failure corrupts advisory output only — no trading path reads it.

### `src/trading/bot_container.py:762` — BotStats.accumulated_fold / accumulated_distribute are never written by any code, yet the simulator stat strip renders 'Mature' from accumulated_fold
`scaffolding` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** Both fields are declared with a comment describing an accumulator, but no code anywhere in the repository ever assigns to them — they hold the dataclass default 0.0 for the life of every bot. One of them has a live consumer: the simulator fleet stat strip sums `accumulated_fold` across bots to populate the operator-facing 'Mature' field.

**Operator sees:** The simulator stat strip's 'Mature' cell always reads 0.00 no matter how much folding the sim fleet does. This is the same class of defect v3.24.8/v3.24.9 were shipped to eliminate — the panel docstring at fleet_replay_panel.py:981-985 explicitly says telemetry proved the strip 'showed dashes across the whole strip' and that v3.24.9 'added the feed' — but this one field's producer was never built, so it silently reverted to a permanent zero rather than a dash.

**Fix:** Either wire the accumulator (increment `stats.accumulated_fold` by the fold notional in the fold-success path alongside the existing `total_folded_usd` update) or, if 'Mature' is meant to be something else, repoint fleet_replay_panel.py:1016 at a field that is actually written (e.g. `standing_surplus_usd`, bot_container.py:830) and delete both dead BotStats fields.

> **Verifier correction:** None to the substance. One nuance worth carrying into the fix: the consumer uses getattr(st, 'accumulated_fold', 0.0), so if a sim bot's stats object is a fork that lacks the field entirely the result is still 0.0 — either way 'Mature' is a permanent zero, and adding a writer to the LIVE BotStats alone would not necessarily fix the SIM strip.

### `src/trading/extractor_bot.py:1548` — Extractor Positions Held tab shows Current (USD) and delta% frozen at entry value forever — the promised async price refresh does not exist
`scaffolding` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `current_price` is set to the position's own average BUY price, so `current_value_usd` is by construction `cost_basis_base * rate`, which for an uncorrected position equals the entry USD — `delta_pct` is structurally ~0.00%. The docstring two lines above ("The detail dialog can refresh the rendered USD values asynchronously") describes a refresh that was never built: the GUI consumes these dict keys once, verbatim, and never recomputes them.

**Operator sees:** In the Extractor detail dialog's Positions Held tab, "Current (USD)" always equals "Entry (USD)" and "Δ% (USD)" always reads +0.00% — even when the bot's own `_is_in_drawdown()` has already flipped the position to DRAWDOWN state using the live ticker. The operator sees a red DRAWDOWN state cell next to a +0.00% P/L cell and has no way to judge how deep the drawdown is before deciding whether to hit that row's Manual Fire button.

**Fix:** Have the tick loop cache the last observed `alt_price_in_base` per pair on the position (e.g. `pos.last_price_base_per_alt`, updated in `tick()` step 1 where the ticker is already fetched at extractor_bot.py:1477-1478) and have `positions_for_gui()` use that cached price, falling back to `avg_buy_price_base_per_alt` only when no tick has run. Add the cache field to `export_state`/`import_state`. Then delete the stale "the detail dialog can refresh... asynchronously" sentence from the docstring.

> **Verifier correction:** The 'always reads +0.00%' characterisation is an oversimplification and understates the defect. It is exactly 0.00% only for an uncorrected position. artillery_size_usd_at_entry is written once at firing (line 1415) and NEVER updated, while corrections grow pos.cost_basis_base and rewrite pos.avg_buy_price_base_per_alt (line 1059). So a position that has fired drawdown corrections displays a spurious POSITIVE delta equal to (added capital)/(original artillery USD) — the GUI shows a fake gain on precisely the positions that are losing. Severity downgraded high -> medium: display-only, no trading effect.

### `src/trading/extractor_bot.py:1258` — Extractor compounding-tier ROLL branch is byte-identical to the LOCK branch — extractor_max_compounding_tier is an operator-settable control with no effect
`dead-feature` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** Both branches perform exactly the same capital movement (`self._chunk_free_base += base_received`). The only observable difference is the `log_kind` string and a tier counter that increments only when the position survives a partial exit. The documented behaviour of the setting (BotConfig comment at bot_container.py:421-425: "Tier N = roll N-1 times then lock realized base gain to pool") therefore never happens — nothing is ever rolled into a larger next artillery round, and the tier ceiling never gates anything. The in-line comment admits the mechanism is unimplemented, but the field is still presented to the operator as a live control.

**Operator sees:** The operator sets "Max compounding tier" to 5 expecting the bot to roll gains into progressively larger artillery rounds on a winning pair, then sets it to 1 expecting immediate lock-to-pool. Both produce identical trading behaviour and identical capital flows; only the wording of the EXTRACTOR EXIT activity-log line changes. Backtesting or tuning this dial is wasted effort.

**Fix:** Either implement the roll (carry `base_received` forward as the next artillery size for that pair — e.g. store a per-pair `_rolled_artillery_base` that `_fire_artillery` prefers over `_usd_to_base(artillery_usd)` while `compounding_tier < max_tier`), or, if it stays deferred, disable/grey the spinbox in bot_live_settings.py and bot_wizard.py with a "not yet implemented" tooltip so the operator is not tuning an inert dial.

> **Verifier correction:** 'No effect' is very slightly overstated: the branch does have ONE observable side effect beyond the log string — `pos.compounding_tier += 1` when the exit was partial (alt_units > 1e-12), which feeds the 'Tier' column in the Positions Held table (bot_live_settings.py:899). The CAPITAL movement, which is what the setting purports to control, is genuinely identical.

### `src/trading/reconciliation.py:260` — ReconciliationEngine is wired into the GUI but reconcile() is never called, and its orphan detector is hardcoded to flag every live order
`dead-feature` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** Two compounding problems. (1) `reconcile()` has zero callers anywhere in src/, tools/ or main.py — the engine is constructed at main_window.py:3154 and handed to the Journal tab at main_window.py:5054, but nothing ever awaits it, so self._results stays empty and get_latest_result() always returns None. The tab's 'Run Reconciliation' button (journal_tab.py:142) is created, styled and added to the layout but never .connect()'d to anything. (2) Even if it were called, local_order_ids is unconditionally empty, so the `if oid in local_order_ids` test at line 270 can never be true and EVERY open exchange order is classified as an OrphanedOrder with a 'cancel' recommendation from _recommend_action once it is >1h old. cancel_orphaned() likewise has zero callers, which is currently the only thing preventing that misclassification from cancelling real bot orders.

**Operator sees:** The Journal tab's Recovery panel shows 'Last Reconciliation: None' and a never-updating 'Orphaned Orders' label forever, and the 'Run Reconciliation' button does nothing when clicked. The operator has no crash-recovery order reconciliation despite the UI advertising it.

**Fix:** Decide the feature's fate explicitly. If keeping it: populate local_order_ids from ScrummingBot._main_lots / _stack_tranches order_ids and ExtractorBot._positions, wire _btn_recon.clicked to a slot that schedules reconcile() on the async loop, and gate cancel_orphaned behind operator confirmation. If dropping it: delete ReconciliationEngine + OrphanedOrder, remove the _recon_engine construction at main_window.py:3154 and the dead buttons at journal_tab.py:142/149.

> **Verifier correction:** All three proofs re-run and hold. `grep -rn '\.reconcile(' src tools main.py` → zero matches (the bot_container hits are `reconcile_with_exchange` on CapitalRegistry, a different class). `grep -rn cancel_orphaned` over the whole repo minus build/ → only reconciliation.py:300, the definition. `grep -n 'connect(' src/gui/journal_tab.py` → only lines 58, 67, 95 (filter combos and the table); _btn_recon at 142/143/147 is constructed, styled, addWidget'd and never connected — and there is no connectSlotsByName in the file. ReconciliationEngine is constructed at main_window.py:3154 and handed to journal_tab.refresh at main_window.py:5054, confirmed by reading 5052-5055. reconciliation.py:260 `local_order_ids: set = set()` is verbatim at that exact line and is unconditional; the `if oid in local_order_ids` test is at line 271 (claim said 270, off by one). Minor: the claim's quote truncates line 264 mid-sentence ('returns empty' — actual continues '(matches prior').

### `src/trading/risk_manager.py:436` — RiskManager advertises auto-pause and emergency stop; no rule action is ever executed
`dead-feature` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The module header (lines 5-6) says "enforces safety limits across all running bots. Can auto-pause bots that breach thresholds", and the class docstring lists "Actions: ... Auto-pause individual bots, Emergency stop all bots". The rules DEFAULT_RULES ship with PAUSE_BOT on per_bot_loss and PAUSE_ALL on critical_drawdown and rapid_loss. But PAUSE_BOT only adds the bot_id to a set that is read nowhere except to suppress re-alerting (line 274), PAUSE_ALL is a literal `pass` deferring to a caller that does not exist, and the single caller of evaluate() (main_window.py:5017) only forwards `alert.severity == "critical"` to the notification manager. No bot is ever paused or stopped.

**Operator sees:** At 25% portfolio drawdown or a 5%-in-5-minutes flash-crash loss the operator gets a notification popup while every bot keeps trading. The Risk tab shows the rule as action='pause_all', which reads as an armed automatic protection that does not exist.

**Fix:** Either wire the actions — return the alerts to a handler that calls bot_manager.pause_bot(bot_id) for PAUSE_BOT and pause_all() for PAUSE_ALL, and emit AlertEvent.BOT_PAUSED_BY_RISK — or downgrade every DEFAULT_RULES action to RiskAction.WARN and rewrite the module header and class docstring so they promise alerting only.

> **Verifier correction:** Verified. risk_manager.py:435 is `# Execute action`, :436 `if rule.action == RiskAction.PAUSE_BOT and bot_id:`, :437 the set-add, :438 `elif rule.action == RiskAction.PAUSE_ALL:`, :439 `pass  # Caller should handle this` — quote verbatim, cited line 436 is the first code line of the block. Module header lines 5-6 read 'enforces safety limits across all running bots. Can auto-pause bots that breach thresholds.' DEFAULT_RULES confirmed: PAUSE_ALL at :133 and :163, PAUSE_BOT at :139. My own grep for paused_by_risk|PAUSE_ALL|PAUSE_BOT outside risk_manager.py returned exactly one hit — notifications.py:39 `BOT_PAUSED_BY_RISK = "bot_paused_by_risk"`, and I confirmed that enum member is never sent. Sole evaluate() caller is main_window.py:5017; I read 5013-5023 in full — it iterates new_alerts and only sends AlertEvent.DRAWDOWN_CRITICAL for severity=='critical'. No pause, no stop.

### `src/trading/scrumming_bot.py:2131` — update_phantom_config carries a stale hardcoded timeframe blocklist that contradicts the canonical source
`duplication` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** This is a forked copy of the MEM-203 timeframe filter that has drifted from the single source of truth. `__init__` (lines 361-370) reads from `src/exchange/timeframes.py::available_timeframes`, whose coinbase entry is `frozenset({'1m','5m','15m','30m','1h','2h','6h','1d'})` (timeframes.py:39) — i.e. 1m, 2h and 30m ARE supported. The comment at __init__ line 358-359 states this explicitly: 'Coinbase Advanced Trade supports {1m, 5m, 15m, 30m, 1h, 2h, 6h, 1d} — NOT 4h, 12h, 1w'. The hardcoded dict here declares three of those supported TFs unsupported. The comment at line 2129 even claims the filter is 'consistent with __init__' — it is not.

**Operator sees:** An operator who opens Live Settings -> Phantom Bots and applies any phantom-timeframe change on a Coinbase bot silently loses the 30m phantom (and 1m/2h if selected). The caveat string tells them 'Dropped unsupported TFs for coinbase: [30m]' — which is factually wrong, Coinbase does support 30m. The tactical-timing screen the Triple Screen framework depends on quietly disappears, and it only comes back on a full bot restart (which re-runs the correct __init__ filter).

**Fix:** Delete `_EXCHANGE_UNSUPPORTED_TFS` and call the same source __init__ uses: `from ..exchange.timeframes import available_timeframes as _avail; _allowed = set(_avail(self.config.exchange_id)); filtered = [tf for tf in phantom_timeframes if tf in _allowed] if _allowed else list(phantom_timeframes)`, with the dropped set derived as the complement. Better: extract the __init__ block into a `_filter_phantom_timeframes()` helper and call it from both sites.

> **Verifier correction:** None. Every element of the claim reproduced exactly.

### `src/trading/smart_orders.py:85` — Capital-reservation "execution-level backstop" does not exist — SmartOrderEngine has zero production callers
`dead-feature` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** capital_reservation.py:29-35 declares "Two enforcement layers (defense in depth): 1. Decision-level ... 2. **Execution-level**: order placement (smart_orders.py) pre-flights against the registry before submitting to exchange. Catches races between decision and execution." scrumming_bot.py repeats the claim three times: line 9875 "The complementary BACKSTOP at smart_orders.execute() (v3.20.1) catches anything that slips past this check", line 9887 "Registry call raises -> log + fall through (smart_orders backstop is still in effect; we don't cripple all trading on a registry bug)", and line 9928 "# R28-OK: registry call defensive — smart_orders backstop still in effect". SmartOrderEngine is never instantiated anywhere in the product. Live orders go ScrummingBot/ExtractorBot -> BotContainer.guarded_place_order -> exchange.place_order; smart_orders.py is never on that path. So when the decision-level registry pre-check at scrumming_bot.py:9898 raises, the except block logs at DEBUG and falls through to place the sell with NO reservation enforcement at all.

**Operator sees:** A transient registry error (or any exception inside effective_available) silently downgrades inter-bot capital protection from two layers to zero, and the only trace is a DEBUG line. A Scrumming bot can then sell base currency an Extractor has reserved — the exact predation the 2026-05-23 operator directive exists to prevent.

**Fix:** Either (a) delete smart_orders.py and correct the three comment blocks in scrumming_bot.py plus the capital_reservation.py module docstring so they no longer promise a second layer, or (b) re-wire the pre-flight from smart_orders.execute into BotContainer.guarded_place_order so the backstop actually exists on the live path. Do NOT leave the comments as-is: they are what makes the fall-through at scrumming_bot.py:9928 look safe.

> **Verifier correction:** Confirmed with two independent greps. `class SmartOrderEngine:` is exactly at smart_orders.py:85 and the docstring is verbatim. `grep -rn SmartOrderEngine --include=*.py .` (excluding _archive/build/dist) returns ONLY smart_orders.py:33 (its own prose) and :85 (the class def) — zero constructions, zero imports anywhere in src/, tools/, main.py or the live tests/ tree. `grep -rn smart_orders` outside the module returns only prose comments in capital_reservation.py:33, extractor_bot.py:312/488, scrumming_bot.py:9875/9888/9926. I read bot_container.guarded_place_order (940-1045) in full: it dispatches to VolumeGuard.execute or exchange.place_order only — smart_orders is not on the path. Line-number nits: the doc block is at capital_reservation.py:29-35 (correct); scrumming_bot's 'Registry call raises' comment is at 9888 not 9887 (off by 1) and the R28-OK except line is at 9926 not 9928 (off by 2); the `from .capital_reservation import get_registry` is at 9898, `effective_available(` at 9900. The fall-through behaviour is real: except at 9926 → logger.debug at 9927-9931 → execution continues with no enforcement.

### `src/trading/stone_tablets/registry.py:167` — MANIFEST checksum_sha256 is written on every persist but never verified — the archive's tamper-detection column is inert
`scaffolding` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `TabletEntry.checksum_sha256` is populated by `entry_from_tablet` (storage.py:320) and round-tripped by `read_manifest` (storage.py:282), but `_load_from_manifest` never reads `e.checksum_sha256` — the loop uses only asset/timeframe/year/exchange_id. The only checksum comparison in the codebase is inside `read_tablet` (storage.py:226-232), and it compares the tablet against the checksum stored INSIDE THE SAME FILE, which is self-consistent by construction for any file rewritten wholesale. Even when that intra-file check does trip, it is a `logger.warning` and the function returns the tablet anyway (storage.py:233), so the corrupted data loads straight into the sim. The MANIFEST checksum is the only out-of-band witness and nothing ever looks at it.

**Operator sees:** A Stone Tablet that is edited, truncated, or replaced (including by a botched sync of the OneDrive-hosted archive) is loaded into the simulator with no operator-visible signal, in a subsystem the operator's own directive declares immutable. The sim then reports parity results against silently wrong candles.

**Fix:** In `_load_from_manifest`, after `read_tablet` succeeds, compare `tab.compute_checksum()` against `e.checksum_sha256` and on mismatch refuse to load the tablet, record it in an integrity list, and surface it (boot log + Simulator activity log), not just `logger.warning`. Same treatment for the intra-file drift branch at storage.py:228 — it should return None or raise, not hand back suspect data.

> **Verifier correction:** None on the facts. Severity downgraded: read_tablet's in-file comparison does catch hand-edits of the candles array (the common corruption), so the MANIFEST checksum is a second line of defence against a narrower threat (whole-file rollback/replacement) than the claim implies.

### `tools/build_agents_md.py:18` — tools/build_agents_md.py is 100% dead — same missing sadp package
`dead-feature` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** Identical failure to orphan_widget_scan.py: the module docstring says 'The canonical implementation now lives at `sadp._tools.build_agents_md`' and 'This shim preserves the dev-tree invocation surface (`python tools/build_agents_md.py ...`)', but no `sadp` package exists on disk or on sys.path. The `sys.path.insert(0, repo_root)` on line 16 inserts a directory that contains no sadp/. Line 22's `globals().update(vars(_impl)...)` — advertised for 'private helpers that test files import' — is unreachable.
