**Fix:** Restore the generator implementation into tools/build_agents_md.py, or delete the shim and update any documentation that still tells the operator to run it.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded high -> medium for the same reason as the orphan_widget_scan finding: dev tooling, loud failure, no runtime blast radius.

### `tools/orphan_widget_scan.py:10` — tools/orphan_widget_scan.py is 100% dead — imports a package that does not exist
`dead-feature` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** There is no `sadp` package anywhere: no `sadp/` directory at repo root, `find . -maxdepth 3 -type d -name sadp` returns nothing, and `python -c "import sadp"` fails with ModuleNotFoundError. The shim's `sys.path.insert(0, repo_root)` on line 8 cannot help because repo_root contains no sadp/. The whole file is 18 lines whose only purpose is to delegate to a module that was deleted with the SADP tree. The harness's own rule module confirms the retirement: tools/harness/rules/hallucination.py:124-129 lists `"sadp/"` in `_RETIRED_NAMES` as a subsystem that 'no longer exists in the current tree'.

**Operator sees:** The operator runs `python tools/orphan_widget_scan.py` — the tool built specifically to find orphaned GUI widgets, the defect class they have been repeatedly burned by — and gets a bare ModuleNotFoundError traceback instead of the documented 373-widget scan. The GUI orphan-detection capability is entirely gone with no notice.

**Fix:** Either restore the implementation into `tools/orphan_widget_scan.py` directly (the shim's whole purpose is dead), or delete the file and remove the `Bash(python tools/orphan_widget_scan.py)` allowlist entry from .claude/settings.local.json:3100 so it stops advertising a capability that does not exist.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded high -> medium: this is dev tooling with zero runtime blast radius on the trading app, and it fails LOUDLY (immediate ModuleNotFoundError traceback) rather than silently returning a wrong answer — the operator cannot be misled into thinking a scan ran.

### `main.py:648` — Four shared subsystems constructed at boot and never wired to anything
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** All three names under the comment `# Shared subsystems` are assigned once and never read again. The MainWindow constructed 4 lines later at main.py:653 takes only `bot_manager=` and `settings_manager=` — none of the three is passed in, stored, or registered. `RiskManager.__init__` (src/trading/risk_manager.py:168-187) is pure field initialization with no side-effecting registration, so constructing it accomplishes nothing. `keyring = KeyringManager()` at main.py:511 has the same shape: assigned, never used. Contrast the neighbouring lines that DO wire their objects — `bot_manager.set_volume_guard(volume_guard)` (640) and `bot_manager.set_data_pool(data_pool)` (645).

**Operator sees:** The boot sequence reads as if risk management, analytics and notifications are wired into the running app; they are not. Anyone tracing why a risk rule never fires, or why an analytics snapshot is empty, starts from a line that looks like the wiring point and is actually a no-op. Four objects are also allocated and immediately abandoned on every launch.

**Fix:** Either wire them (pass into MainWindow / register on bot_manager the way volume_guard and data_pool are at lines 640/645) or delete lines 511 and 647-650 together with their misleading `# Shared subsystems` comment. If `get_notification_manager()` is intentionally called for singleton-initialization side effects, drop the unused binding and add a comment saying so.

> **Verifier correction:** One of the four is mischaracterized. `get_notification_manager()` is a lazy singleton getter — src/core/notifications.py:353-357 reads `global _instance / if _instance is None: _instance = NotificationManager() / return _instance`. So the call at main.py:650 DOES initialize the process-wide notification manager, and any other module calling the same getter receives that instance. Notifications are therefore not 'unwired'; only the local name `shared_notif` is unused. `shared_risk`, `shared_analytics` and `keyring` are direct constructions with no such side effect and are genuinely allocated-and-abandoned. Severity stays low.

### `src/competition/competition_engine.py:278` — Grand Accumulator rarity tier is permanently unreachable — consecutive_top1 is hard-coded to 0 at the only call site
`dead-feature` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** classify_tier gates the Grand Accumulator tier on 'if rank_pct <= 0.01 and consecutive_top1 >= 3' (src/competition/season_schedule.py:124). This is the only call site of classify_tier in the tree and it passes the literal 0, so 0 >= 3 is never true and the branch is unreachable. The data to satisfy it already exists and is already maintained: BotRating.consecutive_top1 (src/competition/challenge_protocol.py:64, commented 'for Grand Accumulator eligibility') is incremented for the winner at line 121 and reset for the loser at line 122 by RatingRegistry.record_result, and is serialised in to_dict at line 84 — but nothing anywhere ever READS it back. So a full accounting layer feeds a value into a parameter that is discarded. The tier itself is fully specified: RarityTier(name='Grand Accumulator', max_ever=1_000, base_value=500) at season_schedule.py:92-100, hard-capped again at local_testnet.py:391, exposed as remainingGrandAccumulator in the on-chain ABI at base_config.py:179, and given its own 80-line SVG generator (trophy_generator.py:115 grand_accumulator_svg, dispatched at trophy_generator.py:176).

**Operator sees:** A 500-ACRV tier with a hand-drawn trophy, a supply cap, an on-chain view function and a working Elo tracker behind it can never be awarded to anyone. In practice the neighbouring tiers are near-unreachable too, since adjudicate computes rank_pct = (i+1)/n at line 271: with n=3 bots (run_demo_competition's default) the winner's rank_pct is 0.333, which clears only Harvest. Gold Fold needs n>=10, Grand Accumulator n>=100, Ekthelius n>=1000.

**Fix:** Thread the registry through. Give CompetitionEngine an optional 'rating_registry: Optional[RatingRegistry] = None' constructor arg alongside 'ledger', and at line 278 pass 'consecutive_top1 = (self._rating_registry.get_or_create(sub.bot_id).consecutive_top1 if self._rating_registry else 0)'. Then have adjudicate call record_result(winner_id=ranked[0].bot_id, loser_id=...) so the counter actually advances between seasons. Delete the TODO once done. If the tier is intentionally shelved with the rest of PoA (MEM-178), say so in the comment instead of leaving a TODO that reads as work-in-progress.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded medium -> low: the competition subsystem has no reachable GUI surface (main_window.py:3804 _competition_tab=None, :3810 _testnet_tab=None), so no tier is ever awarded to anyone by any path today.

### `src/competition/season_schedule.py:52` — RarityTier.qualifies() has zero callers and has already drifted from the classify_tier logic that replaced it
`duplication` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** This is the declarative, per-tier eligibility test — it reads self.rank_pct_max off the RarityTier instance, which is the single source of truth for each tier's threshold. It is called from nowhere. The live path is classify_tier (line 115), a separate function that hard-codes every threshold as a magic number in an if-chain: 0.001 at line 122, 0.01 at line 124, 0.25 at line 126, 0.10 at line 129, 0.50 at line 131. Those literals duplicate the rank_pct_max fields declared at lines 71, 78, 88, 97 and 106. The two implementations have already diverged in behaviour: qualifies() checks rank_pct_max BEFORE the Bear Slayer regime test, and classify_tier's Bear Slayer branch checks the regime first and never consults the tier object at all. Changing any rank_pct_max in the RARITY_TIERS table would therefore change nothing about actual tier assignment — the classic 'fix one, the other stays wrong' shape. The same duplication extends to the supply caps: RarityTier.max_ever is 10_000 / 1_000 / 21 (lines 89, 98, 108), enforced generically in TokenLedger.award (token_ledger.py:114-119), but re-hard-coded as bare literals in LocalRegistry.adjudicate (local_testnet.py:387 '>= 21', line 391 '>= 1000') and again in LocalTestnet.get_competition_stats (local_testnet.py:539-540 '21 - ...', '1000 - ...'). Those three copies happen to agree today.

**Operator sees:** No runtime symptom today — this is a trap set for the next person to tune the tier thresholds. They will edit the RARITY_TIERS table (the obvious, documented, self-describing place), observe no change in adjudication, and lose hours before finding the magic numbers in classify_tier.

**Fix:** Delete classify_tier's magic numbers and drive it off the table: iterate RARITY_TIERS sorted by base_value descending and 'return next((t for t in tiers if t.qualifies(rank_pct, market_regime) and _extra_conditions_met(t, perfect, consecutive_top1)), None)'. That gives one source of truth and makes qualifies() live. Then replace the hard-coded 21/1000 in local_testnet.py:387, :391, :539 and :540 with TIER_BY_NAME['Ekthelius'].max_ever and TIER_BY_NAME['Grand Accumulator'].max_ever. If instead qualifies() is genuinely obsolete, delete it — a dead method that looks authoritative is worse than no method.

> **Verifier correction:** Several secondary line cites are off by 1-2. Actual: rank_pct_max declarations at 69, 78, 87, 96, 105 (claim said 71, 78, 88, 97, 106); the 0.10 test is at line 128 and the 0.50 test at line 130 (claim said 129 and 131 — those are the return lines); max_ever=21 is at line 107 (claim said 108). The 0.001@122, 0.01@124, 0.25@126 cites are exact. Substance unaffected.

### `src/core/mini_display.py:843` — mini_display.py — 848-line hardware display subsystem with zero consumers repo-wide
`dead-feature` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The section header at line 829 says the singleton is 'initialised lazily by AcervatorOS'. Nothing initialises it. `init_displays`, `get_manager`, `MiniDisplayManager`, `DisplayType`, `MessageType` and all five adapter classes have no importer anywhere in src/, tools/, main.py or tests/. The only references left in the repo are archived doc generators and an archived coverage test. Nothing ever constructs a DisplayMessage, so `notify_trade`, `notify_price`, `notify_error` and the whole compatibility matrix are unreachable.

**Operator sees:** 848 lines of hardware-driver code ship in every build and appear in the product manual as a feature ('AcervatorOS Mini Display Manager'). Plugging in an SSD1306 or e-Paper panel does nothing — no I2C scan is ever performed because `detect_displays` is never called.

**Fix:** Delete the module, or wire it: call `init_displays()` from main.py's startup alongside the other subsystem initialisers and route `notify_trade` / `notify_bot_state` / `notify_error` off the existing event bus (`trade.filled`, `bot.state`, `bot.error`). Note that if it is revived, `notify_price` at line 747 emits a literal '%%' — `f"{arrow} {change_pct:+.2f}%%"` inside an f-string is not an escape.

> **Verifier correction:** Correct, including the exact line number. `def init_displays(...)` is at line 843 and the file is exactly 848 lines (`wc -l` -> 848). The quoted six lines are verbatim. The section header at line 829 reads `# Module-level singleton (initialised lazily by AcervatorOS)`. I re-ran the caller search myself: `grep -rn "mini_display|MiniDisplayManager|init_displays|get_manager|DisplayType|MessageType|DisplayMessage" src/ tools/ tests/ main.py --include=*.py`, filtering out the module's own file, returns ZERO hits — no dynamic getattr, no signal/slot string, no importlib reference. I also confirmed the 'ships in every build' sub-claim, which the scanner asserted without proof: Acervator_win.spec:47 adds the whole `src` tree as datas and line 75 does `collect_submodules('src')`, and `grep -c mini_display build/Acervator_win/Analysis-00.toc` -> 4.

### `src/core/notifications.py:288` — NotificationManager._send_in_app computes a log level and discards it — CRITICAL alerts are logged at INFO
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `level` is assigned on all three branches and then never read — the emit on line 293 is a hardcoded `logger.info`. Every in-app notification lands at INFO severity regardless of priority. `logger` here is `acervator.notifications`, a child of the `acervator` logger that LogManager attaches its system.log FileHandler to (logging_engine.py:313-326), so these records are written to ~/.acervator_logs/console/system.log formatted as `[INFO]`.

**Operator sees:** An operator triaging a fault by grepping console/system.log for ERROR or WARNING sees nothing for BOT_PAUSED_BY_RISK, DRAWDOWN_CRITICAL, BOT_ERROR or CONNECTION_LOST. All of them are buried in the INFO stream alongside routine chatter.

**Fix:** Replace the hardcoded call with the computed level: `getattr(logger, level)("NOTIFICATION [%s]: %s — %s", priority.value, title, message)`, or an explicit if/elif dispatching to logger.error / logger.warning / logger.info.

> **Verifier correction:** Code defect confirmed exactly; the claimed effect is overstated. Lines 288-293 are verbatim: `level` is assigned on all three branches and the emit at 293 is a hardcoded `logger.info(...)`. I read the full method — `level` appears zero times on any right-hand side. `logger = logging.getLogger("acervator.notifications")` at notifications.py:17, and logging_engine.py:313-326 is exactly as cited (`self._sys_logger = logging.getLogger("acervator")` at 313, `FileHandler(self._console_dir / "system.log")` at 322, `addHandler` at 326), so the child propagates into console/system.log formatted `[INFO]`. DRAWDOWN_CRITICAL's rule is at 111-115 and BOT_PAUSED_BY_RISK's at 92-95, both CRITICAL with IN_APP. WHERE THE CLAIM OVERREACHES: it says the operator 'sees nothing for BOT_PAUSED_BY_RISK, DRAWDOWN_CRITICAL, BOT_ERROR or CONNECTION_LOST'. Only DRAWDOWN_CRITICAL and PNL_MILESTONE are ever emitted anywhere in the codebase — `grep -rn "AlertEvent\.|notif_manager.send|check_pnl_milestone" src/ main.py` outside notifications.py returns only main_window.py:5020-5023 (DRAWDOWN_CRITICAL, live via RiskManager.evaluate) and main_window.py:5040 (check_pnl_milestone). BOT_PAUSED_BY_RISK, BOT_ERROR and CONNECTION_LOST have no emit site at all. Also, the log line text is `NOTIFICATION [critical]: ...`, so the priority IS greppable in system.log — it is the levelname field that is wrong, not the information.

### `src/core/sound_engine.py:421` — SoundEngine plays nothing on macOS/Linux — playback is gated on win32 with no fallback
`dead-feature` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** There is no `else` branch and no darwin/linux path. On any non-Windows platform `play()` runs the full generation pipeline (the ~400ms sniper synth, coins-in-bucket, water drip — all pure-Python sample loops), writes the WAV to a temp dir, then does nothing with it and returns True-ish silence. macOS is a supported build target: Acervator_mac.spec and build_mac.sh both exist, and the operator maintains a Mac Mini install. No darwin playback branch exists anywhere in src/core.

**Operator sees:** On the Mac build, every sound toggle in Settings (buy/sell/error/bot-state/fire/track/profit/drip) is a switch that does nothing. Scrum and fold fire events, error alerts, and the DRAWDOWN_CRITICAL SOUND channel are all silent, with only a debug-level log line if anything goes wrong.

**Fix:** Add platform branches after the win32 case: on darwin `subprocess.Popen(["afplay", path])`, on linux `subprocess.Popen(["aplay", "-q", path])`, falling back to PySide6's QSoundEffect if neither binary is present. Also short-circuit `_ensure_sounds()` when no backend is available so the synth loops do not burn CPU generating WAVs that will never be played.

> **Verifier correction:** Correct, with one wording nit. The quoted block is lines 419-425; cited line 421 is the `if sys.platform == "win32":` gate — right anchor, verbatim. There is no `else`, no darwin/linux branch. I independently verified the wasted-work half, which the scanner asserted: `_ensure_sounds` at lines 39-60 is NOT platform-gated — it unconditionally mkdtemps and runs `_gen_tone` x4, `_gen_sniper`, `_gen_coins_in_bucket`, `_gen_water_drip`, then sets `self._available = True` on any OS, so `play()` clears the 411-417 guards and falls into a no-op. `grep -rn "darwin|afplay|aplay|playsound|QSound" src/core/ src/gui/` returns exactly one hit, src/gui/screen_recorder.py:673, which is a screen-capture platform branch, not audio. Acervator_mac.spec and build_mac.sh both exist. NIT: the claim says play() 'returns True-ish silence' — `play()` returns None; it has no return value. Immaterial to the defect.

### `src/core/system_load_oscillator.py:55` — system_load_oscillator.py — 260-line Nuclear Mode oscillator with zero callers
`dead-feature` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The class docstring and module header describe a contract with an engine that never materialised: 'Caller gets current multiplier via current_multiplier() on each tick; the engine scales both tick_interval and workload count.' No engine imports it. `SystemLoadOscillator`, `current_multiplier`, `tick_workload`, `effective_tick_interval` have no reference anywhere in src/, tools/, main.py or tests/ — only an archived test file and archived manual generators. The v3.13.7 operator directive quoted at lines 4-7 ('system speed... now oscillates in 2m intervals with a 30s sustain at max load speed') is unimplemented at the call site.

**Operator sees:** Nuclear Mode runs at a flat tick rate. The operator-requested 2-minute load pulse with 30s sustain at 4x, and the COOLING protection that was supposed to back it off under STRESS/CRITICAL, never engage — and there is no error to indicate that.

**Fix:** Wire it into the Nuclear Mode tick loop in src/gui/simulator_tab/nuclear_controller.py (construct with the SystemLoadMR, call `start()` on run, `effective_tick_interval(base)` for the sleep and `tick_workload()` for candles-per-tick, `stop()` on halt), or delete the module and the v3.13.7 directive record with it.

> **Verifier correction:** Zero-callers claim confirmed with exact line numbers; the claimed EFFECT is partly unverified. `class SystemLoadOscillator:` is at line 55 and the file is 260 lines. The quoted docstring (lines 56-60) is verbatim. The operator directive is at lines 4-7 (line 4 is the 'User directive Session 18 (turn 11):' label, 5-7 the quote). I re-ran the search: `grep -rn "SystemLoadOscillator|system_load_oscillator|tick_workload|effective_tick_interval|current_multiplier" src/ tools/ tests/ main.py --include=*.py`, excluding the module's own file, returns ZERO hits — including `current_multiplier`, which the scanner listed but the effect narrative depends on. CAVEAT I could not close: the claimed effect asserts 'Nuclear Mode runs at a flat tick rate'. I verified nothing calls the oscillator, but I did NOT open the Nuclear Mode tick engine to confirm it uses a fixed interval rather than some other pacing mechanism, so 'flat tick rate' is inference, not proof. The safe statement is: the operator-directed 2-minute pulse / 30s sustain / COOLING backoff is not wired to anything.

### `src/core/usb_auth.py:468` — USB hardware-mode credential READ path has zero callers — the whole security feature is inert
`dead-feature` · **low** · verdict PARTIAL · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The module header (lines 21-24) promises: 'When hardware mode is active for an exchange: App scans mounted volumes for .acervator_auth + matching serial. If found, decrypts credentials from USB — never from local vault. If USB not present, exchange is locked.' None of that happens. `import_credentials_from_usb` and `find_auth_volume` have zero production callers repo-wide. The GUI (src/gui/usb_auth_widget.py) only calls `list_usb_volumes`, `export_credentials_to_usb`, and `verify_auth_file`, and it persists `hardware_mode`/`hw_volume_serial` into settings — but nothing ever reads those keys back. The only real credential-load path, src/gui/main_window.py:5787-5802, goes straight to the local vault with no hardware_mode check.

**Operator sees:** The operator exports credentials to a USB key, flips the widget to 'Hardware Mode: ON', sees a green LED, and believes the exchange is locked without the key. It is not. Credentials continue to be decrypted from the local vault whether or not the USB is inserted, and removing the USB does not lock anything.

**Fix:** Either (a) wire it: in main_window's connect path, before the `decrypt(exch_config["api_key_enc"], ...)` block at 5798, check `exch_config.get("hardware_mode")` and when true call `import_credentials_from_usb(exch_config["hw_volume_serial"])`, failing the connect with the returned message when the key is absent; or (b) if hardware mode is not intended to ship, remove the toggle from usb_auth_widget.py and the two ExchangeConfig fields so the GUI stops advertising a protection that does not exist.

> **Verifier correction:** Every FACT in the claim is correct, but the claimed EFFECT is impossible and the severity is wrong. I reproduced: `import_credentials_from_usb` (line 468) and `find_auth_volume` (line 328) have zero hits outside src/core/usb_auth.py itself and _archive/tests_pre_2026_07_25/. `hardware_mode`/`hw_volume_serial` appear only at src/core/settings.py:129-130 (field decls), usb_auth.py:19 (docstring), and src/gui/usb_auth_widget.py (writes + widget-state reads). main_window.py:5787-5802 is verbatim as quoted — straight to the local vault, no hardware branch. WHAT THE SCANNER MISSED: `USBAuthWidget` is itself never instantiated. `grep -rn 'USBAuthWidget|usb_auth_widget' src/` returns ONLY its own class definition at usb_auth_widget.py:225, and `grep -rn 'USB|Hardware Key' src/gui/*.py` excluding that file returns ZERO hits. The widget is not embedded in settings_dialog.py or main_window.py. So the operator cannot 'flip the widget to Hardware Mode: ON' or 'see a green LED' — there is no reachable UI. Nothing writes hardware_mode into settings either, so the false-assurance scenario cannot occur. This is an orphaned ~500-line module, not a security control that lies. Per the taxonomy ('wired into the GUI or public API but non-functional') it does not even qualify as dead-feature in the operator-visible sense.

### `src/exchange/ccxt_connector.py:986` — `place_order` fetches market metadata into a variable that is never read
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `market` is assigned and never referenced again anywhere in `place_order` (lines 959-1078) or in the rest of the module. The line survives only as an accidental symbol-existence assertion — `self._ex.market()` raises `BadSymbol` for an unknown pair — which is undocumented, so the next reader cannot tell whether the call is load-bearing or leftover. It sits between `_ensure_connected()` and `amount_to_precision()` on the live order-submission path, where the limits/precision data it returns is exactly what a min-cost or min-amount pre-flight check would need. That check does not exist: `place_order` submits whatever amount it is handed, and a below-min-notional order is only caught by the exchange.

**Operator sees:** None today beyond a dead round-trip, but it is a trap: a reader deleting the "unused" line silently removes the only symbol validation on the order path. And the natural pre-flight guard it enables is absent, so an under-minimum SCRUM or FOLD fails at the venue with a raw exchange error rather than a clear local one.

**Fix:** Either delete line 986, or make its intent real — use the returned `market["limits"]` to reject below-min-cost / below-min-amount orders locally with an operator-legible message before `create_order`, and add a comment naming the check.

> **Verifier correction:** Line 986 is verbatim `        market = self._ex.market(symbol)`. I ran my own broader grep for `\bmarket\b` across the whole file rather than the scanner's narrower pattern: hits are 132 ('Exited US market Nov 2019', comment), 140 and 143 (URL path segments in health-probe endpoints), 986 (the assignment), 989/991/999 (the English word in market-buy comments), and 1194 ('market orders' in the _parse_order docstring). No read of the variable anywhere. I also read place_order end-to-end (959-1078) and confirmed nothing consumes it — line 987 immediately calls `self._ex.amount_to_precision(symbol, amount)`, which takes the symbol, not the market dict. The 'accidental symbol-existence assertion' characterisation is fair: ccxt's Exchange.market() raises BadSymbol for an unknown pair. The observation that AssetInfo.min_amount/min_cost are built from the same limits dict in get_markets (1173-1174) also checks out.

### `src/exchange/data_pool.py:664` — `MarketDataPool.refresh_all` / `get_candles` / `is_fresh` / `get_status` are all dead; the module docstring still documents the dead architecture
`dead-feature` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The pool was rewritten in v3.23.74/76 to passive on-demand coalescing (`get_or_fetch_ticker` / `get_or_fetch_ohlcv` / `get_or_fetch_balance`), and the original push architecture was never removed. `refresh_all` (line 664), `get_candles` (line 211), `is_fresh` (line 226) and `get_status` (line 730) now have zero callers. The module header at data_pool.py:8-19 still teaches the dead model verbatim — "Bots register their data needs; the pool fetches once and distributes" and the worked example `await pool.refresh_all(connectors)  # 2 API calls, not 3`. The dead `refresh_all` also carries a latent bug that would bite anyone who revived it: at lines 690-694 it copies `last/bid/ask/volume_24h/fetch_time` but NEVER copies `ticker.timestamp`, so `TickerEntry.timestamp` would stay 0.0 while `get_or_fetch_ticker` (line 368) does set it. `CacheEntry.subscribers` / `TickerEntry.subscribers`, maintained by `register`/`unregister` (lines 185, 191, 198, 205), are read only by these dead methods.

**Operator sees:** A developer reading data_pool.py's header believes there is a scheduled pool refresh driving the caches, and will look for the (nonexistent) scheduler when diagnosing stale data — the same class of misdirection as the Nuclear Mode placeholder comment. Anyone who wires `refresh_all` back in gets tickers whose `timestamp` is silently 0.0.

**Fix:** Delete `refresh_all`, `get_candles`, `is_fresh`, `get_status` and the now-unused `self._lock`, `self._total_fetches`, `self._cache_hits`; or, if `refresh_all` is meant to come back, add `te.timestamp = ticker.timestamp` at line 694 and wire it to a caller. Either way rewrite the module docstring (lines 8-19) to describe the actual passive `get_or_fetch_*` coalescing model.

> **Verifier correction:** Line 664 and the 3-line quote are verbatim. Dead-method claim reproduced by my own greps: `refresh_all` — only hits in the tree are data_pool.py:19 (docstring) and :664 (definition); every other repo hit is `refresh_all_privacy_widgets` (main_window.py:2517/2518/4485) or testnet_tab's `_refresh_all`, which are unrelated symbols. `is_fresh` — the definition at data_pool.py:226 is the ONLY occurrence in src/, tools/, tests/, main.py. `get_candles` — the only external hit is fleet_replay_panel.py:715 calling it on the Stone Tablets registry (registry.py:284), a different class, exactly as claimed. Pool `get_status` — I grepped `pool.get_status` across the tree: zero hits; main_window.py:2403 uses `pool.pull_rate_summary()` (line 2404), confirmed by reading 2398-2415. The stale-doc half is confirmed: data_pool.py:10 says 'Bots register their data needs; the pool fetches once and distributes' and line 19 is `  await pool.refresh_all(connectors)            # 2 API calls, not 3` (the claim's body reproduced this with collapsed whitespace — cosmetic). The latent timestamp bug is confirmed: refresh_all copies last/bid/ask/volume_24h/fetch_time at 690-694 and never touches `te.timestamp`, while get_or_fetch_ticker sets `entry.timestamp` at line 368. ONE OVERSTATEMENT: the claim that `subscribers` is 'read only by these dead methods' is wrong — unregister reads it live at lines 198-199 and 205-206 to decide slot deletion. It is only the aggregate reads at 683, 703 and 732 that are dead.

### `src/gui/bot_visualizer.py:2185` — Paper Swarm "Net PnL" and Simulator Swarm "Aggregate trades" summary readouts are hardcoded to a dash and can never show a number
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** Both labels sit in prominent summary group boxes ("PORTFOLIO SUMMARY", "SWARM SUMMARY") and both have live data available that the code declines to use. `update_paper_run` (2059-2073) receives `pnl` and stashes it as `h["_pnl"]` on every tick, and `_update_paper_summary` is called at the end of that same function — yet it overwrites the label with a literal dash instead of summing `_live_paper_rows` values. The sim side does sum `_pnl` for its PnL row (2172-2177), proving the pattern was known, but `update_sim_run` (1998-2010) likewise receives `trades` and the aggregate-trades row is still a literal dash. These are the operator's "column that renders dashes forever" pattern.

**Operator sees:** With live paper sessions running and per-row PnL updating correctly, the Portfolio Summary bar directly beneath still reads "Net PnL: —". Same for "Aggregate trades: —" in the Simulator Swarm summary. The operator cannot tell whether the number is genuinely zero, still loading, or broken.

**Fix:** In `_update_paper_summary`, mirror the sim PnL block: `pnls = [h.get("_pnl", 0) for h in self._live_paper_rows.values()]` then set `f"Net PnL: ${sum(pnls):+,.2f}"` when non-empty. In `_update_sim_summary`, have `update_sim_run` stash `h["_trades"] = trades` (alongside the existing `h["_pnl"]` at 2009) and sum it for the aggregate row.

> **Verifier correction:** Minor: the sim side's data is less readily available than claimed. `update_sim_run` (1998-2010) writes `trades` into `h["trades_lbl"]` but does NOT stash it on the handle (unlike `_pnl` at 2010), so summing aggregate trades would require adding a stash, not just a sum. The paper-PnL half is exactly as claimed — `update_paper_run` (2059-2073) does `h["_pnl"] = pnl` at 2072 then calls `_update_paper_summary()` at 2073, which overwrites with the literal at 2185. Also the constructors' initial text differs slightly from the summary text: `_sim_swarm_trades` is built as 'Trades: —' at 1366 and only becomes 'Aggregate trades: —' at 2178.

### `src/gui/journal_tab.py:142` — Journal tab's "Run Reconciliation" and "Save Snapshot" buttons have no clicked handler
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict PARTIAL

**Broken:** Both buttons are constructed, styled and added to the layout, but neither ever gets a `.clicked.connect(...)`. Every other button in this file and its sibling tabs is connected at construction (compare alerts_tab.py:81, 111, 152). JournalTab holds live `self._recon` and `self._recovery` handles (lines 37-38) and reads from them in refresh(), so the backing subsystems exist -- only the invocation path is missing.

**Operator sees:** In the Trade Journal & Recovery tab's State Recovery panel, clicking "Run Reconciliation" or "Save Snapshot" does nothing at all -- no dialog, no log line, no state change. The adjacent labels ("Last Snapshot: None", "Last Reconciliation: None") therefore stay at their defaults unless some other subsystem happens to run a reconciliation.

**Fix:** Connect _btn_recon to a slot that schedules self._recon.reconcile(...) on the async loop and then calls self.refresh(), and _btn_snapshot to self._recovery.save_snapshot() + self.refresh(). If the recovery subsystem is not meant to be operator-triggered, remove both buttons.

> **Verifier correction:** The unconnected-button fact is exact: my grep for `_btn_recon|_btn_snapshot` over the whole repo returns exactly the 6 hits at journal_tab.py lines 142, 143, 147, 149, 150, 154 — construction, setStyleSheet, addWidget. No .connect, no setObjectName (so no Qt auto-connect), no getattr dispatch. Quote at 142-147 is verbatim. BUT the claimed effect cannot occur: JournalTab is never instantiated. main_window.py:3840 is `self._journal_tab = None` under the comment '--- Trade Journal — REMOVED per P1.7 / MEM-178 ---' and stock_main_window.py:378 does the same. The only other reference is a null-guarded refresh at main_window.py:5052-5053. There is no 'Trade Journal & Recovery tab' in the shipping UI to click in.

### `src/gui/main_window.py:4421` — Pulse target list still animates the retired, layout-less P/L card and omits the two cards that replaced it
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** v3.15.50 replaced the P/L card with the Scrummed / Folded high-score cards. `_stat_pnl` is constructed at line 3268, immediately `setVisible(False)` at 3269, and is deliberately excluded from the header layout loop at 3306-3308 — so it is a parentless, permanently hidden orphan widget. _setup_pulse was never updated: it attaches a QGraphicsOpacityEffect to that orphan and drives setOpacity on it every 80 ms forever, while `_stat_scrummed` and `_stat_folded` — the two cards the operator actually asked for — get no effect at all. `refresh_all_privacy_widgets` (line 4501) was correctly updated to the new card set; _setup_pulse was not, so the two lists have drifted.

**Operator sees:** The Scrummed and Folded cards sit visually dead next to Trades/Bots/Errors, which breathe — the header strip pulses inconsistently. Meanwhile a hidden widget is repainted 12.5 times a second for the entire session.

**Fix:** Replace `self._stat_pnl` with `self._stat_scrummed, self._stat_folded` in pulse_targets so the list matches the five cards actually laid out at line 3306. If `_stat_pnl` is genuinely only a programmatic value holder, drop the StatCard and keep a plain float attribute instead of an orphan widget.

> **Verifier correction:** Quote verbatim at lines 4421-4424. Every supporting line re-read from scratch: 3268 `self._stat_pnl = StatCard("P/L", "$0.00")`, 3269 `self._stat_pnl.setVisible(False)`, and the layout loop at 3306-3308 `for card in [self._stat_scrummed, self._stat_folded, self._stat_trades, self._stat_bots, self._stat_errors]: top_row.addWidget(card, stretch=1)` — _stat_pnl is genuinely excluded, and the comment at 3264-3267 says so explicitly ('the card just isn't laid out'). My own `grep -n "_stat_pnl"` returns 3264(comment), 3268, 3269, 4422, 4755 — never passed to addWidget. Drift against the sibling list confirmed: refresh_all_privacy_widgets at 4501-4503 iterates ('_stat_scrummed','_stat_folded','_stat_trades','_stat_bots','_stat_errors') — updated correctly — while _setup_pulse at 4421-4424 still carries _stat_pnl and lacks both new cards. _pulse_tick at 4458-4466 does drive setOpacity over self._pulse_effects every 80ms (timer started at 4433). Minor overstatement to correct: 'a hidden widget is repainted 12.5 times a second' — setOpacity on a QGraphicsOpacityEffect attached to a widget with setVisible(False) does not trigger a repaint; the call is made but costs nothing beyond the call itself.

### `src/gui/main_window.py:134` — Three GUI classes and a CSS constant are defined but referenced nowhere in the live tree
`dead-feature` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

PulseManager (line 134) is never instantiated — its role was superseded by MainWindow._setup_pulse/_pulse_tick; note its __init__ unconditionally starts a 50 ms QTimer, so the class is booby-trapped for anyone who does construct it. NotificationSpool (line 541) was superseded by the _NotifyStub defined inline at line 3473 when MEM-247 removed the notifications panel, but the class body was left behind.

**Operator sees:** No runtime effect, but ~80 lines of the 6,900-line file read as live UI machinery. A future maintainer wiring 'the pulse manager' would get a second, conflicting 50 ms opacity animation fighting the real one in _pulse_tick.

We use QTimer-based opacity pulse instead.`, line 134 `class PulseManager:`. The cited anchor 134 is the class line and the claim correctly attributes PULSE_CSS to 125 and NotificationSpool to 541 in its prose. Re-ran `grep -rn "NotificationSpool|_NotifyStub|PulseManager|PULSE_CSS" --include=*.py src/ tests/ tools/ main.py`: hits are ONLY main_window.py:125 (PULSE_CSS def), 134 (PulseManager def), 541 (`class NotificationSpool(QTextEdit):`), plus _NotifyStub at 3473/3488/3490/3539/3540 which is the live replacement (`self._spool = self._NotifyStub(self._status_log)` at 3540). Zero instantiations of PulseManager or NotificationSpool, zero applications of PULSE_CSS, and no live test or tool touches any of the three. The booby-trap claim is accurate: PulseManager.__init__ at 136-141 ends with `self._timer.start(50)`, unconditionally, so constructing it starts a second 50ms opacity animation that would fight _pulse_tick's 80ms one.

### `src/gui/main_window.py:3819` — hasattr guard on _paper_trader protects a branch that can never be true
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `self._paper_trader` is hard-assigned to None at line 3779, forty lines above this check, and the only other assignment (`self._paper_trader = self._paper_trader_equity` / `_paper_trader_crypto` in _toggle_trading_mode at 6393/6411) is itself gated on `_paper_trader_stack is not None`, which is hard-assigned None at line 3780 and never reassigned. So the attribute is permanently None and this branch is unreachable. It also calls set_bot_viz, which as shown separately exists nowhere in the codebase — so even if it were reachable it would AttributeError into a bare pass.

**Operator sees:** None at runtime, but the code implies a Paper Trader tab is wired up when Phase E has not landed. Anyone tracing 'why doesn't the Paper Trader see the Bot Swarm' will chase this branch for nothing.

**Fix:** Delete the block (and its twin at 3819-3823) until Phase E actually lands a Paper Trader. Re-add it together with the tab, using `hasattr(self._paper_trader, 'set_bot_viz')` on the callee rather than a hasattr on self.

> **Verifier correction:** Quote verbatim at lines 3819-3823; cited anchor 3819 is exact. I re-ran `grep -n "_paper_trader" src/gui/main_window.py` myself: 3749 (a docs path, not code), 3779 `self._paper_trader = None`, 3780 `self._paper_trader_stack = None`, 3781/3782 the crypto/equity sentinels, 3819/3821 this branch, and 6391-6393 / 6409-6411 in _toggle_trading_mode. Both reassignment sites are gated exactly as claimed — `if getattr(self, '_paper_trader_stack', None) is not None:` at 6391 and 6409 — and `_paper_trader_stack` has exactly one assignment in the entire file, `= None` at 3780. I also ran `grep -rn "_paper_trader" --include=*.py src/ main.py tools/ | grep -v main_window.py` which returns nothing, ruling out external assignment. So `self._paper_trader` is permanently None and line 3819's `is not None` can never be true. The secondary point is also correct: set_bot_viz does not exist (see finding 3), so the body would AttributeError into a bare pass even if reached.

### `src/gui/main_window.py:4608` — Four abbreviation-tooltip entries can never fire because the KPI labels render uppercase and the match is case-sensitive
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The dictionary carries entries keyed 'Realised', 'Locked', 'Mature' and 'Exch' (lines 4556-4558, 4591) that exist solely to explain the SpendableProfitsWidget KPI columns. But v3.19.29 changed those labels to all-caps: the loop at line 849 builds QLabel('REALISED'), QLabel('LOCKED'), QLabel('MATURE'), QLabel('EXCH'). `abbrev in text` is a case-sensitive substring test, so none of the four ever matches, and no other key matches those strings either (I checked every 2-3 char key against each label: REALISED/LOCKED/MATURE/EXCH contain none of TA, BB, TF, WS, Vol, API, Grid, ...). The four dictionary entries are dead data.

**Operator sees:** Hovering the REALISED, LOCKED, MATURE or EXCH labels in the Spendable Profits strip produces no tooltip, even though the definitions the operator would want ('profits from positions filled 30+ days ago, safely withdrawable') are sitting in the dictionary.

**Fix:** Make the match case-insensitive: `_t = text.lower()` then `if abbrev.lower() in _t`. Beware this widens matching, so also sort keys longest-first before the loop so 'StochRSI' wins over 'RSI'. Alternatively set the four tooltips directly in SpendableProfitsWidget.__init__ next to the SPENDABLE one at line 820, which is more deterministic than a 5-second rescan.

> **Verifier correction:** Quote verbatim at lines 4607-4610 (anchor 4608 is inside the block). Dictionary entries confirmed at the exact lines claimed: 4556 'Realised', 4557 'Locked', 4558 'Mature', 4591 'Exch'. Label construction confirmed at 849-853: `for label_text, key in [("REALISED", "total_realised"), ("LOCKED", "locked"), ("MATURE", "mature"), ("EXCH", "exchanges"),]` with `lbl = QLabel(label_text)` at 866 — the claim's cited line 849 is exactly the loop header. `abbrev in text` at 4608 is a plain case-sensitive substring test with no .lower() anywhere in the path. I independently re-enumerated all 35 dictionary keys against each of the four uppercase strings: the only all-caps keys are P/L, P&L, TA, BB, MACD, RSI, EMA, SMA, TF, OHLCV, API, WS, CCXT, IDLE, RUNNING, PAUSED, COOLDOWN, and none is a substring of REALISED, LOCKED, MATURE, or EXCH (the claim's parenthetical is right — MATURE contains 'AT', not 'TA'). Also confirmed the skip guard at 4605 `if not text or label.toolTip(): continue` does not save them: these four QLabels get no setToolTip anywhere, unlike _spend_label which gets one at 820-822, so they enter the loop and fall through with nothing.

### `src/gui/screen_recorder.py:535` — screen_recorder.py (686 lines) is entirely unreachable - nothing in the repo imports it
`dead-feature` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** RecorderToolbarWidget is the only entry point (it is what constructs ScreenRecorder, at line 640), and it is never instantiated anywhere. No module imports src.gui.screen_recorder at all. The docstring says it is "for embedding in the simulator toolbar", but the simulator tab does not reference it. Reviving it would immediately hit two latent bugs: (a) _open_folder does `Path(self._output_dir).resolve()` (line 669) with output_dir defaulting to "" -- Path("").resolve() is the process CWD, not the logs/simulator/recordings tree that ScreenRecorder.__init__ computes at lines 116-123; (b) _write_cv2 does `np.frombuffer(img.bits()).reshape(img.height(), img.width(), 3)` (line 511-512) ignoring QImage's 4-byte row padding, so any capture width not a multiple of 4 raises ValueError into a `log.debug` swallow at line 515 -- the cv2 path is the FIRST-priority engine, and the GIF path 33 lines earlier gets this right by passing img.bytesPerLine() (line 478).

**Operator sees:** None today - the feature simply does not exist in the UI despite 686 lines of engine-priority code and four capture backends. If it is re-wired, the folder button will open the wrong directory and the default cv2 engine will silently produce an empty MP4 for 3 out of 4 possible widget widths.

**Fix:** Delete the module, or wire RecorderToolbarWidget into the simulator toolbar and fix both latent bugs first: pass the recorder's resolved _out_dir back to the toolbar for _open_folder, and reshape via `np.frombuffer(img.constBits(), dtype=np.uint8).reshape(h, img.bytesPerLine())[:, :w*3].reshape(h, w, 3)`.

> **Verifier correction:** Reproduced. My grep for `screen_recorder|RecorderToolbarWidget|ScreenRecorder` across the repo excluding _archive/.session26_backups returns only in-file hits: the module docstring (line 2), class ScreenRecorder (100), a log line (147), class RecorderToolbarWidget (535) and the ScreenRecorder(...) construction at 640 inside that unused class. Lines 535-536 verbatim. Both latent bugs verified: _open_folder line 669 is `folder = str(Path(self._output_dir).resolve())` with _output_dir defaulting to "" (549/554) while ScreenRecorder.__init__ computes the real logs/simulator/recordings path at 113-123 — so Path("").resolve() yields the CWD; and _write_cv2 lines 511-512 do `np.frombuffer(img.bits(), dtype=np.uint8).reshape(img.height(), img.width(), 3)` with no bytesPerLine, swallowed by `log.debug(f"cv2 write: {e}")` at 515, while the GIF path at 478 correctly passes img.bytesPerLine(). Only correction: the file is 685 lines, not 686.

### `src/gui/simulator_tab/fleet/sim_exchange.py:169` — FleetSimExchange.step_symbol has zero callers and would desync a series from the master clock if used
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** It advances one series by ONE INDEX via `CandleSeries.step()` — precisely the index-based stepping that v3.24.3 replaced because it 'produced desynchronized playback' (master_clock.py:5-13). It does not touch `self._master_clock`, so any call permanently offsets that symbol from every other series and from the clock the controller uses as its denominator. The class docstring at lines 68-71 advertises it as a supported alternative ('# or per-symbol: ex.step_symbol("BTC/USD")'), which invites exactly the misuse the master clock was introduced to prevent.

**Operator sees:** None today — but it is a loaded gun on the public surface of the sim exchange, and the docstring recommends it. A future caller following the documented pattern silently reintroduces the 15-65 minute cross-series drift that v3.24.3 shipped to eliminate.

**Fix:** Delete `step_symbol` and the '# or per-symbol' example at sim_exchange.py:70-71. If per-symbol advancement is genuinely wanted later, route it through `MasterClock` so the clock and the series cannot diverge.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded medium -> low. The claim itself concedes 'None today', and per the downgrade rule a method that never executes is low. It is a real latent hazard with a docstring that recommends it, but nothing today can trip it.

### `src/gui/simulator_tab/nuclear_sim_exchange.py:303` — NuclearSimExchange.get_my_trades still lacks the `params` kwarg that FleetSimExchange added in v3.24.17 — TypeError on every YTD sync
`duplication` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The two sim exchanges are forks of the same ledger pattern (sim_exchange.py:16-18 says so explicitly) and they have drifted. FleetSimExchange.get_my_trades gained `params` at sim_exchange.py:467-481 with the note: 'ScrummingBot.sync_ytd_trade_count passes params={"paginate": True, "until": ...}; without this the call raised TypeError on EVERY bot on EVERY sync, so sim trade counts never populated and each run emitted 35 identical failures.' The identical call site at scrumming_bot.py:3823-3827 hits NuclearSimExchange too, because NuclearController drives a real unmodified ScrummingBot against it. The Nuclear fork never got the fix.

**Operator sees:** Once Nuclear Mode has tapes, the scout's YTD trade-count sync raises `TypeError: get_my_trades() got an unexpected keyword argument 'params'` on every sync attempt; the Nuclear panel's 'Trade count' / 'Scrum / Fold' fields stay wrong and 'Controller exceptions' climbs with no explanation of the root cause.

**Fix:** Add `params: Optional[dict] = None` to nuclear_sim_exchange.py:303-305 and `del params` in the body, mirroring sim_exchange.py:467-481. Better: pull the shared ledger/order-fill body of the two sim exchanges into one module so the next connector-signature change cannot land in only one fork.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded high -> low. The divergence is real and the TypeError is certain once reached, but the whole Nuclear path is unreachable today (no tapes; Start disabled at nuclear_mode_panel.py:258). Blast radius when armed is also small — a wrong sim trade counter, not corrupted state. Contrast claim 1, which is latent behind the same gate but writes to the operator's live on-disk registry.

### `src/stocks/stock_accumulation_bot.py:172` — StockAccumulationBot.tick() returns on line 227 forever — _market_open is never set to True by anyone
`dead-feature` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

That attribute is initialised False at line 141 ('self._market_open = False') and the only writer is set_market_open() at line 358, which has ZERO callers in the entire tree. tick() gates on it at line 227 ('if not self._check_market_hours(): return'), so every tick returns before touching a single line of accumulation logic. Separately, the comment names MarketHoursTracker as the 'actual implementation' — no class by that name exists anywhere in the repo. ### `src/stocks/stock_accumulation_bot.py:274` — StockAccumulationBot HARVEST/FOLD never place an order — self.broker is stored and never used
`dead-feature` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The constructor stores the broker at line 103 ('def __init__(self, config: StockAccumulationConfig, broker=None)' -> 'self.broker = broker') and that attribute is read NOWHERE in the 405-line file. The HARVEST branch (lines 269-283), the FOLD branch (lines 286-302) and both MR-Inspector boost branches (lines 305-333) mutate only in-process floats — self._holdings, self._fold_queue_usd, self._boost_fold_q — and then emit logger.info('HARVEST %s: %.4f shares @ $%.2f = $%.2f', ...). No await self.broker.place_order(...) anywhere, no order object, no fill reconciliation. self._holdings starts at 0.0 (line 111) and is never seeded from broker.get_position(), so even the simulated arithmetic starts from a position the bot does not own. The module header calls this a '1:1 transposition of the crypto Accumulation Bot for stock markets'; it is a detached arithmetic model with a trade-shaped log line.

**Operator sees:** get_status() (line 376) reports 'trades', 'harvests', 'folds', 'pnl', 'volume', 'win_rate' and 'unsettled' as if real equity orders had been filled at a broker. If finding #2 were fixed, this bot would produce a plausible-looking non-zero P&L dashboard while the operator's actual Alpaca account never moved a share.

**Fix:** Either make it trade — await self.broker.place_order(self.config.symbol, OrderSide.SELL, harvest_qty, ...) in the HARVEST branch and OrderSide.BUY in the FOLD branch, updating _holdings only from the returned StockOrder.filled_qty — or rename the class to StockAccumulationSimulator and change every logger.info from 'HARVEST %s' to 'SIM HARVEST %s' so no log line can be mistaken for a fill. Do not leave a class named 'AccumulationBot' with a dangling broker handle.

> **Verifier correction:** 'self.broker = broker' is at line 106, not line 105 (the def __init__ cite of 103 is correct). The anchor line 274 sits in the middle of the quoted block, which actually begins at line 272. Severity downgraded high -> low: unreachable package.

### `src/stocks/stock_accumulation_bot.py:259` — StockAccumulationBot's TA confidence is a hard-coded coin flip; harvest_threshold/fold_threshold are inert and the VotingEngine result is discarded
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** harvest_conf and fold_conf are derived solely from 'bullish' (line 248: last candle close > open) and can only ever be 0.50 or 0.0. They are compared at lines 270 and 287 against config.harvest_threshold (default 0.25) and config.fold_threshold (default 0.15), whose docstrings at lines 56-57 read 'TA confidence to harvest' / 'TA confidence to fold'. Because the left side is binary, EVERY threshold value in the open interval (0.0, 0.50] behaves identically — the operator can set harvest_threshold to 0.01 or 0.49 and observe zero change — and any value above 0.50 disables the branch outright. Meanwhile the real TA is computed and thrown away: line 253 'summary = self._voting_engine.compute_all(candles, self.config.ta_timeframe)' assigns to self._last_summary, and self._last_summary is read NOWHERE in the file. The class docstring at line 90 nevertheless claims 'Identical harvest-fold cycle as crypto' and the GUI dialog advertises a '7-indicator TA voting engine'. Compounding it, the compute_all call is wrapped in 'except Exception: pass' (lines 255-256) with no logging, so a genuine TA engine failure — bad candle shape, indicator divide-by-zero — is completely invisible to the operator on a path where it matters.

**Operator sees:** Two config knobs the operator can turn that do nothing, plus a marquee feature (the 7-indicator voting engine, shared with the crypto side) that runs, costs CPU on every tick, and has its verdict dropped on the floor. Any TA exception is swallowed with no trace.

**Fix:** Feed the summary through: 'harvest_conf = self._last_summary.sell_confidence if self._last_summary else (0.50 if bullish else 0.0)' and the mirror for fold_conf, using whatever the crypto ScrummingBot reads off VotingSummary so the two stay in sync. Replace 'except Exception: pass' at 255-256 with 'except Exception: logger.exception("TA compute_all failed for %s", self.config.symbol)'. Delete config.enable_phantoms / config.phantom_timeframes and the PhantomBalanceManager import at line 29, or wire them — self._phantoms_enabled is set and never read.

> **Verifier correction:** The class docstring line 'Identical harvest-fold cycle as crypto' is at line 89, not 90. The anchor 259 is the harvest_conf line; the quoted block starts at the comment on line 258. Severity confirmed as medium-in-principle but downgraded to low in practice: unreachable package.

### `src/stocks/stock_accumulation_bot.py:188` — PDT protection can never fire — _record_day_trade() has zero callers, so _day_trades is always empty
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** self._day_trades (initialised empty at line 142) is written to by exactly one method, _record_day_trade, which is never called from anywhere — not from the HARVEST branch, not from the FOLD branch, not from either MR-Inspector boost branch, not from any other module. _check_pdt (line 176) filters that permanently-empty list at line 181 and tests 'if len(recent) >= self.config.pdt_max_day_trades' at line 182, which is 0 >= 3, so _check_pdt returns True on every call. It is consulted as a trade gate at lines 271, 289 and 310, meaning the gate is open unconditionally. Note the semantic gap even if it were wired: _record_day_trade would need to fire only on a same-day buy-then-sell round trip, and there is no date bookkeeping anywhere in the class to detect that. The config carries three knobs for this feature (pdt_protection, pdt_max_day_trades, pdt_window_days, lines 71-73) and get_status publishes a 'day_trades_5d' field (line 399) computed from the same empty list.

**Operator sees:** get_status() reports 'day_trades_5d': 0 permanently, and the bot-creation dialog at src/gui/stock_main_window.py:571 promises the operator 'PDT protection (3 day-trades / 5 days)'. If the stocks path were ever revived against a real cash account, the operator would be told they are protected from the FINRA pattern-day-trader rule while the guard is structurally incapable of blocking anything.

**Fix:** Call _record_day_trade() from the FOLD branch (and the boosted fold-back branch) when the matching harvest happened on the same calendar date — that requires storing the harvest date alongside _fold_queue_ref, e.g. self._fold_queue_date = date.today(), and comparing it before recording. Until that exists, do not advertise the feature: strip the 'PDT protection' bullet from the stock_main_window dialog text and mark the three config fields as reserved.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded medium -> low: unreachable package (finding #1). The 'if the stocks path were ever revived' framing in the claimed effect is the accurate one.

### `src/stocks/stock_bot.py:220` — StockBot signal handling: orders are never submitted, P&L is structurally always 0, and sells are a no-op
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** Three compounding defects in the signal path. (1) StockBotStats.avg_entry_price and StockBotStats.current_position are declared at lines 80-81 and are NEVER ASSIGNED anywhere in the file — _queue_buy (line 193) does not update either. So avg_entry_price is permanently 0.0, the ternary at line 220 always takes the else branch, pnl is always 0, and the classifier at lines 233-236 ('if pnl > 0 ... elif pnl < 0') can never increment winning_trades or losing_trades. (2) Because current_position is permanently 0, line 215 'qty = signal.get("quantity", self.stats.current_position)' defaults to 0 for any alert that omits an explicit quantity, and line 217-218 'if qty <= 0: return' silently drops the sell — the common TradingView payload shape documented in the bridge's own setup instructions ({"symbol":"AAPL","action":"buy","price":{{close}}}) carries no quantity. (3) Neither _queue_buy nor _queue_sell touches self.broker (stored at line 103, read nowhere); they append a dict with status 'queued' to self._trade_history, and nothing in the file or the tree ever drains _trade_history. Additionally, handle_signal increments stats.signals_acted_on at line 191 unconditionally — even when action was neither buy/long nor sell/short/close and no branch ran — so signals_acted_on always equals signals_received.

**Operator sees:** get_status() (line 112) publishes a stats block — total_trades, total_volume, winning_trades, losing_trades, total_pnl, current_position, avg_entry_price — in which the trade and volume counters climb on every alert while P&L, win/loss and position are frozen at zero. This is the exact failure mode the operator has been burned by: a panel that renders forever-zero columns because nothing calls their setter.

**Fix:** If StockBot is kept: make _queue_buy update stats.current_position and recompute stats.avg_entry_price as a running weighted average before _queue_sell can read it, and have both methods actually await broker.place_order and reconcile from the returned StockOrder. Move the stats.signals_acted_on increment inside the two action branches at lines 187 and 189 so unrecognised actions do not inflate it. If StockBot is not kept (see finding #1 — it has no users at all, and StockAccumulationBot is the intended successor per StockBotMode.ACCUMULATION 'Core engine (transposed from crypto)'), delete the module.

> **Verifier correction:** One proof statement is imprecise: grep -rn 'StockBot' does return hits outside stock_bot.py (stock_main_window.py:65 'class StockBotTable' and :228) — those are a different symbol. The substantive point survives: grep -rn 'StockBot(' across the tree returns ZERO construction sites. Severity downgraded high -> low: unreachable package.

### `src/trading/bot_container.py:3006` — BotState.ERROR is never assigned anywhere in the codebase, so get_aggregate_stats()['errored'] is permanently zero
`dead-feature` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The error path in `_run_with_guard` (lines 1187-1215) increments the counters and, at the consecutive-error threshold, sets `self.state = BotState.COOLDOWN` — never `BotState.ERROR`. No other module assigns it either. The enum member exists (line 50) and is read in exactly one place: this comparison. `errored` is therefore always 0, and so is the `"errored"` key returned to every consumer.

**Operator sees:** Any dashboard or analytics surface reading `agg['errored']` reports zero bots in error even while bots are actively cycling through failures. The v3.16.46 comment at bot_container.py:794-798 describes this exact stat as the thing `total_errors` was added to compensate for — but the broken stat was left in the returned dict rather than removed, so a future consumer wiring it up gets a silent constant.

**Fix:** Either set `self.state = BotState.ERROR` in the exception handler at line 1188 (before the cooldown check, resetting to RUNNING on the next successful tick), or drop `BotState.ERROR` and the `errored` key entirely and let `total_errors_lifetime` / `consecutive_errors` be the single source of truth. Setting the state is preferable — `BotState.ERROR` is part of the documented state machine in the module header (lines 17-21).

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded medium -> low, and the claimed effect is wrong. The claim says 'Any dashboard or analytics surface reading agg["errored"] reports zero' — I grepped and NO consumer reads that key today. src/gui/main_window.py:4746 and :5434 call get_aggregate_stats() but read 'total_scrummed/folded' and 'total_errors_lifetime' respectively; src/trading/analytics_engine.py:126 reads 'total_realised_pnl' and 'running'. So this is the identical latent-trap class as claim [11], not an active misreport. The scanner's own claimed_effect overstates it.

### `src/trading/bot_container.py:764` — BotStats.extended_positions_created is exposed in the public status dict as 'extended_positions' but is never incremented
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Operator sees:** Low today — nothing renders the key — but it is a live trap: it sits inside the `stats` sub-dict that the bot table and any future status consumer iterate, so the next surface that adds an 'Extended positions' column gets a permanent zero with no compile-time or test signal.

**Fix:** Delete `extended_positions_created` from BotStats and remove the `"extended_positions"` key from `get_status()`, in the same sweep as the two `accumulated_*` fields above. If any persisted bot_state.json carries the key, the existing `setattr`-guarded stats restore loop (bot_container.py:2745-2747, `if hasattr(bot.stats, key)`) already ignores unknown keys, so no migration is needed.

> **Verifier correction:** None. The claim is accurate and already self-rated low, which is the right severity — no consumer exists, so today it is purely a latent trap.

### `src/trading/gate_coverage.py:298` — gate_coverage classify_trades computes _in_log_gap and throws the answer away — both branches assign LOG_GAP
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The elif and the else assign the identical status, so the _in_log_gap() call is a pure no-op — its return value cannot change the outcome, and gap_threshold_s (LOG_GAP_THRESHOLD_S = 1800.0) has no effect on any classification. _in_log_gap is defined at line 220 and called nowhere else. The module docstring (lines 38-43) defines LOG_GAP specifically as 'trade falls between two gate entries that are far enough apart that the log was not being written then', a distinction the code no longer draws: a trade that merely fell outside the ±300s tolerance while the log WAS being written is reported identically to one in a genuine 30-minute writer stall.

**Operator sees:** format_coverage_lines() (line 336) labels every non-paired, non-pre-logging trade as 'fall in a gate-log gap (app down or writer stalled)'. Given the known 2026-06-11 gate.log writer stall this is precisely the count the operator would use to size the stall, and it is inflated by every near-miss tolerance failure.

**Fix:** Introduce the missing fourth status (e.g. GateStatus.NO_MATCH_IN_WINDOW = 'no_gate_within_tolerance') for the else branch and give it its own label in the `label` dict at line 331, so _in_log_gap's answer actually partitions the two causes. Add a unit test pinning that a trade 400s from a dense run of gate entries is NOT reported as LOG_GAP.

> **Verifier correction:** Line-exact and verbatim. I counted from 285: :296 `elif ts < earliest:`, :297 BEFORE_LOGGING, :298 `elif _in_log_gap(candidates, ts, gap_threshold_s):`, :299 LOG_GAP, :300 `else:`, :301 LOG_GAP. Both branches do assign the identical status, so the call is a pure no-op and gap_threshold_s cannot influence any classification. My own repo-wide grep for `_in_log_gap` returns exactly two hits: gate_coverage.py:220 (def) and :298 (the discarded call).

### `src/trading/scrumming_bot.py:3581` — import_scrumming_state drift check is structurally unreachable and its docstring promises a warning that can never fire
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** MEM-254 removed `current_holdings` from persistence — `export_scrumming_state` no longer writes it (see the comment at 3253-3254 'MEM-254: current_holdings REMOVED from persistence') and `import_scrumming_state` explicitly does not restore it (comment at 3418-3424: 'Leaving self._current_holdings at its default (0.0 from __init__) here'). Nothing between `__init__` and this line assigns it. Therefore `self._current_holdings` is always exactly 0.0 when line 3581 evaluates, the condition is always False, and the entire 3581-3598 drift-warning block is dead. The method docstring at 3335-3338 still advertises it: 'Logs a warning if main_lots.sum(units) disagrees with the saved current_holdings by more than 0.1%'. The trailing comment at 3603 also still says 'Saved _current_holdings becomes our starting assumption'.

**Operator sees:** The 'STATE RESTORE WARNING: main_lots total X drifts Y% from saved holdings' message can never appear. An operator debugging a restore problem who greps for it, or who reads the docstring and concludes the restore path validates lots against holdings, is misled — there is no such validation. Corrupted `main_lots` passes through restore silently and becomes authoritative at the init handshake (line 4702).

**Fix:** Either delete lines 3577-3598 and correct the docstring (3335-3339) and the trailing comment (3600-3604), or make the check meaningful by comparing `lots_sum` against the saved-but-currently-ignored `data.get('current_holdings')` when present, purely as a forensic signal.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded medium -> low: no runtime or data consequence, purely a dead branch plus a misleading docstring and trailing comment.

### `src/trading/scrumming_bot.py:3779` — YTD_TRADE_MAX_PAGES is declared as the safety cap but the pagination loop hardcodes 12
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The constant is never read by any code. The v3.23.58 rewrite replaced cursor pagination with a 30-day-window walk and hardcoded the bound inline: line 3820 `while _cursor < _now and _windows < 12:  # 12*30d = 360d`. The constant was left behind with a comment ('20k-trade ceiling, safety cap') describing a bound that no longer governs anything, and its stated meaning (pages x 500 trades) no longer matches the window-walk model at all.

**Operator sees:** An operator or developer tuning YTD reconciliation for a high-frequency bot will raise YTD_TRADE_MAX_PAGES and see zero effect — the walk still stops at 12 windows (360 days). Any bot whose real history predates the 360-day window silently under-counts, and the class constant that looks like the knob is inert. Low blast radius because the counter is floored by `max(_persisted, _prev_exc, _count)` at 3880, but the tuning surface is a lie.

**Fix:** Either use it — `_max_windows = int(getattr(self,'YTD_TRADE_MAX_PAGES', 12) or 12)` and `while _cursor < _now and _windows < _max_windows:` — renaming it to `YTD_TRADE_MAX_WINDOWS` with a comment that matches the 30-day-window model, or delete the constant and the two test references and leave the literal 12 with its existing inline comment.

> **Verifier correction:** None. Reproduced exactly, including the sibling-constant contrast.

### `src/trading/stone_tablets/fetcher.py:656` — Every CLI flag except the subcommand name is parsed and thrown away
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

### `src/trading/stone_tablets/fetcher.py:187` — CoinGeckoAdapter is a hard-coded failure stub but is still handed out by _build_adapter and advertised as a working fallback
`dead-feature` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

The module docstring at line 17 bills it as "CoinGeckoAdapter — recent-only fallback (free-tier limits)", contradicting the body. The error text also names v3.23.99 while the tree is at v3.24.19.

### `src/trading/stone_tablets/registry.py:241` — has_coverage and missing_ranges accept a `timeframe` argument their bodies never reference
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict PARTIAL

**Broken:** `missing_ranges` uses `step_ms = _TF_SECONDS[NATIVE_TIMEFRAME] * 1000` (registry.py:250) and `_native_ts_set` (271) — `timeframe` is never read. Neither validates the argument against `_TF_SECONDS`, unlike `get_candles` which raises ValueError on an unsupported timeframe (registry.py:292-295) — so `missing_ranges(asset, a, b, "7m")` is accepted without complaint.

(1) The claim says "missing_ranges' docstring does not [hint at it]" — FALSE. registry.py:246-247 reads 'Return list of [start_ms, end_ms] gaps in the requested window at 5m granularity.' It explicitly documents the 5m behaviour. (2) No caller can currently be misled: I grepped has_coverage across src/, tools/, main.py and it has ZERO callers anywhere, and missing_ranges' sole caller is the dead GapFiller (fetcher.py:233), which passes NATIVE_TIMEFRAME explicitly.

### `src/trading/stone_tablets/storage.py:118` — listed_at_ms is a pure alias of first_ts_ms and can never carry distinct information; both listed_at_iso properties have zero callers
`scaffolding` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The v3.24.6 comment at storage.py:71-78 sells the field as a distinct platform-level listing marker — "for assets Coinbase listed AFTER YTD_START, this is the actual listing date". But `Tablet.listed_at_ms` returns `first_ts_ms` unconditionally, `entry_from_tablet` copies that value into the manifest (storage.py:326), and `read_manifest` falls back to `first_ts_ms` when the key is absent (storage.py:275). There is no code path in which the two values can differ, so the field costs a schema slot and buys nothing. Worse, it is per-YEAR: for a multi-year asset the 2026 tablet's `listed_at_ms` is 2026-01-01 regardless of the real listing date. Separately, `TabletEntry.listed_at_iso` (storage.py:82) and `Tablet.listed_at_iso` (storage.py:126) have no callers anywhere — not in src, not in tools, not in tests.

**Operator sees:** Anyone reading the code believes there is a real listing-date marker to consult, and any future correct listing date written into `Tablet` would be overwritten by the alias on the next persist. The two dead `listed_at_iso` helpers give a false impression that the storage layer formats listing dates.

**Fix:** Either make `listed_at_ms` a real stored field on `Tablet` (settable, defaulting to first_ts_ms, and aggregated across years at write time) or delete it and the two unused `listed_at_iso` properties and let `AvailabilityInfo` — which correctly takes `min()` across year tablets at registry.py:425-427 — be the single source of listing truth.

> **Verifier correction:** The cited line 118 is the `def listed_at_ms` line; the quoted block opens with `@property` at line 117. Severity medium -> low: this is a documentation/schema smell with no behavioural consequence today, since every consumer already takes the first_ts_ms fallback and gets the correct value.

### `tools/harness/gui_archetype.py:539` — Bandit B101 test-file suppression forked into coding_archetype only — gui_archetype drifted
`duplication` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `_BANDIT_SEVERITY_OVERRIDES` is copy-pasted into two modules — coding_archetype.py:115-128 and gui_archetype.py:380-384 — both mapping B101 to 'high'. In v3.24.5 the coding copy gained a guard (coding_archetype.py:427 `_is_test_file = "/tests/" in str(target).replace("\\\\", "/")` and lines 430-431 `if _is_test_file and rule_id == "B101": continue`) because pytest uses bare `assert` as its native assertion syntax. That guard was never applied to the gui copy. The two forks have now drifted: reviewing a GUI test file yields opposite verdicts depending on which archetype you invoke.

**Operator sees:** Running the GUI archetype over any pytest file under tests/ that exercises widgets escalates every `assert` to HIGH, driving `passed` to False and failing the gate — while running the coding archetype over the same file passes. Fixing the rule in one place leaves the other wrong, which is exactly the trap the duplication creates.

**Fix:** Extract `_BANDIT_SEVERITY_OVERRIDES` and the entire `_run_bandit` body into a shared helper (e.g. tools/harness/rules/_bandit.py) that both archetypes import, so the B101 test-file suppression and any future override live in exactly one place.

> **Verifier correction:** One factual overstatement: the two _BANDIT_SEVERITY_OVERRIDES dicts are NOT 'byte-identical'. coding_archetype.py:115-128 is one key per line with an explanatory comment on each; gui_archetype.py:380-384 is a compact 4-line literal with no comments. They map the same 12 rule ids to 'high', so they are semantically identical — the fork conclusion holds, the 'byte-identical' phrasing does not. Severity downgraded medium -> low: the release gate never runs the GUI archetype over tests/ (check_release_readiness.py:95 points GUIArchetype at the single fixture docs/audits/2026-07-24_gui_docs_archetypes/gui_fixtures/known_good_widget.py), so this can only bite an ad-hoc review a human initiates.

## P3 — MISLEADING DOCS  (22)

### `src/gui/bot_live_settings.py:2018` — "Aggressive Trading" checkbox promises IOC-limit takers on every order, but _execute_buy/_execute_sell never read the flag
`stale-doc` · **high** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The claimed Sub-phase 2D wiring never landed. `_aggressive` is consulted in exactly ONE execution site — the Stack-tranche placement path — and nowhere in the two primary order paths. `ScrummingBot._execute_sell` (scrumming_bot.py:9769) picks its order type at lines 9995-10001 and `ScrummingBot._execute_buy` (scrumming_bot.py:10179) at lines 10599-10605; both use the identical block `if self._invisible: ot = OrderType.MARKET ... else: ot = OrderType.LIMIT` with no reference to `self._aggressive`. Every ordinary SCRUM and FOLD therefore ignores the flag.

**Operator sees:** An operator who ticks "Aggressive Trading" on a live bot to guarantee immediate fills gets passive LIMIT orders on every scrum and fold (or MARKET when the bot is in Internal/invisible mode). Orders they believe are crossing the spread instead rest on the book and may never fill — real money, real missed cycles, with the GUI actively asserting the opposite.

**Fix:** Either (a) low-risk: rewrite the tooltip and comment at bot_live_settings.py:2013-2027 and bot_wizard.py:615-620 to state truthfully that the flag currently only affects Stack-Mode tranche placement; or (b) implement the promised behaviour by threading `self._aggressive` into the order-type selection in `_execute_sell` (scrumming_bot.py:9995-10001) and `_execute_buy` (scrumming_bot.py:10599-10605), mirroring line 9592. Option (b) changes live order routing and needs a gated release.

> **Verifier correction:** One proof detail is misattributed: the single IOC_LIMIT site at scrumming_bot.py:9592 lives in `_open_stack_from_scrum` (def at 9539), not `_reconcile_stack_tranches_invisible` (9722). Same conclusion — stack-tranche placement only. Also the order-type blocks start one line later than cited: 9996-10001 and 10600-10605, not 9995/10599.

### `main.py:555` — Boot log and Qt application version hardcoded to 3.1.26 while the app is 3.24.19
`stale-doc` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** main.py:540 and main.py:555 hardcode the literal '3.1.26'. Twenty-three lines later, main.py:612 sets `current_version = "3.24.19"`, which matches src/__init__.py:15 `__version__ = "3.24.19"`. So the file carries two mutually contradictory version constants and the WRONG one is what gets written to the operator's log stream as the first line of every run. The release-readiness gate does not catch this: `check_release_readiness._read_version()` (line 53-59) reads only src/__init__.py and never compares against main.py, despite main.py's own header at lines 10-11 declaring 'current_version string here must match src/__init__.py __version__'.

**Operator sees:** The first line of every session's log reads 'Acervator v3.1.26 starting'. This is the exact failure mode that cost three sessions in the v3.15.97 stale-binary incident — the operator believing they were running a version they were not. Anyone diagnosing from logs is told the running build is 3.1.26.

**Fix:** Import the single source of truth and use it: `from src import __version__` (already imported inside SplashScreen.paintEvent at line 778) and replace both literals with it, plus set `current_version = __version__` at line 612. Then add a main.py-vs-src/__init__.py consistency assertion to check_release_readiness._read_version so the documented invariant is actually gated.

> **Verifier correction:** One supporting proof is stated in a way that will mislead a reader who re-runs it. The claim says `grep -rn applicationVersion --include=*.py .` returns ZERO consumers. That grep is case-sensitive and does NOT match the setter `setApplicationVersion` either (capital A) — so a reader re-running it will wonder why main.py:555 is missing. The conclusion is nonetheless correct: I ran a case-insensitive tree-wide walk over every .py file and the ONLY hit anywhere is main.py:555 (the setter). There is no reader of Qt's applicationVersion in the codebase, so that value is inert. The log line at 540 is the part with real operator impact.
