### `src/core/state_manager.py:92` — StateManager.save_state docstring promises to preserve existing smart wires; the code overwrites them with []
`stale-doc` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The parameter docstring at lines 74-75 states 'When None, the current file's existing wires field (if any) is preserved.' No read-modify-write happens anywhere in `save_state` — the on-disk file is never consulted, and `list(None or [])` is `[]`. The same falsy-collapse means an empty list from a failed export also wipes the field. In `BotContainer.save_all_state` (src/trading/bot_container.py:2334-2356) `wires` and `ledgers` are initialised to `[]` and only assigned inside try blocks whose `except` merely logs a warning — so any exception from `export_wires()`/`export_ledgers()` results in a save that erases the persisted Smart Wire topology and lifetime wired_in/wired_out ledgers.

**Operator sees:** This is the exact operator-reported bug the v3.16.57 comment cites ('Smart Wire credits are not persisting across platform restarts'). A single transient failure inside SmartWireManager.export_ledgers during shutdown silently zeroes lifetime wired_in/wired_out totals on the next save, and the docstring tells the next reader that cannot happen.

**Fix:** Make the docstring true: when `smart_wires` / `smart_wire_ledgers` is None, load the existing file and carry the prior field forward. Separately, in bot_container.save_all_state, pass `None` (not `[]`) when an export raises, so a failed export preserves rather than destroys the last good snapshot.

> **Verifier correction:** The defect is real and the quote is verbatim, but the LINE NUMBER IS OFF BY ONE. Line 92 is the comment `# v3.15.68 — Bot Swarm state preservation`; the quoted block is lines 93-95 (`"smart_wires": list(smart_wires or []),` at 93, the v3.16.57 comment at 94, `"smart_wire_ledgers": list(smart_wire_ledgers or []),` at 95). The operator clicks these, so it should be cited as state_manager.py:93. Everything else checks out: the docstring promise 'When None, the current / file's existing wires field (if any) is preserved.' is at lines 74-75; `state = {...}` at 86-96 is built from scratch and `self._path` is only touched at 104 (tmp suffix), 109/111 (backup read), and 114 (atomic replace) — never read to merge, so no caller can ever get the documented preserve behavior. bot_container.py: `wires = []` is at 2335 and `ledgers: list = []` at 2336 (the claim's 2334-2356 range is right, its endpoints are one line loose), and both `except Exception as exc:` branches call only `logger.warning(...)`. I also confirmed `save_all_state` at 2355 is the ONLY caller of save_state repo-wide, and it always passes both kwargs explicitly — so the None-branch in the docstring is unreachable and the live hazard is specifically the `[]`-on-exception path the claim describes.

### `src/gui/market_inspector.py:322` — Market Inspector status line has no branch for the only source the fetcher emits, so a successful scan reports "No data yet"
`stale-doc` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

The current fetcher, market_inspector_fetcher.fetch_htf_universe, only ever sets meta["source"] to "exchange" (lines 282 and 294) or "no-exchange" (line 232). **Operator sees:** The operator presses Refresh, waits the 10-30 s the tooltip warns about, watches the HTF Signals and Opposing Pairs tables fill with real rows -- and the status label reads "No data yet — press Refresh." The market count, elapsed time and any partial-connector error string (combined_error) are never surfaced.

**Fix:** Add an "exchange" branch that renders symbol_count, elapsed_seconds and the optional error tail, e.g. `if src == "exchange": return f"Exchange scan  ·  {n} markets  ·  {meta.get('elapsed_seconds', 0):.0f}s" + (f"  ·  partial: {err}" if err else "")`. Delete the dead coingecko/network-partial branches.

`stale-doc` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

### `src/competition/token_ledger.py:67` — TokenLedger class docstring advertises a remaining_supply() method that does not exist
`stale-doc` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The class docstring enumerates TokenLedger's public API. Three of the four names resolve; remaining_supply() does not exist on this class. ### `src/core/privacy_mask_registry.py:290` — privacy_mask_registry: set_masked accepts new field ids but mask_or refuses to mask them — a newly wired field silently leaks
`stale-doc` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** The class docstring at lines 136-137 promises the opposite: '`set_masked` records the value even for unknown ids so a wired-up call site that uses a new id starts working without a separate registration step.' It does record the value, and `is_masked` returns it — but `mask_or`, which is how every render site actually applies the mask (main_window.py, indicator_panel.py, bot_visualizer.py), hard short-circuits on any id not in `ALL_FIELD_IDS`. So a new field id set via `set_masked` renders unmasked forever. The same file's count comments are stale in three places (line 52 'Exactly 18 entries', line 137 'set_all only touches the 18 canonical ids', line 169 'The 18 canonical ids') while the assert at line 101 pins 19.

**Operator sees:** A developer adds a privacy dot for a new field, calls `mask_or(value, "newfield.x")`, sees `set_masked` accept the id and the dot turn red — and the value stays visible on screen. The failure mode of a privacy control is exactly the one that must never fail open, and the docstring actively points the next reader at the wrong mental model.

**Fix:** Pick one contract and make both halves agree. Either have `set_masked` raise/log on unknown ids (making registration mandatory, matching mask_or), or have `mask_or` consult `reg.is_masked(field_id)` for any id not in the explicit `TA_FIELD_IDS_EXCLUDED` denylist — keeping the TA leak-guard as a denylist rather than an allowlist. Also fix the three '18' comments to 19.

> **Verifier correction:** Line and quote exact (290-293), and the contradiction is real. Docstring lines 135-137 do promise '``set_masked`` records the value even for unknown ids so a wired-up / call site that uses a new id starts working without a separate / registration step.' `set_masked` at 154-158 writes `self._mask_state[field_id]` unconditionally; `mask_or` at 290-293 returns `str(value)` before consulting the registry. The stale count comments are confirmed at line 52 ('Exactly 18 entries'), 137 ('set_all only touches the 18 canonical ids'), 169 ('The 18 canonical ids') against `assert len(ALL_FIELD_IDS) == 19` at 101. All 14 mask_or call sites the claim lists are exact (main_window.py:469/492/511/914/918/921/924/926, indicator_panel.py:771/957, bot_visualizer.py:685/704/884/885). TWO THINGS THE SCANNER MISSED, both severity-reducing: (1) mask_or's OWN docstring at 286-288 documents the short-circuit as the deliberate TA-leak-guard contract, so the contradiction is between two docstrings, and the class docstring at 135-137 is the wrong one — the fix is to correct that sentence, not to change mask_or. (2) There is a second render path: several sites call `reg.is_masked(...)` directly (bot_visualizer.py:2235/2263, indicator_panel.py:754, main_window.py:638/1431/2528) and mask themselves, which WOULD honor a new id — so 'renders unmasked forever' is only true for the mask_or path.

`stale-doc` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

### `src/trading/mr_inspector.py:16` — MRInspector header contradicts its own gate thresholds and inverts the boost-size rule
`stale-doc` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** Three contradictions with the code below it. (1) '30-candle SMA' — scan() uses a 20-candle window: `closes = [c.close for c in candles[-20:]]` then `sma = sum(closes) / 20` (lines 132-135). (2) 'BB position > 0.85' — the code tests 0.80: `at_upper = z > self._z_threshold and bb_pos > 0.80` (line 156). (3) The boost-size rule is inverted: the header says all three gates passing yields a 2.5% sell, but 2.5% (`rec_pct = self._base_pct * 0.5` with base_boost_pct=0.05) is the branch taken when tightening is NOT confirmed (lines 188-189); with all three gates confirmed rec_pct ranges 5%–20% (line 184). A fourth inconsistency sits inside the header itself: the architecture block at line 28 says 'Layer 1: z-score > 2σ' while the note at line 13 and the constructor default both say 1.8.

**Operator sees:** An operator or maintainer tuning the mean-reversion boost from the header will set thresholds against numbers the code does not use, and will expect a confirmed triple-gate signal to sell 2.5% when it actually sells up to 20% of holdings — an 8x sizing surprise on a live equity accumulation bot.

**Fix:** Rewrite the AI DEVELOPER NOTE and the architecture block to match scan(): 20-candle SMA, bb_pos > 0.80 / < 0.20, z threshold 1.8 in both places, and state the sizing correctly (tightening confirmed -> 5%–20% scaled by strength; unconfirmed -> 2.5%). Pin the thresholds with a test so the header and the constants cannot drift again.

> **Verifier correction:** All four contradictions verified line-by-line, header against code. Header: :12 'Gate 1: z-score > 1.8σ above 30-candle SMA', :13 'Gate 2: BB position > 0.85 (near upper band)', :16 'When all 3 gates pass → Boost Sell (sell 2.5% of holdings)' — cited line 16 is exact and is the sharpest contradiction. Code: :132 `closes = [c.close for c in candles[-20:]]` and :135 `sma = sum(closes) / 20` (20-candle, not 30); :156 `at_upper = z > self._z_threshold and bb_pos > 0.80` (0.80, not 0.85); :182-184 tightening_confirmed → `rec_pct = self._base_pct + (self._max_pct - self._base_pct) * strength`, which with :101-102 defaults base_boost_pct=0.05 / max_boost_pct=0.20 spans 5%–20%, while :188-189 the UNconfirmed branch is `rec_pct = self._base_pct * 0.5  # Only 2.5% without confirmation`. The rule is inverted exactly as claimed. Internal header inconsistency also real: :28 'Layer 1: z-score > 2σ' vs the 1.8 at :12 and the constructor default at :101 (the claim said 'the note at line 13' — it is line 12, off by one). Live consumer confirmed: stock_accumulation_bot.py:135 `self._mr_inspector = MRInspector()`.

## P4 — HARDENING  (11)

### `main.py:124` — Stale-binary guard raises NameError at import time — 'guard must NEVER raise' is false
`error-handling` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `_check_stale_dist_binary()` is invoked at module scope on main.py:130, but the module-level `logger` name is not bound until main.py:225 (`logger = logging.getLogger("acervator")`). Every one of the function's three except handlers (lines 89, 121, 124) calls `logger.debug(...)`. Any exception inside the try therefore triggers a NameError raised FROM INSIDE the except handler, which propagates straight out of the 'never raises' guard to unguarded module scope at line 130 — killing the import. Reachable triggers: `sys.stderr.write(msg)` on line 111 when sys.stderr is None (pythonw.exe / PyInstaller --windowed), a UnicodeDecodeError or PermissionError while reading either __init__.py, or a failed marker write at line 118.

**Operator sees:** Acervator refuses to start at all, with a NameError traceback that names `logger` — pointing the operator at logging when the real problem is a stale dist/ tree or a windowed-mode stderr. This is precisely the diagnostic-blindness scenario the v3.15.97 guard was written to prevent, and it fires hardest under `pythonw`/frozen-windowed launches where stderr is None.

**Fix:** Replace the three `logger.debug(...)` calls inside `_check_stale_dist_binary` (lines 89, 121, 124) with `pass` or `print(..., file=sys.stderr)` guarded by `if sys.stderr:`, and additionally guard line 111 with `if sys.stderr is not None:`. Alternatively move the `logger = logging.getLogger("acervator")` assignment above line 130.

> **Verifier correction:** The mechanism is real and I reproduced the escaping NameError, but the claimed effect ('Acervator refuses to start at all', 'fires hardest under pythonw/frozen-windowed launches') is materially overstated. Lines 78-79 of the guard are `if not os.path.isfile(live_init) or not os.path.isfile(dist_init): return` — so nothing after that point executes unless dist/Acervator/_internal/src/__init__.py exists. It does NOT exist in this tree (`ls dist/Acervator` -> No such file or directory; dist/ contains only sadp_harness wheels/tarballs). I ran the extracted function against the real repo and it returned cleanly. Under a PyInstaller --windowed frozen launch __file__ points inside the bundle, so dist_init won't resolve there either — that specific 'fires hardest' trigger is largely unreachable. The live scenario is: a dev tree that HAS a stale dist/ build (the operator does produce these via build_windows.ps1) launched under pythonw, or an unreadable src/__init__.py. Latent, not currently firing. Downgraded critical -> medium.

### `src/gui/start_all_progress_dialog.py:108` — Start-All progress dialog reads event.payload; Event only has .data, so the dialog is completely inert
`silent-failure` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** src/core/event_bus.py:35-47 defines `Event` with fields topic/timestamp/`data` and a `__getattr__` that resolves unknown attributes out of `self.data`, raising AttributeError when the key is absent. EventBus.emit (line 129-136) builds `Event(topic=topic, data=kwargs)`, and BotManager.start_all emits `phase=`, `total=`, `started=`, `bot_id=` as kwargs -- so there is never a `payload` key. `event.payload` therefore raises AttributeError("Event has no data field 'payload'") on the very first event, and the handler's `except Exception: pass` at lines 113-114 discards it. _handle_progress_main_thread is never reached for any phase.

**Operator sees:** Clicking Start All pops the dialog, which sits at "Preparing to auto-start bots..." with an empty list and a disabled Close button for the entire staggered start, then never auto-dismisses -- the operator has to close it manually and gets zero progress feedback. This is exactly the feature the 2026-04-28 operator directive asked for.

**Fix:** Change the four `event.payload.get(...)` calls to `event.data.get(...)`, and replace the bare `except Exception: pass` at 113-114 with a logger.exception so a future schema drift is visible instead of silent.

> **Verifier correction:** Everything reproduces. src/core/event_bus.py:34-48 defines Event with fields topic/timestamp/data and a __getattr__ that raises AttributeError(f"Event has no data field '{name}'") for any key not in self.data; emit() at line 136 builds `Event(topic=topic, data=kwargs)`. All six emits in BotManager.start_all (bot_container.py:2858-2909) pass phase/total/started/bot_id (+timeout_seconds) as kwargs, never `payload`. So line 108 raises on the first event and the bare `except Exception: pass` at 113-114 discards it; _handle_progress_main_thread is unreachable. The dialog IS on a live path — main_window.py:6302 (claim said 6303, off by one) constructs and shows it from the start_all command. Confirmed the headline default 'Preparing to auto-start bots...' (line 53) and _close_btn.setEnabled(False) (line 81), so the described stuck state is accurate.

### `src/trading/bot_container.py:2802` — restore_bots_from_state ignores register()'s refusal tuple — refused bots are counted and reported to the operator as "Restored"
`error-handling` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `register()` returns `(granted, refusal_reason)` and, on a CapitalRegistry over-allocation refusal, explicitly does NOT add the bot to `self._bots` (bot_container.py:2001-2007: emits `bot.register_refused` and `return False, reason` before the `self._bots[bot.bot_id] = bot` line at 2017). The restore path discards the return value entirely, then unconditionally appends `bid` to `restored` and logs a success line. The other production caller does check it — src/gui/main_window.py:6824-6837 unpacks the tuple and aborts bot creation with an operator-visible error on refusal — so this call site is the outlier, not the norm.

**Operator sees:** On a launch where saved bots exceed the rehydrated CapitalRegistry allocation, the startup status log says "Restored 12 bot(s) from previous session" while only, say, 9 exist in the manager. The missing bots never appear in the bot table and never trade; the operator's only clue is a WARNING-level `bot.register_refused` event with no correlation to the restore count. The subsequent auto-restart pass (`start_all(eligible_filter=...)`) silently has nothing to start for them.

**Fix:** Capture the tuple: `granted, reason = self.register(bot)`; on `not granted`, log an ERROR naming `bid`, `config.symbol` and `reason`, and `continue` without appending to `restored`. Optionally return a second list of refusals so main.py can surface "N bot(s) refused: <reasons>" alongside the restored count.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded high -> medium. Correct in every particular, but it only fires when the rehydrated CapitalRegistry actually refuses (over-allocation at restore time), and register() wraps the whole registry consult in a try/except that falls through to success on any registry exception (lines 2008-2015) — so the refusal branch is narrow, not a routine path.

### `src/trading/bot_container.py:927` — One transient get_markets() failure permanently disables the pre-flight order guard for that symbol; docstring claims the negative cache is brief and invalidatable
`silent-failure` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** There is no TTL on the cache entry and no invalidation method anywhere on the class, so "briefly" is actually "for the entire lifetime of the bot object". Once `(0.0, 0.0, 8)` is stored, the guards at lines 1002 (`if _min_amount > 0 and ...`) and 1009 (`if _min_cost > 0 and ...`) are both short-circuited forever for that symbol, and every subsequent order goes to the exchange unchecked. The failure is logged at DEBUG only, so the operator never sees it. The __init__ comment at lines 873-879 compounds the contradiction: "Lazy-loaded from exchange.get_markets() on first need; refreshed only when explicitly invalidated" — nothing in the codebase ever invalidates it.

**Operator sees:** If `get_markets()` fails once during startup (rate-limit burst during a 35-bot staggered start is the documented failure pattern — see start_all's v3.23.86 note about ~3 initial start failures per boot), that bot spends the rest of the session re-sending under-minimum orders to the exchange and getting them rejected server-side. That is precisely the API-spam the v3.15.78 pre-flight guard was built to stop per the operator directive quoted in the docstring ("We should not be spamming the APIs with improperly valued orders"), and the operator sees only generic 'SELL FAILED' / 'BUY FAILED' lines with no PRE-FLIGHT REJECTED prefix to distinguish them.

**Fix:** Store `(limits, timestamp)` and treat a negative-cached entry as expired after a short TTL (30-60s) so the next order retries the fetch; keep positive entries long-lived. Raise the failure log from `logger.debug` to `logger.warning` so a persistent markets-fetch outage is visible. Optionally add an `invalidate_market_limits(symbol=None)` method so the comment at lines 873-879 becomes true.

> **Verifier correction:** Two corrections to the impact story, neither fatal to the finding. (1) 'Permanently' means for the lifetime of that BotContainer INSTANCE — _market_limits_cache is per-bot, not module-level, so a bot stop/start cycle that rebuilds the object clears it; the claim's own body says this correctly but the title overstates. (2) The min_cost guard at line 1009 is ALREADY inert for the Extractor and Scrumming market-order paths regardless of this bug, because it is gated on `price is not None` and every market-order call site passes price=None (extractor_bot.py:1384, 1023, 1223). So the only guard this defect actually disables on those paths is the min_amount check at line 1002.

### `src/trading/scrumming_bot.py:8456` — DIST balance clamp compares a float to a Balance object — TypeError swallowed every time
`silent-failure` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED · shared-with-live

**Broken:** `bal` is the return of `self._get_balance(...)` which is an `exchange.base.Balance` dataclass (declared @dataclass with no order=True, so no __lt__), not a float. Every other read in this file does `float(getattr(_bal, 'free', 0) or 0)`. `min(float, Balance)` raises TypeError, which is caught by the bare `except Exception: pass` on the very next line (8457-8458, annotated 'R28-OK: best-effort optional update'). The clamp therefore never applies — dist_asset always stays at the full `_dist_accumulator` value.

**Operator sees:** The guard that is supposed to stop DIST from selling more of the asset than the exchange actually holds does nothing. This is exactly the MEM-235 failure class documented in the comment block at lines 8232-8265 (operator's BONK bot repeatedly hitting Coinbase INSUFFICIENT_FUND on DIST sells). Whenever `_dist_accumulator` drifts above the real balance, DIST fires an oversized sell, the exchange rejects it, and the operator sees repeated 'DIST ABORTED' with no explanation of the real cause.

**Fix:** Change to `_bal_free = float(getattr(bal, 'free', 0) or 0); dist_asset = min(dist_asset, _bal_free)`. Narrow the except to `(TypeError, ValueError, AttributeError)` and log at WARNING with the exception type rather than `pass`, so a future shape change surfaces instead of silently disabling the clamp.

> **Verifier correction:** The effect narrative overstates the blast radius. The MEM-235 INSUFFICIENT_FUND incident documented at 8232-8265 was root-caused and fixed at the accumulator formula itself (extra_asset instead of whole-above-target excess — a stated 551x reduction), not at this clamp. This clamp is defense-in-depth that never fires; it is not the cause of the operator's BONK incident. Downgraded high -> medium accordingly.

### `tools/harness/check_release_readiness.py:193` — Release gate prints the green banner and writes a green sidecar with every check skipped
`silent-failure` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `--no-pytest`, `--no-archetypes` and `--no-claims` short-circuit all three checks (lines 165, 173, 180). `failures` stays empty, so control reaches line 193 and emits the identical `[OK] Release-ready (vX.Y.Z, N tests)` string the operator's discipline treats as authorization to bump banners. The sidecar payload (lines 133-138) records only version/tests/timestamp/generator — it carries NO field saying which checks were skipped. The consuming PreToolUse hook .claude/hooks/verify_release_gate.py reads only `timestamp` freshness (lines 67-81) and never inspects `tests`, so a `tests: 0` sidecar unblocks Write/Edit on src/__init__.py and main.py exactly like a real 875-test pass.

**Operator sees:** The operator's stated hard rule ('no banner without a green gate' — added after shipping 3.20.35 banners on an 18-failure suite) is bypassable by a single documented flag, with no on-screen warning and no forensic trace in the sidecar. A later reader of .release_ready.json cannot tell a real pass from a skipped one.

**Fix:** When any --no-* flag is set, refuse to write the sidecar and print `[SKIP]`/`[PARTIAL]` instead of `[OK] Release-ready`. If a sidecar must still be written for dev flow, add a `"checks_skipped": [...]` field and make verify_release_gate.py deny when it is non-empty. Also correct the docstring's exit-2 claim on line 17.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded high -> medium: the hole requires the operator to deliberately pass three documented dev-only flags, so it is not a silent-by-default failure. The forensic gap (sidecar records no skip state) is real and is the part worth fixing.

### `tools/harness/coding_archetype.py:213` — Archetypes report uninstalled static-analysis tools as 'ok' — release gate passes vacuously
`silent-failure` · **medium** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** The 'missing' branch can never fire for the four tools invoked as `[sys.executable, "-m", "<tool>", ...]` (ruff line 293, mypy line 342, bandit line 408, vulture line 448). `sys.executable` always exists, so subprocess.run returns normally with returncode 1 and EMPTY stdout when the module is absent — the diagnostic goes to stderr, which is never inspected. Each runner then hits its `if not proc.stdout.strip(): return findings, "ok"` early-out (line 298 for ruff) or an empty parse loop, and reports status "ok" with zero findings. The module docstring's FALSIFICATION clause claims '(a) any tool is not installed (report shows "missing")' — that is false. The same pattern exists in gui_archetype.py:495/528 and docs_archetype.py:267 (proselint).

**Operator sees:** On any machine where the linters are not pip-installed (a fresh clone, the Mac-Mini install, CI), `check_release_readiness.py` step 2 runs each archetype against its known_good fixture, gets passed=True with no findings, prints `[OK] Release-ready`, and writes the green sidecar. The operator gets the exact banner their discipline requires while zero static analysis actually ran. Reviews of known-BAD code would likewise come back clean.

**Fix:** In each `_run_*` runner, detect the not-installed case explicitly before parsing: if `proc.returncode != 0 and not proc.stdout.strip()` and `'No module named' in proc.stderr`, raise FileNotFoundError so the existing 'missing' branch fires. Apply to coding_archetype.py `_run_ruff`/`_run_mypy`/`_run_bandit`/`_run_vulture`, gui_archetype.py `_run_ruff`/`_run_bandit`, and docs_archetype.py `_run_proselint`.

> **Verifier correction:** Severity downgraded high -> medium on one ground only: all six tools ARE installed on this machine (I checked importlib.util.find_spec for ruff, mypy, bandit, vulture, semgrep, pyright, proselint — all INSTALLED), so the gate is not currently vacuous here. The defect is latent and fires on a fresh clone / CI / the Mac-Mini install. Everything else in the finding is accurate, including the correct scoping to exactly the four `sys.executable -m` tools — pyright (line 378) and semgrep (line 473) are invoked as bare executables and CAN legitimately raise FileNotFoundError.

### `src/gui/live_bot_window.py:288` — Lite Live Bot window wedges permanently when the exchange connect fails
`error-handling` · **low** · verdict PARTIAL

**Broken:** _on_start optimistically sets btn_start.setEnabled(False), btn_stop.setEnabled(True) and lbl_status to "running" (lines 270-272) before the worker thread has connected. When _main's connect raises, it logs and returns without ever assigning self._bot, so self._bot stays None. _on_stop then hits its first guard `if self._bot is None: return` (lines 328-329) and does nothing -- it never re-enables START, never resets the status label, and never runs the unsubscribe loop at lines 342-345. The four event-bus subscriptions registered at lines 250-255 leak and keep pumping into the panes.

**Operator sees:** Typo an API key, press START, see "CONNECT FAILED: ..." in the console pane while the header still says "Status: running BTC/USD", START greyed out and STOP doing nothing. The only recovery is killing and relaunching the window.

**Fix:** On the connect-failure path, marshal a UI reset back to the GUI thread (a _sig_failed Signal connected to a slot that re-enables START, disables STOP, resets lbl_status and tears down self._unsubs). Also make _on_stop reset the buttons and unsubscribe even when self._bot is None.

> **Verifier correction:** The control-flow trace is exactly right. _on_start sets btn_start.setEnabled(False)/btn_stop.setEnabled(True)/lbl_status 'Status: running {symbol}' at 270-272 after spawning the thread at 261-268 and after registering the four bus subscriptions at 250-255. In _main, connect() failure hits `self._sig_console.emit(f"CONNECT FAILED: {e}")` / `return` at 288-289 before self._bot is ever assigned (assignment is at 306), and _on_stop's first statement is `if self._bot is None: return` (328-329), so the unsubscribe loop (342-345), the button re-enable (349-350) and the status reset (351) are all skipped. BUT reachability is limited: LiteLiveBotWindow is only instantiated in this module's own standalone entry point (`main()` at 361-367, guarded by `if __name__ == "__main__"` at 370). My grep for LiteLiveBotWindow/live_bot_window across the repo finds no other construction site — the shipping app (main.py -> MainWindow) never opens this window.

### `src/gui/main_window.py:3816` — Bot Swarm injection into the Simulator tab calls a method that exists nowhere; the AttributeError is swallowed by a bare except
`silent-failure` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** `set_bot_viz` is not defined on SimulatorTab, on any of its panels, or anywhere else in the codebase — the only two occurrences of the name in the whole repo are these two call sites. Every boot therefore raises AttributeError on line 3816, which the bare `except Exception: pass` discards with no log line at any level. The guard chain (`hasattr(self,'_bot_viz')`, `hasattr(self,'_simulator')`) checks the caller's own attributes, not the callee's method, so it gives false confidence that the wiring succeeded.

**Operator sees:** The Simulator tab never receives the BotVisualizationTab reference, so whatever the Bot Swarm cross-feed was meant to render inside the Simulator is silently absent. The operator sees no error, no warning in the Activity Log, and no entry in acervator.log — the feature just does not exist while the source reads as if it is wired.

**Fix:** Either implement `set_bot_viz(self, viz)` on SimulatorTab (storing the reference and forwarding to whatever panel needs it), or delete the block. If it is kept, replace the bare `except Exception: pass` with `except Exception as exc: logger.warning('Bot Swarm injection into Simulator failed: %s', exc)` and guard with `hasattr(self._simulator, 'set_bot_viz')` like the neighbouring v3.23.80/v3.23.81 hooks at lines 3767 and 3774 already do.

> **Verifier correction:** Quote is verbatim at lines 3812-3818; the cited anchor 3816 is the actual `self._simulator.set_bot_viz(self._bot_viz)` call line. Re-ran `grep -rn "set_bot_viz" --include=*.py .` over the whole repo: exactly two hits, main_window.py:3816 and 3821 — both call sites, zero definitions. Confirmed `class SimulatorTab(QWidget)` at simulator_tab.py:56 and `grep -rn "__getattr__" src/gui/` returns nothing, so there is no dynamic-dispatch escape hatch. I additionally verified the precondition the claim asserted but did not prove: `self._bot_viz = BotVisualizationTab()` at line 3704 is unconditional (12-space method-body indent, no enclosing try), and `self._simulator = SimulatorTab()` at 3762 likewise — so the outer guard at 3813-3814 does pass and line 3816 genuinely executes and raises on every boot. The bare `except Exception: pass` at 3817-3818 has no logging at any level. The claim's point that the hasattr chain checks the CALLER's attributes rather than the callee's method is correct and is the reason this reads as successful wiring.

### `src/stocks/tradingview_bridge.py:201` — TradingViewBridge.stop() cannot stop the fallback webhook server — AttributeError swallowed, then it logs "stopped" while still accepting trade alerts
`silent-failure` · **low** · verdict CONFIRMED

**Broken:** stop() assumes self._server is always an aiohttp AppRunner. On the fallback path it is not: when aiohttp is missing, start() catches ImportError at line 189 and awaits _start_basic_server(), which at line 297 assigns 'self._server = server' where server is an http.server.HTTPServer. HTTPServer has no cleanup() method, so 'await self._server.cleanup()' raises AttributeError, which the bare 'except Exception: pass' discards with no log. The server object is never shut down and the daemon thread started at line 302 keeps running server.serve_forever(). The method then sets self._running = False and logs 'TradingView webhook server stopped' — a false statement. Because _check_auth is consulted per-request (line 273) rather than gated on _running, the socket keeps listening on port 8742 and keeps dispatching parsed alerts to every registered handler (lines 282-286, where handler exceptions are ALSO swallowed by a bare pass).

**Operator sees:** An operator without aiohttp installed who clicks 'Stop' on the webhook bridge sees the log line 'TradingView webhook server stopped' and the get_summary() panel flip to running=False, while an HTTP server on port 8742 continues to accept and execute inbound trade alerts. That is a stop button that reports success and does nothing, on the path that injects trades.

**Fix:** Branch on the server type in stop(): 'if hasattr(self._server, "cleanup"): await self._server.cleanup()' / 'elif hasattr(self._server, "shutdown"): self._server.shutdown(); self._server.server_close()' / 'else: logger.error("Cannot stop unknown server type %s", type(self._server))'. Set self._server = None after a successful stop. Replace both bare 'except Exception: pass' blocks (lines 202-203 and 285-286) with logger.exception calls — a webhook handler that raises is a trade that did not happen and must not be invisible.

> **Verifier correction:** The anchor 201 is the 'await self._server.cleanup()' line; the quoted block actually begins at line 199 ('if self._server:'). Severity confirmed medium-in-principle, downgraded to low: TradingViewBridge is never constructed anywhere in the tree (grep -rn 'TradingViewBridge' -> only the definition at line 38), so neither path executes.

### `src/trading/live_monitor.py:61` — TradeJournal.verify() never verifies the hash chain — it counts lines and always returns True
`silent-failure` · **low** · verdict PARTIAL · shared-with-live

**Broken:** This is the tamper-evidence check for the chained journal built by record() at lines 44-60 (each entry's chain_hash = sha256(prev_hash + record_hash)). verify() never recomputes a single hash, never reads chain_hash, and never compares anything — it returns a hardcoded True with a line count. A journal with edited, deleted or reordered rows verifies clean. The chain that ReportGenerator.generate() then stamps into the official report's `integrity` block (lines 106-108, journal_hash + journal_records) is therefore unvalidated. Compounding it, _resume() at lines 37-43 rebuilds only self._s['trades'] on restart — volume, harvests, folds, boost_sells, boost_folds, wires, wins, peak and max_dd all reset to 0 and are then published in the report's `activity` and `portfolio.max_dd` fields.

**Operator sees:** Any integrity claim made from this journal — the report's `integrity.journal_hash`, the LiveMonitor handshake's [HASH:...] proof-of-authenticity — rests on a check that cannot fail. After any restart the generated 'official' report also understates volume, harvest/fold counts and max drawdown, because those counters were never rebuilt from disk.

**Fix:** Implement verify(): walk the file recomputing chain_hash = sha256(prev + record_hash(row_minus_chain_fields)) and return (False, first_bad_seq) on the first mismatch; have callers surface the failure. Separately, extend _resume() to replay every counter in self._s (volume, harvests, folds, boost_*, wires, wins, peak, max_dd) rather than trades alone.

> **Verifier correction:** The code is exactly as quoted. live_monitor.py:61-67 is verbatim: verify() opens the file, increments n per line, `return True, n` — no hash recomputation, no chain_hash read. record() at :46 does compute `ch = hashlib.sha256(f"{self._last_hash}:{trade.record_hash}".encode()).hexdigest()`. _resume() at :37-43 restores only _last_hash, _seq and `self._s["trades"] += 1` — volume, harvests, folds, boost_sells, boost_folds, wires, wins, peak, max_dd all stay 0, and those feed the report's activity block (:104-105) and portfolio.max_dd (:103). integrity block at :106-108 as claimed.

TWO PROOF ERRORS. (1) The instantiation attribution is WRONG: main_window.py:3153 `self._journal = TradeJournal()` imports from ..trading.reconciliation (see main_window.py:3149) — that is a DIFFERENT TradeJournal class defined at reconciliation.py:81, with no hash chain and no verify() at all. The only live_monitor.TradeJournal construction is bot_container.py:1700, and I read 1695-1707: it is reached only from configure_live_monitor, which returns early at :1695 unless settings has enabled AND api_key. (2) `grep -rn '\.verify()' src tools main.py` returns ZERO matches — verify() is never called by anything in the product, so no runtime integrity claim currently rests on it.
